the GD recursion x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i is substituted. A formal substitution
need not itself be a legal trajectory; any use of that distinction is
explicit below.

The flow gives e^T M e=0: Te=e+P and
2sum_(i<4)h_i P_i=s^2-sum h_i^2, whereas
e^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2. PSD forces Me=0 by applying nonnegativity to
(e+z*q)^T M(e+z*q) for both signs of z. It also gives

    |x^T M y|^2 <= (x^T M x)(y^T M y).                 (3)

If one energy is zero, the mixed term is zero, by the same polynomial
argument. No positive-definiteness assumption is needed.

Define the two ORIGINAL cut columns

    jcut=e0+e1, kcut=e3+e4,
    S=jcut^T M jcut, Tcut=kcut^T M kcut, r=jcut^T M kcut,
    B0=Lambda30+Lambda40, B1=Lambda31+Lambda41,
    Bmass=Hpre*B0+beta*B1.                              (4)

Thus S,Tcut,B0,B1>=0 and |r|<=sqrt(S*Tcut). None of the formal gradients
are identified, even in the boundary v=0.

## 2. Exact cost of the repaired last-cut geometry

Consider scalar base and auxiliary data, with saturated values
f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2 in each array:

    xB=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t),        gB=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
    xA=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,-a*b*t),
    gA=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*t,-a*b*t).                        (5)

Both arrays obey the ORIGINAL four steps, because c=(1+v)*t. Their
normals x_i-g_i are

    yB=(Hpre,beta,0,-v*t,0),
    yA=(0,0,0,-a*b*v*t,0).

For saturated values Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)*y_j. Take their orthogonal sum
with weights ell and mu: positions and gradients are
(sqrt(ell)*xB_i,sqrt(mu)*xA_i) and
(sqrt(ell)*gB_i,sqrt(mu)*gA_i), and values are ell*fB_i+mu*fA_i.

Here is the complete list of nonzero finite residuals, with repeated
indices denoting their original ordered pairs:

    Q30=Q40=-ell*mu*Hpre,
    Q31=Q41=-ell*mu*beta,
    Q03=-a*b*u*mu*v*t,
    Q23=-a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t.                             (6)

Every other ordered finite residual is zero. In particular Q13=0:
the base residual -v*t*(1+t) and the auxiliary residual
a^2*b*v*t*(1-b*t) cancel after weighting. For Q03, the positive cost
is v*t*mu*[ell+a*b*(1+a*b*t)]=a*b*u*mu*v*t. For Q23 it is
v*t*mu*[ell+a^2*b^2*mu]=a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t. These identities also
hold when ell=0 or v=0.

The zero optimum jet can be added. Residuals directed toward it vanish.
The opposite residuals are the negatives of <G_i,X_i-G_i>; at targets
0,1,3 these inner products are respectively ell*Hpre, ell*beta, and
v*t^2*(ell+mu*a^2*b^2), all nonnegative. The other two are zero.

These are samples of one actual global convex differentiable function
with 1-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer. Indeed take Kphys to be the
convex hull of zero and the five displayed gradient vectors, and set

    Fphys(X)=max_(G in Kphys)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).

The residual checks say precisely that X_i-G_i is an outward normal
at G_i. Completing the square makes the unique maximizer the Euclidean
projection onto Kphys. Adding the two projection normal inequalities
gives ||G(X)-G(Y)||^2<=<G(X)-G(Y),X-Y>, hence the projection is
1-Lipschitz. Optimality at X and X+z bounds
Fphys(X+z)-Fphys(X)-<G(X),z> between zero and ||z||^2, proving
differentiability. Convexity follows from the maximum of affine
functions, and Fphys>=0 with Fphys(0)=0. This construction also covers
degenerate Kphys at ell=0 or v=0. It is one static function.

For any saturated-value data the squared-distance telescope in (2) is

    V_h=||X0||^2-||X4+s*G4||^2.

Here X4=G4 and ell+mu*a^2*b^2=a^2*b*beta. Consequently the energy is
exactly Rcut in (1). The full identity (2), with all costs (6), gives

 Rcut=ell*mu^2*Tcut+mu*gA^T M gA+ell*mu*Bmass
          +a*b*u*mu*v*t*Lambda03
          +a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t*Lambda23.                 (7)

The scalar column gA in this formula is the five-entry column in (5).
Every summand is nonnegative for a feasible array. In particular

    Rcut>=0,
    ell*[mu^2*Tcut+mu*Bmass] <= Rcut.                  (8)

Notice the cross-term-containing energy that has NOT disappeared:

    gA=u*e0-a*beta*jcut-a*b*mu*kcut+a*b*e,
    gA^T M gA=||u*e0-a*beta*jcut-a*b*mu*kcut||_M^2.    (9)

Here ||z||_M^2 means z^T M z. Formula (9) will delimit the relaxation
solved below; it is not set to zero in a claimed full certificate.

## 3. First-cut and first-row budgets, retained simultaneously

Set

    eta=b/(1+w+v), Fcut=A^2-C^2*eta^2.                  (10)

The scalar function with derivative clamp(x,[-eta,1]) and minimum zero,
started at A, has gradients (1,1,-eta,-eta,-eta) for this word. Its
endpoint equals its gradient and its primitive energy is Fcut. Its
twenty residuals are nonpositive, so (2) and Me=0 give

    Fcut>=0,  S<=Fcut/(1+eta)^2.                       (11)

There is a second, sometimes stronger budget from the initial lifted
cut. The original diagonal is M00=1-a^2-a*sum_(j>0)Lambda0j. The
constant-kernel row moment is sum_(j>0)P_j*Lambda0j=2u. Therefore full
feasibility implies 0<a<1. The scalar bound C*eta<=A and C>A give
a*eta<1. Put

    delta=u^2-2,
    nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,
    Erow=a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2,
    chi=1/nu+1/(1+eta)^2.                              (12)

The lifted-cut accounting, reproved in Appendix A, is

 Erow=nu*(M00+a*(Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40))
       +(1+eta)^2*M11
       +(1+eta)*eta*((w+v)*Lambda12+v*Lambda13).        (13)

The construction and identity (13) need only 0<a<1, a*eta<1 and the
current positive a,b,c with v>=0; the stronger first-row gap from the
appendix has its original additional hypotheses. PSD and weighted
Cauchy--Schwarz applied to sqrt(M00),sqrt(M11) show

    S<=(sqrt(M00)+sqrt(M11))^2<=chi*Erow.

Define the completely explicit nonnegative budget

    Gcap=min(Fcut/(1+eta)^2, chi*Erow).                 (14)

Thus S<=Gcap. A negative Fcut or Erow already excludes feasibility.
No choice between the two actual functions changes the multiplier
array: both inequalities must hold for the SAME original M and Lambda.

For d=v-1>0 retain the original quantitative result2948d4cd3660413f
exactly: with Qenergy=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*d^2,
Wrow=max(1+b,1+b*c*d), Krow=max(u,a*Wrow), and a>sqrt(2)-1,

 u*delta<=Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu),
 Erow>=nu*[u*delta/(sqrt(Qenergy+Krow*u*delta)
                                 +sqrt(Qenergy))]^2>0. (15)

Here 0<=Qenergy<1 is necessary. If additionally a*b<=1 and c*d<=1,
the strict consequences remain Erow>nu*delta^2/u^2 and
b<(a+3)*(C-a-2)/((a+1)*(C+a+2)). Appendix A supplies their full proof,
including Qenergy=0. No reversal or extension to other endpoint regimes
is inferred from (15).

## 4. The original cross rows price a prefix deficit

Let

    Dprefix=N^2*b^2-A^2.                               (16)

This is the negative of the primitive energy of the actual two-step
prefix (u,beta), on the scalar cut started at A with gradients
(1,1,-b). To apply the full identity, extend these data FORMALLY by
constant gradient -b through the last two updates and affine function
values. The extension need not be legal; this is a substitution in (2).
Its full primitive energy equals the prefix energy A^2-N^2*b^2, as
direct substitution or telescoping the two constant-gradient updates
shows.

The formal gradient column is beta*jcut-b*e. The only negative
residuals are Q20=Q30=Q40=-u*beta. The only positive residuals are

    Q03=Q13=w*b*beta,
    Q04=Q14=(w+v)*b*beta.

All remaining ordered residuals are zero. Hence (2) gives the EXACT
full-array identity

 Dprefix=b*beta*[w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)
                         +(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14)]
             -beta^2*S-u*beta*(Lambda20+B0).           (17)

Independently, expand the original rows0 and1 against kcut. Since
T e0=u*e-a*e0 and
T e1=beta*e-beta*e0-b*e1, one obtains

 2*e0^T M kcut=a*B0-Lambda03-(v+1)*Lambda04,
 2*e1^T M kcut=beta*B0+b*B1-Lambda13-(v+1)*Lambda14.

Thus

 w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)+(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14)
    =w*(Apre*B0+b*B1-2r)
                         -v*(w-1)*(Lambda04+Lambda14).

Substitution in (17) yields

 Dprefix/beta=(b*w*Apre-u)*B0+b^2*w*B1-2*b*w*r-beta*S
                -u*Lambda20-b*v*(w-1)*(Lambda04+Lambda14). (18)

The two subtracted terms have their original nonnegative signs. Define

    kappa=max((b*w*Apre-u)/Hpre, b^2*w/beta)>0,
    tau=kappa/mu, zeta=b*w/mu.                          (19)

Since B0,B1>=0, their two positive-or-signed contributions in (18) are
at most kappa*Bmass. This remains valid if the first coefficient is
negative. No outgoing terminal coefficient was discarded before its
cross-row contribution was accounted for.

If ell>0 put Ecut=Rcut/ell. Combining (8), (18), and (3), with
x=sqrt(S), y=mu*sqrt(Tcut), gives

 Dprefix<=beta*tau*Ecut-beta*tau*y^2
                       +2*beta*zeta*x*y-beta^2*x^2,
    0<=y^2<=Ecut, 0<=x^2<=Gcap.                       (20)

This is the new simultaneous energy/sign interface. Dropping r would
lose its mixed term. Replacing small positive energy by a zero row would
also be invalid; (20) does neither.

## 5. Complete exact decision of the two-energy optimization

For Earg,Garg>=0 define Psi(Earg,Garg) as follows. The fixed positive constants
beta,tau,zeta are those in (19).

If zeta^2<=beta*tau, set

    Psi(Earg,Garg)=beta*tau*Earg.                               (21)

If zeta^2>beta*tau, set

 Psi(Earg,Garg)=beta*tau*Earg+beta*(zeta^2/tau-beta)*Garg
                         when Garg<=tau^2*Earg/zeta^2;
          2*beta*zeta*sqrt(Earg*Garg)-beta^2*Garg
                         when tau^2*Earg/zeta^2<=Garg
                                      <=zeta^2*Earg/beta^2;
          zeta^2*Earg       when Garg>=zeta^2*Earg/beta^2.       (22)

At the shared endpoints the expressions agree. These formulas include
Earg=0 or Garg=0 without division by either energy. In particular Psi(0,Garg)=0
and Psi(Earg,0)=beta*tau*Earg.

Here is a direct proof that Psi is exactly the maximum in (20). For
fixed y, the unconstrained optimal x is zeta*y/beta, truncated at
sqrt(Garg). Completing the square in x gives

 -beta^2*(x-zeta*y/beta)^2+(zeta^2-beta*tau)*y^2.

If the last coefficient is nonpositive, the maximum is zero, attained
at x=y=0, which proves (21). Otherwise this expression increases along
the unconstrained x optimum until y reaches sqrt(Earg) or x reaches
sqrt(Garg). On the latter boundary the concave quadratic in y is maximized
at min(zeta*sqrt(Garg)/tau,sqrt(Earg)); this value is at least the entry point
beta*sqrt(Garg)/zeta because zeta^2>beta*tau. These alternatives give the
three cases in (22), respectively. They also prove attainment in the
closed rectangle, rather than only an upper bound.

The final division-free necessary condition is

                  ell*Dprefix <= Psi(Rcut,ell*Gcap).   (23)

For ell>0 it follows from (20) because Psi is homogeneous of degree one
in (Earg,Garg). For ell=0 the geometry still gives Rcut>=0, and (23) reads
0<=Psi(Rcut,0)=beta*tau*Rcut. It is deliberately vacuous about the
prefix at that boundary. There is no cancellation of a zero factor.

The maximization just solved is the stated TWO-ENERGY relaxation, not
the full original feasibility problem. For example (7) retains the
additional energy (9), as well as two priced original multipliers.
Attaining (21) or (22) alone need not satisfy those terms, the remaining
rows, calibration, or all twenty signs.

## 6. A concrete gap strip in the remaining bc<=1 branch

There is a particularly simple specialization, with NO ordering of
the endpoint steps. If b*c<=1, then

 (b*w*Apre-u)/Hpre-b^2*w/beta
                       =u*(b*c-1)/(beta*Hpre)<=0.

Consequently kappa=b^2*w/beta. Put

    gamma=w/mu=w*(1+v)/(w+v)>=1.

When v>0, gamma>1. Formula (22) simplifies to

 Psi(Earg,Garg)=b^2*gamma*Earg+beta^2*(gamma-1)*Garg
                           if beta^2*Garg<=b^2*Earg;
          2*b*beta*gamma*sqrt(Earg*Garg)-beta^2*Garg
                           if b^2*Earg<=beta^2*Garg<=b^2*gamma^2*Earg;
          b^2*gamma^2*Earg    if beta^2*Garg>=b^2*gamma^2*Earg.  (24)

For v=0 all three expressions have the common maximum b^2*Earg, as
also supplied by (21). In all cases Psi(Earg,Garg)<=b^2*gamma^2*Earg. Thus

                Rcut>=ell*Dprefix/(b^2*gamma^2)         (25)

whenever b*c<=1. This is substantive when ell>0 and Dprefix>0. It
excludes the entire full-cone region

 C>=193/25, a,b,c>0, v>=0, b*t<1, b*c<=1, Dprefix>0,
             0<=Rcut<ell*Dprefix/(b^2*gamma^2).         (26)

The capacity is variable; no upper-capacity slice or endpoint square
is used. Every full-support array is excluded there, not just a center,
sparse ansatz, chosen rank or supplied covariance fiber.

A simpler threshold strip can be stated without Dprefix. If b>=1,
b*c<=1, v>0 and ell>0, then b*gamma=b*w/mu<beta. Also N=A+beta and

 Dprefix/beta^2
    =b^2+2*b^2*A/beta+(b^2-1)*A^2/beta^2
    >=1+A=3+a,

because beta<=2b. Here Dprefix>0. Therefore every feasible array in
this regime obeys the STRICT bound

                         Rcut>ell*(3+a).              (27)

In particular the whole strip 0<=Rcut<=ell*(3+a) is excluded at every
C>=193/25, with v>0 free and no assumption that either endpoint lies
in a previously excluded square or prism. This is a resolved energy
strip of the original cone. It does not assert that this strip survives
every older scalar witness family. No excluded square/prism is a premise.

At Rcut=0 and ell>0 the general result (23), even without b*c<=1,
forces N*b<=A. Thus saturation of the repaired last-cut energy has an
explicit prefix-cut consequence, with its quantitative neighborhood
controlled by (21)--(23). This does not assert arbitrary prefix or tail
heredity away from that energy budget.

### 6.1. An exact remainder and the entire equality face

The simple bound (25) has more structure than its relaxed derivation
shows. Assume b*c<=1 and set

    Kgap=b^2*gamma^2,
    C03=a*b*u*mu*v*t, C23=a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t,
    Ngap=beta*jcut+b*w*kcut,
    Agap=u*e0-a*beta*jcut-a*b*mu*kcut,
    Gap=Kgap*Rcut-ell*Dprefix.

Equations(7) and(18), WITHOUT dropping their extra terms, give the
following exact original-array identity:

 Gap=ell*||Ngap||_M^2+Kgap*mu*||Agap||_M^2
   +ell*[b^2*w*Hpre*(gamma-1)+u*(1-b*c)]*B0
   +ell*b^2*w*beta*(gamma-1)*B1
   +ell*beta*u*Lambda20
   +ell*beta*b*v*c*(Lambda04+Lambda14)
   +Kgap*C03*Lambda03+Kgap*C23*Lambda23.                (33)

For a coefficient check, the terms involving S,r,Tcut are
ell*[beta^2*S+2*beta*b*w*r+b^2*w^2*Tcut], exactly the first square.
The B1 coefficient is ell*b^2*w*beta*(w/mu-1). The B0 coefficient
uses beta*(b*w*Apre-u)-b^2*w*Hpre=u*(b*c-1), giving the displayed
value. The auxiliary energy and all remaining signs are unchanged from
(7),(18). This proves(33) without an optimization or a division by ell.
Every summand is nonnegative on the stated domain, including b*c=1.

Suppose now ell>0 and v>0, still with a,b,c>0 and b*c<=1. Then
gamma>1 and C03,C23>0. If Gap=0, every summand in(33) vanishes, so

 B0=B1=Lambda20=Lambda04=Lambda14=Lambda03=Lambda23=0,
                     M*Ngap=M*Agap=0.                 (34)

Zero PSD energy implies the zero column even when M is singular. The
original first cross row gives e0^T M kcut=0 from(34). Moreover

                 Agap+a*Ngap=u*e0+a*b*v*t*kcut.

Taking its M inner product with kcut gives a*b*v*t*Tcut=0. Every
factor except Tcut is strictly positive, so Tcut=0 and M*kcut=0.
Now (34) gives M*jcut=M*e0=0, hence M*e1=M*e2=0 as well, the last
using Me=0. Formula(7) gives Rcut=0, and Gap=0 with ell>0 then gives
Dprefix=0. Therefore

    Gap=0 implies Rcut=Dprefix=0, M e0=M e1=M e2=M kcut=0. (35)

In particular (25) is STRICT when v>0, ell>0 and Dprefix>0. This
rules out the equality boundary in(26), not merely its open part.

There is an exact classification of the remaining case in(35). Under
the same positive-parameter assumptions a full certificate with Gap=0
exists if and only if

 a=sqrt(2)-1, A=1+sqrt(2),
 b>0, b*N=A, N=A+1+b,
 0<v<=sqrt(2),
 c>0, c*C=N*(1+v), C=N+(1+v)+c.                        (36)

Here the b and c equations each have exactly one positive solution.
Every word in(36) in fact has b*c<1 and b*t<1, so it lies in the
domain being classified. This is an existential classification of the
full cone on this equality face, not a condition on one supplied array.

To prove necessity, (34) and M*jcut=0 imply Lambda13=0 by the second
cross-row formula in Section4. Thus all edges between {0,1} and {3,4}
vanish in both directions. Restrict Lambda to {0,1,2}. Its flow at
0,1 is still -2u,-2beta, so its terminal flow is 2(u+beta). The first
two rows of its original three-gradient primitive matrix are exactly
the corresponding rows of M and therefore zero. This can also be
checked entrywise: the rows retain Lambda01,Lambda02,Lambda10,Lambda12,
Lambda20,Lambda21; the vanished cross edges contribute nothing, while
Te0=u*e-a*e0 and Te1=beta*e-beta*e0-b*e1 eliminate any row2 outgoing
edge to a later index. The constant-gradient scalar form is zero by
the restricted flow, so its remaining diagonal is zero too. Thus the
entire prefix matrix is zero.

Evaluate that zero matrix on the prefix unit-quadratic column
(1,-a,a*b). The direct telescope gives 1-N^2*a^2*b^2=0, hence
N*a*b=1. From Dprefix=0 one has N*b=A. Therefore a*A=1, yielding
a=sqrt(2)-1 and the first two lines of(36).

Substitute N*a*b=1 into(1). It gives

                Rcut=a^2*b*beta*(N^2-C^2*t^2).

Hence Rcut=0 forces C*t=N, which is c*C=N*(1+v). Finally the actual
unit-quadratic column for all four steps is
(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,a*b*c*(v-1)). It obeys Tp=e, so the same direct
telescope and PSD give

 0<=1-[C*a*b*c*(v-1)]^2=1-(v^2-1)^2=v^2*(2-v^2).

Since v>0 this is v<=sqrt(2). This argument allows v<=1 and does not
import the positive-d hypothesis of the earlier strict first-row gap.

For sufficiency, there is an explicit full original array. Put
J=N^2-1 and Kjoin=(C/(1+v))^2. The nonzero entries are precisely

 Lambda01=2,                 Lambda10=2*(1+u),
 Lambda12=J*b,               Lambda21=J,
 Lambda24=(Kjoin-1)*c,
 Lambda32=(Kjoin-1)*v,       Lambda42=Kjoin-1,
 Lambda34=2*Kjoin+(Kjoin-1)*v,
 Lambda43=2*Kjoin*(1+v).                               (37)

All other eleven ordered entries are zero. The FULL remainder is

          M=Kjoin*(2-v^2)*(e3-e4)*(e3-e4)^T>=0.         (38)

Here is a complete way to audit (37)--(38), using the full primitive
join identity already accepted in04219554e41d9750, Section11. A one-step
word z has multipliers Lambda01=2, Lambda10=2(1+z) and remainder
(2-z^2)*(e0-e1)*(e0-e1)^T, as substitution in the original flow and
matrix shows. It is PSD for 0<=z<=sqrt(2), and is zero for z=sqrt(2).
The empty word has zero energy and remainder. For child capacities
Al,Ar and bridge excess q with q*(Al+Ar+q)=Al*Ar, the exact identity is

 V_join=V_left+Kj*V_right-(Kj-1)*E_PP,
 Kj=((Al+Ar+q)/Ar)^2,
 E_PP=sum_j right_j*Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+q*Q_(p,z)
                                +sum_j right_j*Q_(j,z).

The indices p,z are the actual bridge and terminal queries, and j runs
over the right child's nonterminal queries. Every added coefficient is
nonnegative and the full remainder is the original-coordinate sum of
the left remainder and Kj times the right remainder. Applying this
identity first to (sqrt(2)), empty, bridge b gives the first four
entries of(37) and zero remainder. Applying it to that two-step prefix,
the one-step word(v), and bridge c gives exactly the other five entries
and(38), on all five distinct formal coordinates. Thus (37) satisfies
the original flow and all twenty signs, not only a reduced matrix test.
Equations(36) give Rcut=Dprefix=0, so Gap=0.

For completeness, b*c=A*(1+v)/C<1: N>A+1=(A-1)*A and 1+v<=A,
so N>(A-1)*(1+v), and C>N+(1+v)>A*(1+v). Also b*t=A/C<1.

Every capacity in(36) is strictly below77/10. Let
Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4*x*y))/2, the positive root of
z^2-(x+y)*z-x*y=0. It is increasing in each positive argument, either
by this displayed formula or by comparing that polynomial at a root.
We have A<29/12 because sqrt(2)<17/12. At z=201/50,

 z^2-(29/12+1)*z-29/12=131/15000>0,

so N=Omega(A,1)<201/50. Since 1+v<=A,
C=Omega(N,1+v)<Omega(201/50,29/12). At z=77/10,

 z^2-(201/50+29/12)*z-(201/50)*(29/12)=19/1500>0.

Thus C<77/10<193/25. Consequently EVERY full threshold certificate in
the whole domain ell>0,v>0,b*c<=1 must have Gap>0, including Dprefix=0.
The equality face is completely decided and has explicit low-capacity
full certificates; no point on it is an improving threshold seed.

## 7. Relation to full calibration and the uncovered freedom

For d=v-1>0, let p=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,a*b*c*d)^T. Then Tp=e, and
telescoping h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2 proves

                  p^T M p=Qenergy.                    (28)

The columns e,e0,jcut,kcut,p form a basis: any linear combination of
the first four has equal entries at indices3 and4, whereas p3-p4 is
-a*b*c*v, which is nonzero. Thus full PSD is equivalent to PSD of the
FOUR-by-FOUR Gram matrix of e0,jcut,kcut,p. All mixed entries matter.

In particular (7) retains the full quadratic expression (9), not just
S and Tcut. The entry r is retained through (18)--(20), but those
inequalities do not determine e0^T M kcut or kcut^T M p. The earlier
row0 identity prices e0^T M p by actual incoming coefficients; it does
not determine these other entries. A relaxed optimizer from (22) can
fail these remaining joint requirements.

Here is a complete coordinate check showing that no skew/sign freedom
has been silently removed. For positive steps choose P3<rho<s and put

 phi_i=1+P_i, weight_i=2h_i/(rho-P_i) for i<4,
 weight_4=2s/(s-rho), W=sum weight_i,
 Lambda^c_ij=phi_i*weight_i*weight_j/W for i!=j.

The sums sum weight_i*(P_i-rho)=0 and
sum phi_i*weight_i=(1+rho)*W show that this strictly positive center
has the required flow and constant-kernel row moments. They do NOT
show PSD. Let E_diff have columns e_i-e4, i0 through3, and F_null
have columns e_j-(1-P_j/s)e0-(P_j/s)e4, j1 through3. All real
flow/kernel completions are exactly

    L=L^c+E_diff*X*F_null^T, X any real4by3 matrix.     (29)

Indeed the difference annihilates e and P on the right and e on the
left, and these two displayed bases describe precisely those spaces.
The twenty signs remain the simultaneous inequalities

    (E_diff*X*F_null^T)_ij<=Lambda^c_ij, every i!=j.    (30)

Let B_pick=(e0,e1,e2,e3), Rmap=F_null^T*T*B_pick and
K_c=B_pick^T M^c B_pick. Then

 K=K_c+sym(X*Rmap),
 M=[[K,-K*1],[-1^T*K,1^T*K*1]],
 q=(p0-p4,p1-p4,p2-p4,p3-p4)^T,
 q^T K q=Qenergy.                                     (31)

Full M>=0 is equivalent to K>=0. Since T^(-1)P=e-p,
ker(F_null^T T)=span(e,p), so Rmap has rank3 and kernel span(q), with
q nonzero. For any proposed calibrated K put H=K-K_c and

 Szero=[q*(Hq)^T-(Hq)*q^T]/(q^T q).

Then Szero*q=-Hq, and every real completion is

 X=(H+Szero+Z)*Rmap^T*(Rmap*Rmap^T)^(-1),
                 Z^T=-Z, Z*q=0.                      (32)

Multiplication by Rmap verifies this identity; conversely X*Rmap-H
is skew and has value -Hq on q. The free skew space on q-perp is
three-dimensional. None of its three parameters, none of the twenty
signs, and no original gradient coordinate has been deleted. At
Qenergy=0, K>=0 forces Kq=0 without division by that energy.

The new gap excludes every choice in (29)--(32) over the region (26).
Outside it, a calibrated PSD K, a positive center, and an optimizer of
the cut relaxation are still three separate requirements. No full
twenty-sign/high-capacity completion has been produced or excluded.
At zero physical steps one uses the original full matrix and (7)--(23);
the positive-step coordinate paragraph and its independent p column
are not invoked. In particular v=0 never identifies g3 with g4 in a
formal certificate.

## 8. Direct-proof and counterexample screens

The exact endpoint-saturation route was recalled and not re-proved as
a new result; shared6fbfca5386425d02 already handles its conditional
three-step interface. The present mechanism instead retains a positive
cut energy and its actual cross rows.

A first attempt, on positive a,b,c,d with a<sqrt(2)-1 and b*c<=1,
uses row0 and the quadratic energy after dropping a nonnegative incoming
term. Writing m=M00 and Dsmall=2-u^2>0, it gives
u*(m+Dsmall)<=2*sqrt(m*Qenergy). This particular bound minimizes at
m=Dsmall and yields Qenergy>=u^2*(2-u^2), equivalently
C*b*c*d<=a+2. It is the known scalar prefix-clipping condition, and is
not counted as a new obstruction. No redundancy of the entire row0
system is inferred from this one optimized relaxation.
An attempted rational boundary example for independence of the new
gap failed the pre-existing partial-return inequality C*b*c*d<=2+a;
it is not a survivor or a seed. No numerical/model run was made.

The identities were checked against ell=0, Rcut=0, S=0, Tcut=0,
Qenergy=0, b*c=1 and v=0. The general kappa in (19) retains the
b*c>1 case. The smaller kappa and gamma formulas are used only when
b*c<=1. All actual residual signs were enumerated in (6); the prefix
extension in Section4 is explicitly formal, and its positive residuals
are retained rather than asserted legal. The solved optimization is
labeled a relaxation throughout.

Targeted fact_search found no accepted result for this interface.
Matlas searches returned unrelated matrix/optimization statements;
web fallback recalled the already audited composition literature. No
external theorem is imported and no new literature-based branch is
rejected. The proof uses only the accepted primitive identity plus
the complete elementary derivations here.

This round gives the exact cross-row/cut optimization (21)--(23), the
full-cone gap strip (26)--(27), the exact nonnegative remainder (33),
and the full equality-face classification with explicit original arrays
(36)--(38). That face has capacity strictly below77/10, so it is absent
at the threshold193/25. The remaining three-vector energy and full
calibration/sign interface are explicit in (9),(28)--(32). The draft does not
give an actual threshold seed, a full cover of the remaining scalar
domain, general reversal, or a verified original-target result.

## Appendix A. Reproduced first-row proof

The following proof text is reproduced to retain the original hypotheses
and quantitative bounds of2948d4cd3660413f. It is part of this joint
unverified draft, not a citation to an accepted predecessor. The current
main text separately notes where its accounting construction extends
to v>=0; the strict positive-excess conclusions remain exactly scoped.

### 1. Statement and notation

Write a positive-excess four-step word as

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d),    a,b,c,d>0,
    C=1+sum h=5+a+b+c+d.

Suppose it admits the full primitive certificate of accepted fact
04219554e41d9750. Suppose additionally that a>sqrt(2)-1. Define

    u=1+a,             delta=u^2-2,
    H=3+c+d,           eta=b/H,
    R=a+3+1/a,         k=u*delta/a,
    nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,
    Erow=R-k*eta-C^2*eta^2,
    Qenergy=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*d^2,
    Wrow=max(1+b,1+b*c*d),
    Krow=max(u,a*Wrow).

Full feasibility forces a<1, a*eta<1, nu>0, and
0<=Qenergy<1. The following quantitative necessary condition holds:

    u*delta <= Krow*Erow/nu + 2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu).          (A)

In particular,

    Erow >= nu * [u*delta /
          (sqrt(Qenergy+Krow*u*delta)+sqrt(Qenergy))]^2 > 0.  (B)

Thus equality in the previously used lifted middle-cut condition Erow>=0
is impossible throughout this initial-step regime. A version avoiding
square roots is

    Erow>=0,
    [max(u*delta*nu-Krow*Erow,0)]^2 <= 4*Qenergy*Erow*nu.     (C)

If a*b<=1 and c*d<=1, the simpler strict necessary inequality is

    Erow > nu*delta^2/u^2,                                 (D)

equivalently

    b < (a+3)*(C-a-2) / ((a+1)*(C+a+2)).                (E)

The last two product assumptions are explicit hypotheses of this
simplification. They are not silently declared lossless for the
original problem. Conditions(A)--(C) do not require them. The separate
assumption a,b,c,d>0 is also retained explicitly; no assertion about a
zero step or a step at most one is being imported from an unverified
compactness draft.

### 2. Original formal certificate and its constant kernel

Put P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, s=P_4=C-1, and let e be the five-entry all-ones
column. Write e_i for the i-th original standard coordinate of R^5,
with indices0 through4. Let T have diagonal1, entry h_t at(i,t) for t<i, and zero
entries above the diagonal. The original twenty multipliers satisfy

    Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, Lambda_ii=0,
    L=diag(Lambda*e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    D=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),
    L^T e=omega,
    M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0 on ALL of R^5.               (1)

The identity in accepted04219554e41d9750 is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
                      =sum_(i,j) M_ij <g_i,g_j>,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)
          +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2,
    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i.                                (2)

Since Te=e+P and sum_(i<4)h_i P_i=(s^2-sum h_i^2)/2,

    e^TDe=-s^2-sum h_i^2,
    omega^TTe=s^2+sum h_i^2,
    e^TMe=0.

For every real v, the nonnegative quadratic(e+t*v)^T M(e+t*v)
has zero constant coefficient. Considering both signs of t forces
v^TMe=0, hence Me=0. The initial row of this identity is

    sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j=2h0.                        (3)

To check(3) without any suppressed flow condition, direct expansion
gives(Me)_i=h_i(1+P_i)+(LP)_i/2 for i<4. At i=0,
P0=0 and(LP)_0=-sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j.

Let Aout=sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j and m=M00. Its original diagonal is

    m=1-a^2-a*Aout.                                    (4)

Indeed the first column of T is(1,u,u,u,u)^T, so(LT)_00
=(1-u)Aout=-a*Aout. Since m,Aout>=0 and a>0, (4) gives
a<=1. Equality would give m=Aout=0, contradicting(3). Thus a<1.

For any positive semidefinite real matrix, the two-vector inequality

    |v^T M w|^2 <= (v^T M v)*(w^T M w)                 (5)

follows by applying nonnegativity to(v+t*w)^T M(v+t*w).
If w^T M w=0, the linear coefficient must vanish; otherwise minimizing
in t proves(5). This argument does not assume positive definiteness.

### 3. Two exact scalar energies

Here clamp(x,[-eta,1]) means min(1,max(-eta,x)).
The scalar function with this derivative and minimum0
has the actual trajectory

    x=(2+a,1,-b,-(2+d)*eta,-eta),
    g=(1,1,-eta,-eta,-eta).

Here b=(3+c+d)*eta. Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and
1-Lipschitz. At every displayed sample its value is
f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2. The first step gives x1=1; the other three GD
updates follow directly from the displayed relation for b.

For any GD data with saturated values
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, telescoping the squared distances gives

    V_h=||x0||^2-||x4+s*g4||^2.                        (6)

In detail, the first two sums in(2) reduce to
2sum h_i<x_i,g_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2-2s*f4;
the first two terms telescope to||x0||^2-||x4||^2.
Substitution of f4 then gives(6). The scalar trajectory has x4=g4,
so its energy is(2+a)^2-C^2*eta^2. By(1)--(2) it is nonnegative.
Consequently

    C*eta<=2+a,
    a*eta<=a*(2+a)/C<3/5<1,                           (7)

because 0<a<1 and C>5. This proves nu>0.

Next define the actual unit-quadratic gradient column

    p=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,a*b*c*d)^T.

It satisfies Tp=e. Since Le=0,

    p^T M p=p^T D p=1-C^2*p4^2=Qenergy.               (8)

For the telescoping equality, p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i gives
h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2; the terminal diagonal
then supplies-s(s+2)p4^2. Full PSD gives Qenergy>=0.
All a,b,c,d are positive, so Qenergy<1.

### 4. Complete lifted-cut data and its exact multiplier cost

Let U=(1,0), V=(0,1) be orthonormal physical vectors. Set

    z=-eta*U+sqrt(nu)*V.

Then

    ||z||^2=[1+(1-a)*eta]/a,
    <U,z>+a||z||^2=1-a*eta.

Use the five two-dimensional data

    X=(U+u*z, U, -b*U, -(2+d)*eta*U, -eta*U),
    G=(z, U, -eta*U, -eta*U, -eta*U),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (9)

Every GD update has the original step length. For these saturated
values the original ordered interpolation residual is exactly

    Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>.

Here is a check of all twenty ordered finite pairs, grouped by their
target j. Targets1 and4 have zero normal. At target0 the normal is
U+a*z. The incoming pair(1,0) has residual0, whereas each incoming
pair(i,0), i=2,3,4, has residual-a*nu. At target2 the normal is
-(b-eta)U. The only nonzero finite residual there is
Q_12=-(1+eta)(b-eta). At target3 the normal is
-(1+d)*eta*U. The only nonzero finite residual there is
Q_13=-(1+eta)(1+d)*eta. Thus all twenty residuals are nonpositive.
The zero optimum jet can also be added: Q_(i,star)=0 by the chosen
values; the opposite inequalities follow from the same normals and
a*eta<1. These optimum pairs are not additional multipliers in(1).

For completeness these are actual jets of one global smooth convex
function. Let Kphys=conv{0,z,U,-eta*U} and define

    F(X)=max_(G in Kphys)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).

The comparisons just made say that X_i-G_i is an outward normal
to Kphys at G_i. Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer
as the Euclidean projection of X onto Kphys. If projections at X,Y
are G,H, adding their normal inequalities gives
||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>, and hence ||G-H||<=||X-Y||.
To see differentiability directly, optimality at X and X+v bounds
F(X+v)-F(X)-<G,v> between0 and<G(X+v)-G,v>, which is at most||v||^2.
Thus the gradient is the projection and is1-Lipschitz. The function
is convex as a maximum of affine functions of X; it has minimum0 at0
because0 belongs to Kphys. It realizes all of(9).

By(6), its primitive energy is

    ||U+u*z||^2-C^2*eta^2
      =a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2=Erow.            (10)

Let

    Bin=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40.

In the U coordinate the gradient column is-eta*e+(1+eta)*e1;
in the V coordinate it is sqrt(nu)*e0. The constant kernel proved
in Section2 therefore gives the full matrix remainder
(1+eta)^2*M11+nu*m. Substituting every nonzero residual above into
the original identity(2) yields the exact accounting identity

    Erow=nu*(m+a*Bin)+(1+eta)^2*M11
       +(1+eta)*eta*((2+c+d)*Lambda12+(1+d)*Lambda13). (11)

Every term on the right is nonnegative. In particular

    0<=m<=m+a*Bin<=Erow/nu.                              (12)

No incoming coefficient has been set to zero. Identity(11) prices the
original incoming coefficients explicitly.

### 5. The original first row couples the two energies

Define the weighted incoming combination

    Bweight=(1+b)*Lambda20+(1-b*c)*Lambda30
                                    +(1+b*c*d)*Lambda40.

The exact first-row identity is

    2*e0^T M p = u*(m-delta)+a^2*Bweight.             (13)

Here e0 denotes the original first coordinate column of R5. A direct
derivation avoids any coordinate reduction. Since Tp=e and Le=0,
Mp=Dp+T^T L^T p/2. Also Te0=u*e-a*e0, giving

    2*e0^T M p=2(1-a^2)-a*p^T L e0.

The flow at node0 gives Lambda10=Aout+2u-Bin. Hence

    p^T L e0
      =Aout+a*Lambda10-a*b*Lambda20
                        +a*b*c*Lambda30-a*b*c*d*Lambda40
      =u*Aout+2a*u-a*Bweight.

Finally substitute a*Aout=1-a^2-m from(4). The constant term becomes

    (2-u)(1-a^2)-2a^2*u
      =u*((1-a)^2-2a^2)=-u*delta,

which proves(13), with all row and flow contributions retained.

All three incoming multipliers are nonnegative. Since b,c,d>0,
each coefficient in Bweight is at most Wrow. This remains true even
if 1-b*c is negative. Thus Bweight<=Wrow*Bin. Applying(5) with e0,p,
and using(8), gives from(13)

    u*delta <= u*m+a^2*Wrow*Bin+2*sqrt(m*Qenergy)
             <= Krow*(m+a*Bin)+2*sqrt(m*Qenergy)
             <= Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu).        (14)

This is(A). Put t=sqrt(Erow/nu). Because Krow,u,delta are positive,
(14) says

    Krow*t^2+2*sqrt(Qenergy)*t-u*delta>=0.

Solving this increasing quadratic for t>=0 and rationalizing its
positive root gives(B). Its numerator is strictly positive, so Erow>0.
Multiplying(A) by nu and squaring only when its left side after
subtraction is positive gives exactly(C). There is no sign-losing
squaring in this formulation.

### 6. A rational strengthened middle-cut boundary

If c*d<=1, then Wrow=1+b. If also a*b<=1, then
a*Wrow=a+a*b<=1+a=u, so Krow=u. Since Qenergy<1 and Erow>0,
(14) implies the strict inequality

    u*delta < u*t^2+2t,       t=sqrt(Erow/nu)>0.

The right side is strictly increasing for t>=0. Its value at
t=delta/u is

    delta^2/u+2delta/u=delta*(delta+2)/u=u*delta,

because delta+2=u^2. It follows that t>delta/u, which is(D).
The exact algebraic identity

    Erow-nu*delta^2/u^2
      =[(a+3-delta*eta)^2-u^2*C^2*eta^2]/u^2          (15)

can be checked by expanding the constant, linear, and quadratic
coefficients in eta. Those coefficients in the numerator are
(a+3)^2, -2(a+3)*delta, and delta^2-u^2*C^2.

The first factor a+3-delta*eta is positive: by a*eta<1,

    a+3-delta*eta > a+3-delta/a = 1+1/a >0.

Thus(D) and(15) give

    a+3 > (u*C+delta)*eta.

Substitute eta=b/(C-2-a-b), whose denominator is H>0. Since
delta+a+3=(a+1)(a+2), the result is exactly(E). Every denominator
in(E) is positive.

In particular this is a necessary bound on an entire step region,
not a condition on a sparse coefficient family. The exact stronger
condition(A) still retains the actual Qenergy rather than discarding
it in favor of the upper bound1.

### 7. Adversarial scope checks

The initial-step hypothesis is substantive. At a=sqrt(2)-1 the
quantity delta is zero, and(A) supplies no positive gap. This agrees
with the possibility of saturated basic certificates at that endpoint.
We do not extend the strict conclusion by continuity across delta=0.

If b*c>1, the coefficient of Lambda30 in(13) is negative. The proof
does not replace this coefficient by its absolute value or infer a
false lower bound. It uses only the valid upper bound Bweight<=Wrow*Bin.
Thus(A)--(C) still apply in that regime. The simplified statement(E)
requires only its explicitly stated a*b and c*d hypotheses.

If Qenergy=0, formula(B) reduces to Erow>=nu*u*delta/Krow;
no division by Qenergy occurs. If a multiplier vanishes or M has
additional kernels, the semidefinite form of(5) still applies. No
strict complementarity, full rank, or strictly positive support was used.

Here is a purely symbolic test family showing why the separate tests
Erow>=0 and Qenergy>=0 cannot justify the omitted coupling. It is an
analytical counterexample to that shortcut, not a numerical seed proposal
or a claim to survive the other existing necessary inequalities. Take

    a=1/2, eta=7/24, C=39*sqrt(2)/7,
    H=24*(C-5/2)/31, b=7*(C-5/2)/31, Ktail=H-3,
    0<d<min(Ktail/2,1/Ktail,1/(C*a*b*Ktail)), c=Ktail-d.

The elementary bounds 7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7 give
193/25<39/5<C<8. Thus Ktail>0, all four excesses are positive,
a*b<1, c*d<1, and 0<C*a*b*c*d<1. Also C=5+a+b+c+d
and eta=b/(3+c+d). Direct exact substitution gives

    R-k*eta=169/32=C^2*eta^2,
    Erow=0, Qenergy>0,
    nu=1271/576, delta=1/4, u=3/2.

Yet(D) requires Erow>1271/20736. Hence none of these words has the
full certificate. This illustrates the active original-row obstruction;
it is not asserted to be a newly uncovered region beyond every prior
obstruction. No finite numerical values were evaluated for this family.

# A priced nonzero terminal residual and an original-row separation

Status: **UNVERIFIED self-contained joint proof candidate**, max round21,
the final authorized round of the existing ninth launch. No optimization,
numerical evaluation, symbolic-computation job, installation, verifier call,
or subagent is used. None of the claims below is a new fact. The original
all-schedule exact-profile target remains open.

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation convention
and its necessity. All new constructions and estimates are derived here;
earlier unverified findings are motivation, not proof premises. Local
notation is defined independently below.

## 1. An unavoidable terminal price

Normalize the optimum sample to x_star=g_star=f_star=0 and smoothness to1.
For actual terminal position x, gradient w, value f, and residual v=x-w,
the original optimum inequality is

    Q_(end,star)=f-<w,x>+||w||^2/2
                =f-||x||^2/2+||v||^2/2 <=0.             (1)

Thus a change with x fixed and v nonzero MUST lower an old saturated value
f_old=||x||^2/2 by at least ||v||^2/2. Keeping that old value as well as x
and optimum0 would force v=0. The construction below keeps every earlier
point, gradient, and value and pays exactly this required terminal loss.
It does not pretend that the terminal value can be held fixed.

For projection jets the price is an equality:

    f=<x,w>-||w||^2/2,
    J=2f=||w||^2+2<w,v>=||x||^2-||v||^2.               (2)

A physical joining step of size1+r lands at

    d=x-(1+r)w=v-rw,
    Z=||d||^2=r^2||w||^2-2r<w,v>+||v||^2.              (3)

In particular alignment with -rw would be incorrect when v is nonzero.

## 2. The complete reusable boundary contract

A child has a finite nonnegative word p of length ell, positions x_i and
gradients g_i for0<=i<=ell satisfying x_(i+1)=x_i-p_i*g_i. Write

    K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_ell), y_i=x_i-g_i, e_i=<g_i,y_i>.

Require all vertex normals and the optimum normals:

    <g_j-g_i,y_i><=0 for every i,j, and e_i>=0.           (4)

Also supply a nonnegative cone representation of x_0 in the gradients
g_0,...,g_ell. This is a separate input: a nonzero terminal residual does
not automatically give it. Define

    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,
    A=1+sum p_i, R^2=||x_0||^2>0,
    w=g_ell, v=y_ell, J=||w||^2+2<w,v>.

Here and throughout, a convex hull contains all listed gradients, including
duplicates and the genuine queried gradient at every joining endpoint.

Completing the square proves that the maximizer defining F_K is the unique
Euclidean projection P_K(x). Adding the two projection optimality
inequalities gives

    ||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<x-x',P_K(x)-P_K(x')>.

It follows that P_K is1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the two defining maxima at
each other's maximizers bounds
F_K(x')-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),x'-x> between0 and ||x'-x||^2.
Thus F_K is finite, convex, differentiable, and1-smooth, with gradient P_K.
Condition(4) proves that it realizes every supplied jet.

The minimizers are exactly

    K^polar={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in K}.

For sufficiency use the maximum defining F_K. For necessity, F_K(z)=0
implies <z,tg>-||tg||^2/2<=0 for every0<t<=1 and g in K; divide by t
and let t decrease to0. The supplied cone representation gives
<x_0,z><=0 on K^polar. Hence
||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2, with equality at z=0: the true closest-minimizer
distance is R. This is not a choice of a favorable optimum.

Expanding the original residual of fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>, Q_(i,star)=0, Q_(star,i)=-e_i.   (5)

Consequently(4) checks every original row, not a selected subset.

Take two such children L,R, of capacities A,C and initial radii R_L,R_R.
For r>0 assume d=v_L-r*w_L is nonzero, and put Z=||d||^2. Set

    e=d/sqrt(Z), b=x_R,0/R_R, alpha=sqrt(Z)/R_R,
    J_L u=(<u,e>, u-<u,e>e, 0),
    J_R z=(<z,b>, 0, z-<z,b>b).                          (6)

The common space is R direct-sum e^perp direct-sum b^perp. These are
actual isometries. Embed all left data by J_L and all right data by
alpha*J_R. The bridge1+r is exact because

    J_L(x_L,ell-(1+r)w_L)=J_L d=alpha*J_R x_R,0.

The cross Gram is explicitly

    <J_L u,alpha*J_R z>=<u,d><x_R,0,z>/R_R^2.             (7)

Prescribe the left values unchanged and the right values multiplied by
alpha^2. Every within-child and optimum row is retained. The complete
remaining conditions, including all endpoint and bridge incidences, are

    <d,y_L,i><x_R,0,g_R,j>/R_R^2 <= e_L,i,               (8L)
    <d,g_L,i><x_R,0,y_R,j> <= Z*e_R,j                    (8R)

for every i,j. These are necessary and sufficient for this specified
embedding and value prescription, by(5) and(7). If they hold, the convex
hull of0 and all parent gradients gives one actual F_K. The retained left
cone representation gives its true initial radius R_L.

The physical parent capacity is P=A+C+r. Its final gradient and residual
are alpha*J_R w_R and alpha*J_R v_R; both survive. Its exact energy is

    J_parent=Z*J_R/R_R^2.                                (9)

Writing kappa_L=A^2*J_L/R_L^2 and kappa_R=C^2*J_R/R_R^2,
if r*P=A*C then

    kappa_parent=[Z/(r^2*J_L)]*kappa_L*kappa_R.            (10)

In terms of tau=<w_L,v_L>/||w_L||^2 and nu=||v_L||^2/||w_L||^2,
the first factor is

    (1-2tau/r+nu/r^2)/(1+2tau).                          (11)

This keeps BOTH child energies and every terminal term. It is an energy
identity conditional on legality, not a claim that arbitrary children join.

## 3. One terminal-gradient change on the same physical word

Let

    s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,
    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300,
    rho=599/600, delta=1-rho=1/600,
    0<eps<=1/100, E=eps^2,
    Klen=a+rho, z=b-1, m=a+b, eta=z/Klen,
    A=1+2a+b=(143+105s)/50.

Take orthogonal unit vectors u,v_axis,q_axis, and define

    U=u, V=sqrt(eta)*v_axis,
    x_3=rho*V+eps*q_axis,
    w=g_3=rho*V+(eps/2)*q_axis,
    v=y_3=(eps/2)*q_axis,
    g_0=g_1=U, g_2=V,
    x_2=x_3+aV, x_1=x_2+bU, x_0=x_1+aU.                (12)

The word is exactly(a,b,a), unchanged from the physical diagnostic word.
For eps=1/100 the earlier positions and gradients are exactly those of
the previously proposed three-vector seed. Only its terminal gradient is
changed. Allowing smaller eps changes the seed's q-coordinate amplitude
but never its word. We will exhibit the exact full-coupling obstruction
at eps=1/10000.

The residuals and normal energies are

    y_0=(m-1)U+Klen*V+eps*q_axis, e_0=m-1,
    y_1=zU+Klen*V+eps*q_axis,     e_1=z,
    y_2=(Klen-1)V+eps*q_axis,    e_2=(Klen-1)eta,
    y_3=(eps/2)q_axis,          e_3=E/4.                  (13)

Here is the complete seed normal check against vertices0,U,V,w:

* At sample0, the normals against0,V,w respectively are
  -(m-1), -a, -a-delta*z+E/2; the U normal is0.
* At sample1, they are -z, 0, -delta*z+E/2; the U normal is0.
* At sample2, the normals against0 and U both equal -(Klen-1)eta,
  the w normal is -delta*(Klen-1)eta+E/2, and the V normal is0.
* At sample3, the normals against0,U,V all equal -E/4; the w normal is0.

The elementary bounds7/5<s<10/7 imply

    139/100<a<143/100, 2<b<201/100,
    238/100<Klen<243/100, z>1, eta>2/5.

They give delta*z>1/600>E/2 and
delta*(Klen-1)eta>1/1500>E/2. All listed normals are nonpositive;
all four e_i are positive. The function in section2 therefore realizes
these jets and every original Q. There is a genuine nonzero terminal
residual. No seed sample has both gradient and positive residual parallel
to the terminal gradient w.

For the true distance use the explicit positive cone representation

    x_0=mU+(a-rho)V+2w,                                 (14)

whose coefficients are positive. Define

    R^2=m^2+Klen*z+E, N=||w||^2=rho^2*eta+E/4,
    J=2f_3=rho^2*eta+3E/4,
    <w,v>=||v||^2=E/4.                                  (15)

In particular all earlier values are unchanged by replacing the old
g_3=x_3 by w. The terminal value decreases by E/8, exactly the amount
forced by(1). The construction carries this loss into J; it is not hidden
in a new normalization.

For completeness the gradient-energy surplus is positive too. Put
Rbar^2=m^2+Klen*z and Nbar=rho^2*eta. Substituting the displayed a,b,rho,
m,Klen,z,A into [A^2*rho^2*z-Klen*m^2-Klen^2*z]/
[Klen*(m^2+Klen*z)] and using s^2=2 gives

    A^2*Nbar/Rbar^2-1=(-P0+Q0*s)/D0,
    P0=19019047380837966731,
    Q0=13474265781546157318,
    D0=40842989603870102500.

All three integers are positive, and the exact integer identity

    2(2000Q0)^2-(2000P0+D0)^2
      =2441206548123238434623351342332719331750000>0

proves A^2*Nbar/Rbar^2>1+1/2000 by squaring positive numbers.
Also Rbar^2>9 and Nbar<1, so Rbar^2>4Nbar. Adding E/4 to its numerator
Nbar and E to its denominator Rbar^2 raises their ratio. Therefore

    kappa_N,0=A^2*N/R^2>1+1/2000.                        (16)

This paragraph supplies the algebra and sign certificate within this
joint candidate; it does not assume an earlier unverified fact.

## 4. An actual seven-step parent with the residual retained

Choose r_0=theta*A>2. For any r>1 the true outgoing vector is

    d_r=v-rw=-r*rho*V+((1-r)eps/2)*q_axis,
    Z(r)=||d_r||^2=r^2*Nbar+(r-1)^2*E/4.                 (17)

Every seed gradient and residual has nonnegative coordinates in
(u,v_axis,q_axis), and d_r has nonpositive coordinates. Moreover
<x_0,g_i> and <x_0,y_i> are nonnegative. Both inequalities(8L),(8R)
hold for every pair at r=r_0: their left sides are nonpositive and their
right sides are nonnegative. This includes i=3 or j=3, so the actual
queried joining gradient and its nonzero residual are checked.

Set alpha_0^2=Z(r_0)/R^2 and use the explicit embeddings(6). The convex
hull construction is one actual smooth convex function for

    h[1]=(a,b,a,1+r_0,a,b,a).                             (18)

Its closest distance is R by the retained cone representation(14).
Its terminal data are

    w_1=alpha_0*J_R w, v_1=alpha_0*J_R v,
    J_1=alpha_0^2*J, A_1=lambda*A.

The nonzero terminal residual survives in the parent. Every left e_i is
retained and every right e_i is multiplied by alpha_0^2; none is deleted.

For this seed put tau=E/(4N). Equation(15) gives nu=tau, and the terminal
shape is invariant under the right-copy scaling. Since Nbar>39/100 and
E<=1/10000, one has

    0<tau<1/15000.

On the formal balanced word recurrence

    A_j=lambda^j*A, r_j=theta*A_j,
    h[j+1]=(h[j],1+r_j,h[j]),

the unchanged construction, if legal, would have exactly

    kappa_N,j+1=(1-2tau/r_j+tau/r_j^2)*kappa_N,j^2,
    kappa_E,j=(1+2tau)*kappa_N,j,
    kappa_E,j+1=[(1-2tau/r_j+tau/r_j^2)/(1+2tau)]
                    *kappa_E,j^2.                       (19)

Here kappa_N,j=A_j^2*||w_j||^2/R^2 and
kappa_E,j=A_j^2*J_j/R^2. For r_j>1 the first factor in the first line
exceeds1-tau. Thus the scalar energy calculation alone stays positive:

    kappa_N,j >= (1-tau)^(2^j-1)*kappa_N,0^(2^j)>1,       (20)

because(1-1/15000)(1+1/2000)>1. Equations(19)-(20) do NOT supply
legality at j>=2. The next sections exhibit the missing original rows.
They explain why the scalar surplus cannot be interpreted as a parent.

## 5. The next specified rank-one join fails at queried rows

Retain the actual seven-step child(18), including its specified first
embeddings and values. Its initial point is the retained left p=x_0,
with norm R. The next balanced bridge has r_1=theta*A_1=A. Abbreviate

    Z0=Z(r_0), Z1=Z(r_1), D=m+Klen=A-delta,
    H0=r_0*rho*z+(r_0-1)E/2,
    H1=r_1*rho*z+(r_1-1)E/2.

The true outgoing vector of the seven-step child is

    d_1=v_1-r_1*w_1=alpha_0*J_R d_(r_1),
    ||d_1||^2=alpha_0^2*Z1,
    alpha_1^2=||d_1||^2/R^2.                             (21)

The prospective fifteen-step parent has eight samples from the left
child, then eight samples from the right child; its right-child index j
has original parent index8+j. In the rank-one construction(6), expanding
the two cross rows gives

    Q_(2,9)=alpha_1^2*z*(C2-1),
    C2=r_0*rho*eta*H1*(D+E/z)/(Z0*Z1),                  (22)

    Q_(6,13)=alpha_1^2*alpha_0^2*z*(C6-1),
    C6=r_1*rho*eta*H0*(D+E/z)/(Z0*Z1).                 (23)

For(22), child gradient2 is J_L V, while its residual1 and initial point
are retained from the seed. Use

    <J_L V,d_1>=r_0*rho*eta*H1/R^2,
    <p,y_1>=zD+E, e_1=z.

For(23), child gradient6 and residual5 are
alpha_0*J_R V and alpha_0*J_R y_1. Therefore

    <g_6,d_1>=-alpha_0^2*r_1*rho*eta,
    <p,y_5>=-H0*(zD+E)/R^2,
    e_5=alpha_0^2*z.

Substitution in(7) and(5) gives(22)-(23), without suppressing any
within-child row or optimum row. Every index2,9,6,13 is queried in the
fifteen-step word.

Here is a common exact lower bound for C2 and C6. At E=0 both equal

    C0=Klen*(A-delta)/(theta*A^2*rho^2).                  (24)

The numerator factors in(22)-(23) increase with E. Also
Z(r)<=r^2*(Nbar+E/4) for r>1. Consequently

    C2,C6 >= C0/(1+E/(4Nbar))^2.                         (25)

Squaring rational endpoints proves14142/10000<s<14143/10000.
Substitution into a,b,A yields Klen>2.411, A-delta>5.828,
A<5.8301, theta<.4143, and rho^2<.9967. All terminating decimals here
are exact rationals. Thus

    Klen*(A-delta)>2.411*5.828=14.051308,
    theta*A^2*rho^2<.4143*(5.8301)^2*.9967<14.036,
    14.036*(1+1/1000)=14.050036<14.051308.

This proves C0>1+1/1000. Since E/(4Nbar)<1/15000,

    (1+1/15000)^2<1+1/7000,
    (1+1/2000)(1+1/7000)<1+1/1000,

and(25) proves

    C2>1+1/2000, C6>1+1/2000.                            (26)

The next rank-one join is therefore illegal for the entire interval
0<eps<=1/100, despite the positive scalar recursion(20).

## 6. A quantitative angle bound on every transverse coupling

This section allows ALL transverse couplings in the fifteen-step join,
while keeping the actual seven-step child(18) fixed. The first parent
has its explicit embeddings(6). No claim about changing that earlier
embedding is made.

Let L,R be arbitrary linear isometries of the two copies of this child
into any common Euclidean space, and keep left scale1. A legal bridge
forces

    alpha_1*R p=L d_1,

where alpha_1 is necessarily the positive number in(21). Let P_d denote
orthogonal projection perpendicular to d_1 inside the left child space,
and P_p perpendicular to p inside the right child space. Orthogonal
decomposition gives the exact identity

    <L g_6,alpha_1*R y_5>
      =<g_6,d_1><p,y_5>/R^2
          +alpha_1*<L P_d g_6,R P_p y_5>.                (27)

Both mixed parallel/perpendicular terms vanish by the forced alignment.
By the ordinary Cauchy-Schwarz inequality the second term is bounded
below by -alpha_1*||P_d g_6||*||P_p y_5||. This is valid in every
dimension and for every real positive-semidefinite completion; no
particular contraction or matrix rank is imposed.

Because g_6=alpha_0*J_R V and d_1=alpha_0*J_R d_(r_1),
the transverse norm is exactly

    ||P_d g_6||
      =alpha_0*sqrt(eta-r_1^2*rho^2*eta^2/Z1)
      =alpha_0*(r_1-1)*eps*sqrt(eta)/(2sqrt(Z1)).         (28)

Further ||P_p y_5||<=alpha_0*||y_1||. Divide the possible correction
in(27) by the positive row scale alpha_1^2*e_5, where e_5=alpha_0^2*z.
Equations(21),(28) imply the upper bound

    Tcorr <= R^2*(r_1-1)*eps*sqrt(eta)*||y_1||
                     /(2sqrt(Z0)*Z1*z)
          <= R^2*(r_1-1)*eps*||y_1||
                     /(2r_0*r_1^2*rho^3*eta*z).         (29)

The second inequality uses Z0>=r_0^2*rho^2*eta and
Z1>=r_1^2*rho^2*eta. From the rational bounds in section3,

    R^2<15, ||y_1||<2, r_1-1<5,
    r_0>2, r_1>5, rho^3>97/100, eta>2/5, z>1.

For the first two bounds, explicitly
R^2<(3.44)^2+2.43*1.01+.0001<15 and
||y_1||^2<1.01^2+2.43*1.01+.0001<4.
It follows from(29) that

    Tcorr <[150/(2*2*25*(97/100)*(2/5))]*eps<4eps.       (30)

The aligned contribution of(27) is still exactly(23). Thus for EVERY
coupling with the required physical alignment and the prescribed values,

    Q_(6,13)/(alpha_1^2*e_5) >= C6-1-Tcorr
                              >1/2000-4eps.             (31)

In particular at the explicit eps=1/10000,

    Q_(6,13)/(alpha_1^2*e_5)>1/10000>0.                  (32)

This is an exact original-row separation for all transverse choices at
the next join of these fixed actual seven-step children. Both children
are individually legal, have nonzero terminal residual, and retain the
strict energy surplus(16); their prospective fifteen-step parent cannot
exist with this alignment, internal data, and value prescription.

More generally any successful next join inside this parametrized family
must satisfy the explicit compatibility condition

    alpha_1*||P_d g_6||*||P_p y_5||
           >=alpha_1^2*e_5*(C6-1).                      (33)

For0<eps<=1/100 the bounds above give the simpler necessary condition
eps>1/8000. It is only necessary. Increasing eps beyond this bound does
not prove feasibility. The obstruction is to arbitrarily small changes
of this specified geometry, not to every nonlinear geometry.

## 7. The retained terminal normal gives a stronger four-row separation

The original interpolation rows give more information than the norm bound
in section6. This section excludes the next join for the entire interval
0<eps<=1/100, including the original q-coordinate amplitude1/100.
The actual seven-step child and its first embeddings remain fixed.

Two exact vector identities in that child are decisive:

    d_1=-r_1*rho*g_6-(r_1-1)*v_1,
    y_5=z*g_4+(a-rho)*g_6+2*g_7.                         (34)

For the first identity, use w_1=rho*g_6+v_1 and d_1=v_1-r_1*w_1.
For the second, use the seed identity
y_1=zU+(a-rho)V+2w, and apply alpha_0*J_R.
Every coefficient in the second identity is positive. The energy of the
left endpoint residual v_1 is

    e_7=<g_7,v_1>=alpha_0^2*E/4.

Let

    k=(r_1-1)/(r_1*rho)>0, T_y=z+(a-rho)+2=m+delta.

Use arbitrary second-join isometries L,R with
alpha_1*R p=L d_1, as in section6, and write G_i,Y_i for the resulting
parent gradient and residual at original index i. The first identity(34)
implies

    <G_6,Y_13>=-alpha_1^2*<p,y_5>/(r_1*rho)
                        -k*<L v_1,Y_13>.

The second identity(34) says

    Y_13=z*G_12+(a-rho)*G_14+2*G_15.

Since Q_(j,7)=<G_j,L v_1>-e_7 for j=12,14,15, the following nonnegative
combination of ORIGINAL interpolation rows has no free transverse term:

    S_rows=Q_(6,13)+k[z*Q_(12,7)+(a-rho)*Q_(14,7)+2*Q_(15,7)]
          =alpha_1^2[-<p,y_5>/(r_1*rho)-e_5]-k*T_y*e_7
          =alpha_1^2*e_5*[I-1-k*T_y*E/(4z*alpha_1^2)],   (35)

where

    I=H0*(D+E/z)/(r_1*rho*Z0).                           (36)

Here e_5=alpha_0^2*z and the initial pairing <p,y_5> was calculated
explicitly in section5. The identity does not impose a rank or an angle
condition. The queried row(6,13) and the rows entering the queried bridge
endpoint7 all must hold. The term(15,7) is OUTGOING from the unqueried
final sample, so even the accepted unqueried-terminal reduction retains
it. There is no use of a deleted incoming-final row.

At E=0 expression(36) equals C0 from(24). Its numerator factors increase
with E, and Z0<=r_0^2*(Nbar+E/4). Hence

    I>=C0/(1+E/(4Nbar))>1+1/1200,                       (37)

because C0>1+1/1000, E/(4Nbar)<1/15000, and
(1+1/1200)(1+1/15000)<1+1/1000.

The terminal-normal charge is bounded without dropping it. We have
k<1 because r_1*delta<6/600<1, and T_y<7/2. Also r_0>12/5 and
r_1>29/5 follow from the rational bounds on s and A. Equations(17),(21),
Nbar>39/100, and R^2<15 give

    alpha_1^2=Z0*Z1/R^4
       >[(12/5)^2*(29/5)^2*(39/100)^2]/225>1/8.          (38)

The final rational comparison in(38) is, on clearing denominators,
8*144*841*1521>625*10000*225; the two sides are1473593472 and1406250000.
Consequently

    0<k*T_y*E/(4z*alpha_1^2)<7E<=7/10000.

Combining this with(35),(37) gives the strict positive separation

    S_rows/(alpha_1^2*e_5)>1/1200-7/10000=1/7500>0.      (39)

All four coefficients in S_rows are positive. Therefore all four original
rows cannot be nonpositive. This excludes EVERY transverse coupling at
the next join of the displayed actual seven-step children for every
0<eps<=1/100. The terminal residual was not erased: its original normal
is the source of the finite charge in(35), and this charge is too small
to repair the compatibility gap.

More generally the exact necessary compatibility condition for the same
first-parent family is

    I-1 <= k*T_y*E/(4z*alpha_1^2).                        (40)

Within0<eps<=1/100, the coarse estimates already require
E>1/8400, in conflict with E<=1/10000. This is a necessary condition
only; satisfying(40) would not imply the other original rows or a parent.

## 8. Exact scope and the smallest unresolved object

There are now three distinct statements, all at UNVERIFIED draft level:

1. For every0<eps<=1/100, (12) is an actual changed terminal seed and(18)
   is one actual seven-step parent. All original interpolation rows,
   values, the physical bridge, both child effects, normal energies, and
   true closest distance are supplied explicitly.
2. For that fixed first parent, the next rank-one operation fails at
   original queried rows(2,9) and(6,13) throughout this interval.
3. For every0<eps<=1/100, EVERY transverse coupling at the next join
   fails by the nonnegative four-row combination(35)-(39). This permits
   arbitrary ambient dimension, but fixes the first parent's data and
   all displayed values. The weaker angle estimate remains a separately
   derived diagnostic; it is not the scope of the final separation.

The next concrete unresolved instance can be specified without a solver:
take the SAME formulas(12),(17),(18) with eps=1/25, preserving the word,
rho, and all other definitions. This is still an actual legal seed and
first parent. Indeed Klen>12/5 and eta>2/5 imply
delta*(Klen-1)eta>7/7500>1/1250=E/2, and delta*z>1/600>E/2.
These verify the only potentially delicate seed normals; the sign proof
of(8L),(8R) still applies. The positive cone, terminal price, and exact
energy law are unchanged. Formula(16) remains valid because its monotonic
ratio argument holds for every E>0. This larger amplitude is not excluded
by the bounds used to prove(39), and no legality of its fifteen-step join
is asserted. Its smallest pending parent is the explicit word h[2] with
the true alignment(21), all prescribed values, and all original Q rows.

Another remaining freedom is to change the FIRST seven-step embedding,
even at eps=1/100, and then form a parent. Sections6-7 do not cover
arbitrary earlier couplings. General value-height changes are also outside
this result. Thus this is a proper-family obstruction, not a claim that
all terminal residuals or all nonlinear geometry are impossible.

The physical word was retained throughout. A_j=1+sum h[j] is its actual
shifted stepsize sum, NOT the exact Zhang-Jiang objective denominator
B_(4*2^j-1). The actual seed and seven-step objective lower bounds are
respectively J/R^2 and Z0*J/R^4. No identification of these numbers with
1/B_n is made, no new horizon-dependent word is substituted, and no
asymptotic implication for the original target follows from this finite
construction or this proper-family obstruction.

Background audit only: Taylor, Hendrickx, and Glineur,
"Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance
of First-order Methods", arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem4 proof and Corollary1,
were reread in downloads/1502.05666.txt. Their result is not imported as
a new unverified black box; the used interpolation necessity is the
accepted fact cited above and the realizing projection function is proved
directly in section2.
