<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001336 -->
# Anisotropic dependent Walsh simplices: two uniform boundaries and an actual escape

**JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL REPORT.** Worker max2, project
gd-exact-profile-20260926. One invocation after ended70, allocated ended
ceiling71 / attempted72, controller cap1. Formal submissions are closed.
Every new mathematical assertion below belongs to this one joint draft;
none is a verifier-accepted fact or an independently adopted premise.

Two mechanisms are examined, with quantitative WHOLE-word conclusions.

1. A finite all-character simplex with small but strictly positive mixed
   weights, arbitrary full-dimensional initial direction, and arbitrary
   independent-character scales has attained score

       E <= (1+2/D_m)/D_m,       B_n E < 19/20.

   This includes the explicit rank choice r=m, hence dimension 2^m-1,
   and unbounded conditioning. No coordinate of the actual body is deleted.
   The law's entire supremum is between 1/D_m and (1+2/D_m)/D_m.
2. An exact finite inverse selector allows every vertex at every queried
   point, arbitrary unequal weights, and an arbitrary last queried normal.
   On the actual hierarchy all these proposed vertex itineraries freeze:
   every queried gradient is the SAME vertex. The actual terminal projection
   remains free to enter any face. Every member obeys

       E <= 1/(D_m-(1+a)^2),       B_n E < 19/20.

   The first assertion is proved for all vertex-valued queried trajectories,
   not assumed as a permanent carrier in the initialization. Again the
   law contains an attained score 1/D_m, so no nonvanishing relative gain
   over the COMPLETE existing K/ray channel is possible in this law.

These are scoped boundaries. Neither excludes thick mixed weights with
nonvertex queried projections. An explicit disphenoid calculation below
shows noncommuting edge projections and a legal positive-edge to
negative-edge physical transition. Thus the remaining case is real, and
is not converted into a box or into a fixed scalar line.

## 1. Original target, hierarchy, and provenance

For every integer n>=0, finite nonnegative word h, and L>0, the original
quantity is

    W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2),
    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i),
    W_n^star=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h), W=2R.

Its supremum ranges over all finite Euclidean dimensions, all convex
functions with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, and all
x_0!=x_star. The original every-n/every-h question, including zero and
arbitrarily huge steps, is preserved. The original PROBLEM.md is unchanged.
This report proves neither its affirmative lower bound nor a negative
full-function upper bound.

Retain all integer splits in the benchmark:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,
    Gbench(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) Gbench(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Fix any m>=1536 and keep this SAME enclosing m at every child:

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j,
    S=S_m, D_word=1+2S=D_m, h_leaf=1+a.

The hierarchy names and single-letter symbols in this report are local
to their displayed definitions; they do not redefine unrelated project
glossary entries. Here d_m is an auxiliary ruler, not an appended physical step. All original
leaves and bridges remain, and h_(n-1)=h_leaf. The scalar D_word is distinct
from the geometric body D used below.

Accepted fact 931b9653ce0f7201 was read in full. It supplies ONLY its
unconditional comparison B_n/D_m<47/50 on this hierarchy (and its exact
benchmark definitions). Its full-W hypothesis W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) is
UNPROVED and is not used. Accepted b98bb807d514e80d was read in full. Its
complete channel is

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2 sum h),
      max_(0<=i<n) (h_i-1)_+^2 /
            [(1+sum_(t<i)h_t)^2(1+2sum_(t>i)h_t)]}.

In particular K_n(H_m)>=1/D_m. Comparison to any larger channel, including
the complete ray channel, follows simply because it contains this bound;
no particular cut is substituted for the whole channel.

The FULL reports e9278cb7e2f8f4f9 and 8bf359c98a737933 were read. The former
has a bounded-terminal carried triangle with q>=r; neither guard is used
here. The latter's scalar reduction concerns a centered REGULAR simplex on
a specified two-group line, not arbitrary anisotropic starts. Its carried
nonacute laws require different cone/release guards. They are not premises.
The shared finding bac703f383b0fbee is the lead for the scalar envelope;
Sections 5-6 below rederive all its needed claims jointly. The older
1261bd74709219dc only freezes equal-radius vertex walks when every step is
less than2. Section 9 below proves a different whole-hierarchy result in
the presence of unbounded large bridges. No unverified report is cited as
a correctness source.

## 2. One real body, its exact metric, and every projection guard

Choose any finite integer r>=2, put N=2^r, and label vertices by
s in F_2^r. Coordinates are ALL nonzero t in F_2^r. Set

    chi_t(s)=(-1)^(t dot s), b_t>0,
    z_s=(b_t chi_t(s))_(t!=0) in R^(N-1),
    D=conv{z_s:s in F_2^r}, M=sum_(t!=0)b_t^2.

All weights are fixed before GD. We take unequal weights in the declared
laws. The inner product is the ordinary Euclidean inner product in these
literal coordinates. There is no rescaling of GD to a different metric.
The exact vertex Gram and barycentric metric are

    K_su=sum_(t!=0)b_t^2 chi_t(s)chi_t(u), K_ss=M,
    ||sum_s v_s z_s||^2=sum_(t!=0)b_t^2
                                      (sum_s v_s chi_t(s))^2.       (2.1)

The formula applies to all real coefficient arrays v, including sum v=0.
Pairing group elements differing in a bit proves
sum_s chi_t(s)=0 and sum_s chi_t(s)chi_u(s)=N*1_(t=u).
The N Walsh rows including the constant row are therefore orthogonal.
Consequently the vertices are affinely independent, their sum is zero,
and their actual affine span is all R^(N-1). On the coefficient subspace
sum v=0 the eigenvalues of K are exactly N b_t^2. This identifies the
metric, including arbitrarily small or large weights, rather than assuming
an expected Gram.

Every real x has UNIQUE barycentric coordinates summing to one:

    p_s(x)=[1+sum_(t!=0) chi_t(s) x_t/b_t]/N,
    x=sum_s p_s(x) z_s.                                      (2.2)

Both identities follow by the displayed row orthogonality. Thus x belongs
to D exactly when p_s(x)>=0 for EVERY original label s. In particular zero
has all coordinates 1/N and lies in the interior. The actual inward facet
normals are w_s=(chi_t(s)/b_t)_(t!=0); the inequalities are
<w_s,x>>=-1. The inverse weights in these normals are not free prices.

Define ONE static function

    F_D(x)=max_(g in D) [<x,g>-||g||^2/2].                    (2.3)

The maximizer is unique by strict convexity of squared distance. It is the
Euclidean projection g=P_D(x), characterized exactly by

    g in D, <x-g,z_s-g><=0 for EVERY original s.              (2.4)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance along segments to
each vertex. Sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-v||^2 and using convex
combinations of the vertices. Summing the two normal inequalities at x,y
proves

    <x-y,P_D(x)-P_D(y)> >= ||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2.

Hence P_D is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the maximizing expressions in (2.3)
shows a first-order remainder between zero and ||x-y||^2. This proves
that F_D is differentiable with gradient P_D. It is convex and finite as
a maximum of affine functions over a compact body. In a neighborhood of
zero, projection is the identity, so its least smoothness constant is1.

The complete attained minimum is exactly {0}. Indeed F_D(0)=0; for x!=0,
choose t in (0,1) small enough that tx belongs to D. Then
F_D(x)>=(t-t^2/2)||x||^2>0. Consequently the OWN closest radius at any
x_0!=0 is exactly R_init=||x_0||. Multiplying F_D by L preserves every
point and score under the physical steps h_i/L.

For clarity there is a finite exact face formula for ALL actual queries.
For a nonempty subset A of labels choose its first label s0, let E_A have
columns z_s-z_s0 for s in A other than s0, and set

    eta_A=(E_A^T E_A)^(-1)E_A^T(x-z_s0),
    g_A=z_s0+E_A eta_A.                                    (2.5)

For a singleton put g_A=z_s0 and omit the empty matrix. All other displayed
inverses exist by affine independence. Retain A precisely when every
coefficient eta_A is nonnegative, their sum is at most1, and
<x-g_A,z_s-g_A><=0 for EVERY label s. Choose the first retained subset
in increasing-cardinality, then lexicographic order. Existence follows
from the true projection and its minimal face: differentiating along that
face's tangent directions gives exactly (2.5). All retained candidates
are the same point by (2.4). Thus equality guards, repeated faces, and
boundary projections are included. This finite formula is not executed
and is not an optimization oracle used to fit an initialization.

## 3. Every original sample and ordered interpolation row

For any initialized body and point define the original physical samples
by the finite map (2.5):

    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=P_D(x_i),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, 0<=i<=n,
    r_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=<r_i,g_i>.

The terminal g_n is the same body's ACTUAL projection, not an imposed
completion. Comparison with zero in (2.4) gives delta_i>=0. For every
ordered pair i,j, including both terminal directions, expansion gives

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                                (3.1)

Include x_star=g_star=f_star=0: then Q_(i,star)=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-delta_i<=0. All gradients, values, original ordered pairs,
and full Gram entries are fixed by actual real vectors. The full Gram on
(x_0,g_0,...,g_n) is PSD by its definition as a real Gram. No restricted
formal-space PSD certificate for W is being advertised.

The exact attained score in either law is

    E=[2<x_n,g_n>-||g_n||^2]/||x_0||^2.                    (3.2)

Sections 7 and 9 bound this WHOLE-word quantity uniformly, beyond merely
specifying a symbolic recurrence.

## 4. Mechanism I: an explicit all-character small-mixed-weight law

Let e_1,...,e_r denote the standard labels in F_2^r. These are labels of
coordinates, not a replacement of the physical space. Split the physical
coordinates into the r labels e_j and the remaining mixed labels

    T_mix={t!=0:t is not one of e_1,...,e_r}, k_mix=N-1-r.

All mixed coordinates stay present and have positive weights. Define

    delta=(sum_(t in T_mix)b_t^2)^(1/2),
    P_amp=product_(i=0)^(n-1) max(1,|1-h_i|).

This is a literal finite product determined by the word, not an evaluated
recurrence. It is finite and >=1, even for zero or huge steps.

The law is initialized by choosing any finite r>=2 (in particular the
explicit word-dependent choice r=m), any R>0, ANY unit vector u in the
FULL R^(N-1), any positive independent weights b_(e_j), and any positive
mixed profile alpha_t with sum alpha_t^2=1. Choose 0<tau<=1 and set

    x_0=R u,
    delta=tau R/[64 D_m^3(1+S)^2 P_amp^2],
    b_t=delta alpha_t for every mixed t.                    (4.1)

Require the complete weight vector to be nonconstant. No relative-size
restriction among the independent weights is needed for the proof.
For a completely explicit word-dependent sublaw take r=m,
b_(e_j)=j R/D_m, enumerate the mixed labels in binary order t_1,...,t_k,
and take

    alpha_(t_l)=sqrt(3/(1-4^(-k)))*2^(-l), 1<=l<=k.

The geometric sum proves the normalization exactly. The independent
weights and mixed weights are distinct in this sublaw. Initial direction
u remains arbitrary, including nonzero coordinates in EVERY mixed
character. For a single specified start use u equal to the physical unit
vector at coordinate e_1 and tau=1. No initialization optimization is
needed in either the family definition or this representative.

The price condition delta/R is deliberately stated. This law is not a
closure of all anisotropic weights. Its quantitative scale is justified
by the whole-word stability calculation below, not by discarding a
coordinate or silently changing smoothness.

Write a physical point as x=(v,w), where v has independent-character
coordinates and w has mixed-character coordinates. Let

    C=product_(j=1)^r [-b_(e_j),b_(e_j)] in R^r,
    F_C(v)=sum_(j=1)^r b_(e_j)^2 Hub(v_j/b_(e_j)),
    Hub(s)=s^2/2 for |s|<=1, Hub(s)=|s|-1/2 for |s|>=1.

C is a COMPARISON projection of D, not the actual body. Indeed all 2^r
sign patterns of the independent coordinates occur among the real
vertices. Their convex hull is C. More explicitly, for c_j/b_(e_j) in
[-1,1], the nonnegative barycentric weights

    pi_s=product_j [1+(c_j/b_(e_j))chi_(e_j)(s)]/2

sum to1 and give independent coordinates c. They define ONE real point
of D, including its mixed coordinates, whose mixed norm is at most delta.
This is a convex combination in one fixed body, not an average of separate
trajectories or a formal expected Gram. Every point of D has mixed norm
at most delta by the triangle inequality, since every vertex has that
mixed norm.

## 5. Scalar whole-word input, derived jointly: absorption and plateau

We now prove the complete symmetric-Huber bound needed for the comparison.
No result from the unverified bac703f383b0fbee is assumed.

Coarse elementary bounds for m>=1536 are

    beta>99/100, 2<c<5/2, 1/3<a<1/2.

For example sqrt(2)<29/20 and epsilon<1/100 give c<4949/2000<5/2;
a>99/250>1/3 and a<1/lambda<1/2. Put v_mass=a/(c-2).
Since lambda(lambda-2)=1, c>lambda and beta<1 give 0<v_mass<1.
The exact mass formula, verified by substituting A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, is

    A_j=(1-v_mass)2^j+v_mass c^j.                         (5.1)

Thus d_j/A_j<=c-2<1/2, d_j<A_j, and A_j<c^j for j>=1.

Let T_(1+d)(s)=s-(1+d)clip(s,[-1,1]), and let Psi_j be its actual
chronological composition over H_j. Then
Psi_(j+1)=Psi_j o T_(1+d_j) o Psi_j, with Psi_0 the identity.
Every map is continuous and odd. We claim

    |s|<=A_j => |Psi_j(s)|<=1,
    |s|>=A_j => Psi_j(s)=sign(s)(|s|-S_j).                 (5.2)

The second formula follows because every prefix of a word of total mass
S_j leaves magnitude at least1 on the original side. For the first,
induct from A_0=1. If |s|<=A_j, the first child outputs magnitude <=1,
the bridge outputs magnitude <=d_j<A_j, and the second child gives <=1.
For A_j<s<=A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, the first output is s-S_j>1 and the bridge
output is s-A_j-d_j in (-d_j,A_j]. Apply the second child and oddness.
This proves (5.2), including thresholds.

For radius z>=0, the image of [-z,z] under T_(1+d) is the symmetric
interval of radius

    E_d(z)=d z if z<=1;
           d if 1<=z<=1+2d;
           z-1-d if z>=1+2d.                            (5.3)

For z>=1 this follows by maximizing the absolute values on the inner
linear branch and the exterior affine branch; the maximum is
max(d,|z-1-d|). The pieces agree at both thresholds.

Put J_j(z)=max_(|s|<=z)|Psi_j(s)|. Continuity and oddness make the image
EXACTLY [-J_j(z),J_j(z)], so no incompatible child histories are chosen:

    J_0(z)=z, J_(j+1)=J_j o E_(d_j) o J_j.              (5.4)

All J_j are nondecreasing. By (5.2), J_j(z)<=1 for z<=A_j and
J_j(z)=z-S_j for z>=A_j.

Put b_plateau=a beta. Then 0<b_plateau<1/2 and c b_plateau=beta^2<1.
For every 2<=j<=m,

    A_(j-1)<d_j<A_(j-1)+2d_(j-1).                       (5.5)

For the lower inequality at j=2, multiplying d_2-A_1 by c gives

    beta c^2-2c-beta=2epsilon-lambda^2 epsilon^2(1+epsilon)>0.

The sign uses lambda^2<6 and 6epsilon(1+epsilon)<2. If d_j>A_(j-1),
then d_(j+1)-A_j=(c-1/c)d_j-2A_(j-1)>0 because c-1/c>2.
The upper inequality follows from
(c-2)d_(j-1)<A_(j-1), since c-2<1 and d_(j-1)<A_(j-1).

It follows that

    J_j(d_j)=b_plateau for every 1<=j<=m.                 (5.6)

At j=1, d_1=beta<1 and J_1=E_a give a beta. For j>=2 the first
child sends d_j to d_j-S_(j-1), strictly between1 and 1+2d_(j-1).
The bridge envelope sends this whole radius to d_(j-1), and the second
child sends that radius to b_plateau by induction. This proves the exact
plateau through every physical level.

## 6. Scalar all-amplitude bound, including every interval between levels

For every j>=1 and every integer k>=0 in the preceding scope,

    J_j(d_j b_plateau^k)<=b_plateau^(k+1).                (6.1)

Induct simultaneously in j for all k. At j=1 the input is beta times
b_plateau^k<1 and (5.3) gives equality. The case k=0 is (5.6).
For k>=1 and j>=2,

    d_j b_plateau^k=beta^2 d_(j-1)b_plateau^(k-1)
                    <=d_(j-1)b_plateau^(k-1).

The first child thus outputs at most b_plateau^k<=1. The bridge's
inner branch gives at most d_(j-1)b_plateau^k, and the second child
gives at most b_plateau^(k+1). This proves (6.1).

For every real 0<z<=d_j, choose the unique geometric interval with

    d_j b_plateau^(k+1)<z<=d_j b_plateau^k.

Endpoint equality is placed at the upper endpoint. Monotonicity and
(6.1) give J_j(z)<=b_plateau^(k+1)<z/d_j. For d_j<z<=A_j,
(5.2) gives J_j(z)<=1<z/d_j. At zero the image is zero. Consequently

    |Psi_j(s)|<=|s|/d_j for every |s|<=A_j.               (6.2)

This controls EVERY real amplitude, all inverse threshold preimages,
both signs, and all repeated core entries, rather than a finite mesh.

Since a>1/3, A_m<c^m and c^m>2^m>=32>18,

    d_m^2>c^(2m)/9>2c^m>D_m.

For 0<|s|<=A_m the actual terminal is in the quadratic core, so
2Hub(Psi_m(s))/s^2<=1/d_m^2<1/D_m. For |s|=z>=A_m,
(5.2) gives exact payoff (2z-D_m)/z^2. The identity

    z^2-D_m(2z-D_m)=(z-D_m)^2

bounds it by1/D_m, attained exactly at z=D_m. Including s=0 by
continuity yields the homogeneous inequality

    2Hub(Psi_m(s))<=s^2/D_m for EVERY real s.             (6.3)

For ANY positive fixed thresholds b_(e_j), the comparison box runs r
independent copies scaled by those thresholds. Summing (6.3) proves

    2F_C(y_n)<=||y_0||^2/D_m                             (6.4)

for all comparison starts and every finite r. This is the only scalar
input to the perturbation calculation; it has been proved within the
same joint draft.

## 7. All-history perturbation estimate with the FULL initial radius

Let x_i=(v_i,w_i) be the ACTUAL anisotropic-simplex run, with actual
projection g_i=(p_i,q_i). Let y_i be the comparison-box GD initialized at
y_0=v_0. No initial mixed coordinate is required to vanish. Write R=||x_0||,
so ||v_0||<=R and ||w_0||<=R separately; the real denominator is their
combined squared norm R^2, never the independent part alone.

Because ||q_i||<=delta, every physical prefix obeys

    ||w_i||<=||w_0||+delta sum_(t<i)h_t<=R+S delta.        (7.1)

Fix one such physical input (v,w), its actual projection (p,q), and the
box projection c0=P_C(v). Section4 supplies a lift (c0,q0) in D with
||q0||<=delta. Projection optimality and the box normal condition give

    ||v-p||^2+||w-q||^2<=||v-c0||^2+||w-q0||^2,
    ||v-p||^2>=||v-c0||^2+||p-c0||^2.

Thus

    ||p-c0||^2
       <=(||w||+delta)^2-max(||w||-delta,0)^2
       <=4delta||w||+delta^2
       <=4delta R+(4S+1)delta^2.                        (7.2)

For ||w||>=delta the middle difference is 4delta||w||; for ||w||<delta,
its displayed bound follows from ||w||^2<=2delta||w||. This proves
(7.2) uniformly over all active faces, including threshold ties. No face
sequence, small independent threshold, or large transverse start breaks it.

For a scalar clipped gradient, the update s -> s-h clip(s,[-b,b]) is
continuous and piecewise affine with slopes 1-h and1. Its Lipschitz
constant is at most ell_h=max(1,|1-h|). The Cartesian comparison map
has the same Euclidean Lipschitz bound, by summing squared component
bounds. Set t_mix=delta/R and

    e_proj=R sqrt(4t_mix+(4S+1)t_mix^2).

From (7.2), for e_i=||v_i-y_i||,

    e_(i+1)<=ell_(h_i)e_i+h_i e_proj, e_0=0.

Expanding this finite inequality symbolically in the original order gives

    e_n<=e_proj sum_(i<n) h_i product_(i<j<n)ell_(h_j)
        <=e_proj S P_amp.                               (7.3)

No recurrence has been executed. Large-step amplification is paid by the
actual product P_amp in (4.1). In particular it is not replaced by the
scalar quadratic product, which would generally be an invalid bound.

For the comparison function, ||grad F_C||^2<=2F_C. In one coordinate this
is equality in the core and follows from b^2<=2b|s|-b^2 outside; sum over
coordinates. Therefore sqrt(2F_C) is 1-Lipschitz: apply the gradient bound
to sqrt(2F_C+eta), integrate along a segment, then let eta decrease to0.
By (6.4) and (7.3),

    sqrt(2F_C(v_n))/R
      <=1/sqrt(D_m)+S P_amp sqrt(4t_mix+(4S+1)t_mix^2).   (7.4)

The full actual function satisfies for every (v,w)

    F_D(v,w)<=F_C(v)+delta||w||,                         (7.5)

by dropping -||q||^2/2 from (2.3), bounding <w,q> by delta||w||,
and maximizing the independent part over C. Combining (7.1), (7.4),
and (7.5) proves the useful unrestricted sensitivity interface

    E <= [D_m^(-1/2)+S P_amp
                      sqrt(4t_mix+(4S+1)t_mix^2)]^2
                          +2t_mix(1+S t_mix).            (7.6)

It holds for arbitrary positive mixed weights and arbitrary full starts;
only the evaluated small-weight consequence below uses (4.1).

Under (4.1),

    0<t_mix<=1/[64 D_m^3(1+S)^2 P_amp^2].                (7.7)

Since D_m>=1, P_amp>=1, and 4S+1<=4(1+S)^2, one has
(4S+1)t_mix<=1/16<1. Consequently the square of the second term in
brackets in (7.6) is at most

    5 S^2 P_amp^2 t_mix <=5/(64D_m^3)<1/(4D_m^3).

Also S t_mix<=1, so 2t_mix(1+S t_mix)<=4t_mix<=1/(16D_m^3).
All quantities are nonnegative. Substitution yields

    E <= (1+1/(2D_m))^2/D_m+1/(16D_m^3)
       = 1/D_m+1/D_m^2+5/(16D_m^3)
       <= (1+2/D_m)/D_m.                               (7.8)

This is uniform over every parameter in Mechanism I, every initial full
unit direction, every positive independent scale, every mixed profile,
every finite rank including r=m, and every m>=1536.

The lower bracket is attained inside the same law. Let q_j^axis be the
physical unit vector at coordinate label e_j. On this axis, averaging labels with chi_(e_j)=+1 or -1 gives the two points
+/-b_(e_j) q_j^axis in D; all other nonzero characters average to zero. Every
vertex has axis coordinate +/-b_(e_j), so the normal condition proves

    P_D(s q_j^axis)=clip(s,[-b_(e_j),b_(e_j)]) q_j^axis.

Choose x_0=D_m b_(e_1)q_1^axis, set R=D_m b_(e_1), and choose ANY mixed
weights satisfying (4.1). The full run remains on this axis, has unique
minimum0 and radius R, and attains E=1/D_m by (6.3). This is a calibration
member; the upper theorem is not confined to it. Thus

    1 <= D_m sup_(Mechanism I) E <=1+2/D_m.              (7.9)

Since S>n>=m>=1536, D_m>3073>188. Accepted931 gives

    B_n E < (47/50)(1+2/D_m)<19/20.

Moreover E/K_n(H_m)<=1+2/D_m, and the same bound holds relative to any
larger complete channel. The right side tends to1. There is no assertion
that E<=K_n exactly, nor that (7.8) bounds unrestricted W.

## 8. Mechanism II: an exact vertex inverse selector, with arbitrary weights

This is an attempted changing-vertex construction. A common gradient is
NOT imposed. Choose any finite r>=2, any positive unequal weights b_t
with no small-mixed-weight condition, and the original hierarchy. For
example one explicit word-dependent dictionary is r=m and
b_t=1+integer_binary_label(t). Choose a label s_last, put p=z_(s_last),
and choose ANY vector v satisfying the finite normal inequalities

    <v,z_s-p><=0 for every s.                           (8.1)

This normal is unbounded and need not be orthogonal to p. Initialize the
last queried point x_(n-1)=p+v. Its actual projection is p by (2.4).

Now define earlier points backwards for i=n-2,...,0. Given y=x_(i+1)
and h=h_i>1, test EVERY original vertex z_s by the explicit inequalities

    <y+(h-1)z_s,z_u-z_s><=0 for EVERY u.                (8.2)

Among the vertices passing these tests select one minimizing the finite
list of numbers ||y+h z_s||^2, with lexicographic tie breaking. Put
x_i=y+h z_s. This is a fixed finite inverse selector with all guards
specified, not a PEP restatement or an optimization oracle for a start.
It can alternatively allow arbitrary choices among valid vertices; the
bound below covers that larger family as well.

The list is nonempty. Choose any label maximizing <y,z_s> among the
finite labels. Since all ||z_s||^2=M and <z_s,z_u><=M,

    <y+(h-1)z_s,z_u-z_s>
      =<y,z_u-z_s>+(h-1)(<z_s,z_u>-M)<=0.

This proves existence but does NOT replace the specified minimum-norm
selector by that particular maximizing vertex. For every retained vertex,
(8.2) is exactly the normal test for x_i-z_s, so its actual gradient is
z_s and x_i-h_i z_s=y. All n original queried gradients are vertices.
Set the ACTUAL terminal x_n=x_(n-1)-h_leaf p, and obtain its gradient
and value by (2.5)-(2.3), with no vertex or core restriction at the terminal.
Everything is finite for each m. This inverse dictionary is a textual
construction; no enumeration or trajectory was executed.

## 9. Why every vertex-query itinerary freezes on the entire H_m

We prove the stronger claim: for this body and the same enclosing m,
for every j>=1, ANY actual run over H_j whose gradients at all pre-step
queries are vertices has a constant queried gradient. No hypothesis on
the block's terminal projection is made.

First, for two consecutive vertex gradients p,q with common squared norm
M, projection's firm inequality and the physical update give

    ||p-q||^2 <= h <p,p-q> = (h/2)||p-q||^2.

If h<2, they must coincide. This observation alone is insufficient on H_m,
which has very large bridges; the following induction includes them.

At j=1 there is one queried point, so the claim holds. Suppose it holds
for H_j, j>=1, and consider H_(j+1). The queried gradients on the first
child are some vertex p, and those on the second child are some vertex q,
by the induction hypothesis. The last leaf of the first child has step
h_leaf<2, and its output is the bridge query, also a vertex by assumption.
The preceding observation forces the bridge gradient to be p as well.

Let X be the bridge point, b_bridge=1+d_j, and let Y be the last QUERY
of the second child, not its terminal. The literal physical displacements
are

    Y=X-b_bridge p-(S_j-h_leaf)q.                       (9.1)

If p!=q put C_gap=M-<p,q>=||p-q||^2/2>0. The original normal conditions
at X (projection p) and Y (projection q) respectively give

    <X,p-q>>=C_gap, <Y,p-q><=-C_gap.

Substituting (9.1) yields the necessary condition

    b_bridge-(S_j-h_leaf)>=2,
    equivalently d_j+h_leaf>=A_j.                      (9.2)

The actual SAME-m hierarchy has the strict reverse inequality. From (5.1),

    A_j-d_j=(1-v_mass)2^j+v_mass(3-c)c^j.

Both coefficients are positive because 0<v_mass<1 and c<5/2<3.
Both powers strictly increase in j. At j=1,

    A_1-d_1=2+a-beta>1+a=h_leaf

because beta<1. Thus A_j-d_j>h_leaf for every j>=1. This contradicts
(9.2), proving p=q. Induction proves constant queried gradients on all
H_j through H_m. Every parameter uses the same enclosing m.

This proof closes every queried-vertex itinerary in Mechanism II,
including arbitrary inverse tie choices and arbitrary anisotropy. It is
not a reparameterized carrier hypothesis: the attempted all-vertex
switching is shown impossible using a remote original normal inequality
at the last query of a complete child. It also strengthens the older
h_i<2-only obstruction, since no bound is placed on the higher bridges.

Write p for this now-proved common queried gradient and M=||p||^2.
All actual queried points, terminal, and full initial radius reduce to

    x_i=x_0-(sum_(t<i)h_t)p, 0<=i<=n,
    x_0=(S-h_leaf+1)p+v,
    x_n=(1-h_leaf)p+v,
    R_init^2=||(S-h_leaf+1)p+v||^2.                    (9.3)

The terminal gradient is the unrestricted projection (2.5) at x_n and its
value is (2.3). Formula (9.3) is an exact evaluated all-history interface;
no normal component has been made free in the denominator.

Let T=<x_0,p>/M. At the last query, comparing its normal with zero in D
gives T>=S-h_leaf+1>0. Also R_init^2>=M T^2 by Cauchy--Schwarz.
The literal outgoing terminal row Q_(n,n-1)<=0 gives, after completing
the square in the ACTUAL terminal gradient,

    2F_D(x_n)
      <=2f_(n-1)+[(h_leaf-1)^2-1]M
      =M[2T-D_m+h_leaf^2].                             (9.4)

For explicit verification of the first line, that row says
F_D(x_n)<=f_(n-1)-h_leaf<g_n,p>-||g_n-p||^2/2.
Complete the square in g_n+(h_leaf-1)p. The last queried value is
f_(n-1)=<x_0,p>-(S-h_leaf)M-M/2, yielding the second line.
No incoming terminal row is needed for this upper estimate, but the actual
function already satisfies all of them by (3.1).

Put L_mass=D_m-h_leaf^2>0. Combining (9.3)-(9.4), and using

    T^2-L_mass(2T-L_mass)=(T-L_mass)^2>=0,

gives the quantitative WHOLE-law bound

    E<= (2T-L_mass)/T^2 <=1/(D_m-h_leaf^2).              (9.5)

The numerator is nonnegative for an actual member, because it bounds its
nonnegative terminal value. Since h_leaf<3/2, h_leaf^2<9/4<3 and D_m>3073,

    B_n E < (47/50) D_m/(D_m-3)<19/20.                 (9.6)

The last comparison requires only D_m>285. The exact initial choice
x_0=D_m p is admitted: choose v=(S+h_leaf)p in (8.1). All its normals
are valid because <p,z_s-p><=0. It gives constant gradients through the
terminal and E=1/D_m. Even the specified selector admits this start:
for the fixed last point, the freeze theorem forces every admissible
backwards sequence to use p. Thus

    1 <=D_m sup_(Mechanism II)E
        <=D_m/(D_m-h_leaf^2) ->1.                       (9.7)

The same ratio bound holds relative to the COMPLETE K_n and any channel
containing K_n. This is uniform in rank, all weights, all last normals,
and all inverse selections. It is not an upper bound on W_n(H_m).

## 10. A genuine remaining escape: noncommuting disphenoid edges

The weighted dictionary does not reduce to a separable box or a scalar
process once nonvertex projections are admitted. We give an explicit
geometric counterexample to those possible overextensions. It is a local
physical example, not a whole-H_m payoff assertion.

Take r=2, coordinates t=10,01,11, and weights (B,C,E)>0. The four
vertices are

    (B,C,E), (B,-C,-E), (-B,C,-E), (-B,-C,E).

All three nonzero characters occur; the third is the product of the first
two and is not an independent sign. The two opposite edges with first
coordinate +B and -B have transverse directions

    v_plus=(C,E), v_minus=(C,-E), ell^2=C^2+E^2,
    P_plus=v_plus v_plus^T/ell^2,
    P_minus=v_minus v_minus^T/ell^2.

For x=(u,y), the EXACT positive-edge projection is
(B,P_plus y) under the two complete guards

    |<y,v_plus>|<=ell^2,
    2B(u-B)>=|<y-P_plus y,v_minus>|.                    (10.1)

The first guard puts the candidate on its edge. Its residual is
(u-B,y-P_plus y); it is perpendicular to the positive edge. Comparing
with both negative-edge vertices gives the second guard, and comparisons
with the positive endpoints give equality. Thus every original vertex is
checked. The negative-edge formula is (-B,P_minus y), with guards

    |<y,v_minus>|<=ell^2,
    2B(-u-B)>=|<y-P_minus y,v_plus>|.                   (10.2)

On strict cells, the transverse update is y -> (I-h P_plus)y or
(I-h P_minus)y. The two projectors need not commute. For B=3,C=2,E=1,

    P_plus=(1/5)[[4,2],[2,1]],
    P_minus=(1/5)[[4,-2],[-2,1]],
    P_plus P_minus-P_minus P_plus
                       =(1/25)[[0,-12],[12,0]] !=0.    (10.3)

There is also an actual physical transition between these cells. Let h>2,
put eta=(h-2)/(16h^2)>0, and initialize

    x=(3h/2,eta,0), y=eta(1,0).

The positive-edge tangent coefficient is 2eta/5<1. Its residual paired
with v_minus is 4eta/5<h-2<9(h-2), so (10.1) holds strictly. After the actual
step the point is

    x'=(-3h/2,y'), y'=eta(1-4h/5,-2h/5).

Since ||I-hP_plus||=h-1, one has ||y'||<1/16, so the negative-edge tangent
guard is strict. With n_minus=(1,2) perpendicular to v_minus,

    |<y'-P_minus y',v_plus>|
          =(4eta/5)|1-8h/5|
          <=2h eta < h-2 < 9(h-2).

The middle inequality holds for h>2 because
(4/5)(1+8h/5)<2h. This proves (10.2) strictly. Thus both projected
gradients are in relative interiors of different nonparallel edges; all
four original vertices participate across the two supports. The residual
first coordinates have magnitudes 3(h/2-1)>0, so these are genuine exterior
positive-height projections. The whole function remains (2.3), with
unique minimum0 and true radius sqrt(9h^2/4+eta^2). All its ordered rows
hold by (3.1).

One may choose h=1+d_2, an actual bridge value of the diagnostic word,
since beta c>1. This still describes only a local transition: it does NOT
assert a paid full-word initial state reaching x at that bridge, nor a
useful full-word terminal. The example is not being promoted to a lower
bound at B_n. It establishes precisely that nonvertex projection allows
real noncommutation; it does not by itself prescribe a whole-H_m
itinerary. All three weights (3,2,1) are positive and pairwise unequal;
C!=E is exactly what makes (10.3) nonzero.

A separate immediate scope check rules out extending the whole-H_m
constancy assertion to nonvertex queried gradients. Put
r_in=1/sqrt(sum_(t!=0)b_t^(-2)); (2.2) shows that the open ball of this
radius is inside D. Take any nonzero x_0 with ||x_0||<r_in/2. Since
a<1/2, its first two gradients on the original H_m are g_0=x_0 and
g_1=-a x_0, both in the interior and distinct. The same static function
continues legally through all remaining physical steps. This is a
full-word existence counterexample to constancy without the vertex
assumption, with no assertion of a useful terminal score.

## 11. Scope, remaining interface, and author audit

Mechanism I fails uniformly because the coupled mixed-coordinate force,
including an arbitrary mixed initial component, is too small after its
ENTIRE large-step amplification is paid. The precise smallness scale is
(7.7). It is sufficient, not claimed necessary or optimal. The actual
function is full-dimensional and nonseparable. Replacing its projection
by box clipping at any step would be false; (7.2)-(7.6) quantify the error
without doing so. Allowing thicker mixed weights is a genuine surviving
parameter direction, not a counterexample to the proved smallness law.

Mechanism II permits arbitrary weight ratios and attempted changes of
vertex, but its all-queried-vertices requirement is load-bearing. The
remote original normal conditions force constancy. The terminal is not
assumed to be a vertex and its original outgoing row is paid. Nonvertex
queried faces, with their changing norms and noncommuting tangent maps,
are not covered. Section10 realizes the local geometric distinction.

The surviving substantive task is now explicit: choose thick mixed weights
and a fully paid initial state whose ORIGINAL full-H_m nonvertex face
sequence has a useful terminal. Formula (2.5) supplies all exact guards,
and (3.2) supplies the true-radius target, but the general full-word
terminal/radius estimate in that domain is UNRESOLVED. A symbolic face
recurrence alone is not counted as success. No third mechanism is opened.

Author checks within this unverified draft:

* All new mathematical inputs, including the complete scalar profile,
  are jointly derived rather than imported from sibling awareness.
* Every body has all nonzero characters with strictly positive weights;
  the coordinate relations, real metric, and full Euclidean span are given.
* Each function is one static globally smooth convex function, with
  complete attained minimum {0}; no closest-radius transfer is used.
* All actual queries and both orientations of every original interpolation
  row follow the same full-body normal condition, including the terminal.
* The thin-weight law includes arbitrary full initial directions; its
  mixed initial norm is explicitly paid in (7.1), (7.2), and (7.6).
* Its amplification is product max(1,|1-h|), not an illicit contraction
  product; the smallness condition is stated at exactly that scale.
* The scalar proof covers every real amplitude and threshold equality by
  continuous interval images. Both full children retain the enclosing m.
* The vertex induction uses the LAST QUERIED point of the right child,
  with suffix mass S_j-h_leaf, so it does not impose a terminal vertex.
* Its initial normal is unrestricted and its full norm appears in (9.3).
* Both upper results concern declared witness families. Neither is a full
  original-vector PEP dual, an unrestricted W upper, nor a universal lower.
* The local disphenoid transition has no full-word performance claim.
* Zero and huge-step quantifiers in the original target remain untouched.

No useful nonvanishing improvement over the complete K/ray channel was
obtained. No original-target resolution or new accepted fact is claimed.
The original target remains OPEN.

## 12. Retrieval and resource record

Skills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving, and

Matlas returned arXiv1512.02940 Theorems2.4 and2.6, and arXiv2105.04227

That paper distinguishes vertex-difference Gramians from facet-normal
Gramians and relates their signs to nonobtuse dihedral geometry. Its

simplices. The transferable point is that physical projection guards
involve the actual inverse-weight normals, not a freely rescaled regular

# Complete bipartite normals on one finite affine-height triangle

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max4, invocation90, project
gd-exact-profile-20260926. Governing guidance8b730bb4f0c90f3c and
plan1b27e032a28c6b35. This is the one allocated invocation after89.
Exactly two analytical mechanisms were opened. Mathematical programs,
numerical/trajectory/recurrence replays, formal submissions, consults and
subagents: all ZERO. No new fact_id is claimed.

All new mathematical statements in this report are parts of ONE joint
unverified derivation. Earlier unverified reports are awareness, not
predecessors. Accepted d8b59c7d92425121 supplies only the smoothness and
unique-minimum properties of the specified affine-height envelope.
Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the original benchmark context; its
conditional full-class upper-bound hypothesis is NOT established here.
The original all-word/all-horizon minimax problem remains OPEN, with the
original performance normalization W=2R unchanged.

## 1. Outcomes and limits

The complete ordered interpolation rows do have the proposed bipartite
factorization, in the original orientation. The factor is a normal
coefficient at the SOURCE of the interpolation row times a proximal
length at its TARGET. The corresponding gradient-body normal reverses
those roles. Sections3 and7 derive both identities, including ties,
terminal rows and optimum rows.

Mechanism1 tests the following explicit nonnegative pairing of ALL
original sample rows. Give query i its actual step weight k_i=h_i and
give the original terminal weight k_n=1. Put A=1+sum h_i, D=2A-1, and

    lambda_ij=2D*k_i*k_j/A^2, 0<=i,j<=n.

The proposed certificate is the original endpoint slack expressed as
this weighted normal payment plus a nonnegative remainder. This pairing
FAILS on a single explicit finite triangle and a strict-side actual
trajectory through the ENTIRE original H_m, for EVERY m>=1536. Indeed,
the normal payment exceeds even the initial squared radius by more than
9A^2/(1200a^2). Thus no nonnegative remainder can make this particular
pairing work. The same obstruction excludes coordinatewise larger
nonnegative row multipliers, including adding nonnegative optimum-row
payments. It does not exclude other weights or prove a failure of the
actual objective bound.

Mechanism2, opened only after that decisive first obstruction, rederives
the fixed sample-gradient body and its reduction to at most five listed
generators. It tests ONE complete-child law: contraction of gradient
norm squared above the minimum squared norm of the fixed sampled-gradient
hull, at the exact same-face normal-product rate. The same legal entire
H_m trajectory refutes it with a quantified gap greater than111/1024.
The original first leaf passes this test. The later constant-gradient
children expose its missing term: the fixed two-face reference energy
lies strictly below the energy of the occupied face.

Both failures use the original start and full original terminal. The
example's actual original-radius score is nevertheless strictly below1/D,
as is every finite orthogonal tilt of that example. No arbitrary-switch
upper bound, full-triangle upper bound, full-W upper bound, or minimax
resolution is claimed. No third mechanism is opened.

## 2. Setup, parameters, and complete side guards

Fix m>=1536. All children inherit these SAME parameters:

    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))*(1+eps),
    a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D_j=1+2S_j,
    n=2^m-1, S=S_m, A=A_m, D=D_m.

The hierarchy symbols here have the local meanings of accepted931.
No original physical step is omitted or replaced. In particular the
first physical leaf has h=1+a. Define pi_0=1 and, for j>=1, let pi_j
be the magnitude of the product of1-h_i over a complete H_j. Directly
from the concatenation,

    A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,
    pi_(j+1)=d_j*pi_j^2,
    pi_j=beta^(2^j-1)*c^(-j).                         (2.1)

The signed product for nonempty H_j is -pi_j. Indeed its length is odd,
and all of its steps exceed1. The formula for pi follows by substituting
the displayed expression into its recurrence and checking j=0.

Let eta=a/(c-2). Since c>1+sqrt(2)>2 and c(c-2)>1>beta,
one has0<eta<1. Substitution in the mass recurrence proves

    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,
    A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    A_j>=d_j/(c-2),
    D_j*pi_j^2<2*c^(-j)<1 for j>=1.                  (2.2)

We will use these coarse uniform bounds:

    2/5<a<5/12, 2<c<5/2, A>=100.                   (2.3)

For the lower bound on a, sqrt(2)>24/17 implies sqrt(2)-1>7/17.
Also(1-eps)/(1+eps)>99/100, since199eps<1. Thus
a>(7/17)*(99/100)=693/1700>2/5. For the upper bound,
sqrt(2)<17/12 gives a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12. The bound c<5/2
follows from sqrt(2)<17/12 and eps<=1/6144. Each step exceeds1,
so A>=2^m>=128, in particular A>=100. All these estimates are textual
inequalities; no word or recurrence was evaluated by a program.

Fix any finite L,H>0 and write

    R_tri=sqrt(L^2+H^2), u=L/R_tri, v=H/R_tri,
    K=conv{0,(L,H),(L,-H)}, alpha=(1,0),
    e_sigma=(u,sigma*v), n_sigma=(-v,sigma*u),
    phi(z)=min_(y in K)[alpha.y+||z-y||^2/2].

Here sigma is+1 or-1; u,v>0 and u^2+v^2=1. R_tri is a side
length, not the problem's performance normalization. The envelope has
gradient z-P_K(z-alpha), minimum0 and complete minimum set{0}, by
the target-class part of acceptedd8b59c7d92425121: alpha strictly
exposes0 in this compact triangle.

In a side frame define r=e_sigma.z, w=n_sigma.z. The closed
sloping-side cell is precisely

    u<=r<=u+R_tri, w>=-v.                            (2.4)

To verify it, write z-alpha=(r-u)e_sigma+(w+v)n_sigma.
The proposed projection y=(r-u)e_sigma lies on the finite side.
The residual is a nonnegative multiple of its outward normal and has
nonpositive inner product with Y-y for every Y in K. Expanding squared
distances proves this is exactly the projection condition. Conversely,
on the relative interior of this side the tangent residual is zero
and the normal residual is nonnegative; taking the closure gives(2.4).
A larger vertex normal cone is not included by this convention.

Put p=r-u and b=w+v on a legal side sample. Then

    0<=p<=R_tri, b>=0,
    y=p*e_sigma,
    z=alpha+p*e_sigma+b*n_sigma,
    g=alpha+b*n_sigma=u*e_sigma+w*n_sigma,
    2phi(z)=2u*p+||g||^2=2u*r-u^2+w^2.              (2.5)

In global coordinates z=(s,t), the sign is forced, because

    sigma*t=v*r+u*w=v*p+u*b>=0.                     (2.6)

Equality is possible only when p=b=0, giving z=alpha=(1,0).
The two closed side cells intersect only there. Both labels give the
same y=0 and g=alpha. A queried tie cannot have its next sample in
the union of the side cells: every actual h_i>1 sends it to(1-h_i,0),
whereas the only axis point in the union is(1,0). A terminal tie is
allowed. The formulas below remain label-independent at that tie.

In the remainder every original query AND the original terminal must
satisfy(2.4) with its actual sign. The same finite cap R_tri is used
throughout. No base, vertex-cone, or interior history is introduced.

## 3. Complete original interpolation rows and the update

For original samples i,j, including the original terminal, use the
project's original orientation

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i.(z_j-z_i)+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    f_i=phi(z_i).

Substitute z_i=y_i+g_i and f_i=alpha.y_i+||g_i||^2/2.
All pure gradient norm terms cancel, leaving

    Q_ij=(g_i-alpha).(y_j-y_i).                     (3.1)

The products n_sigma.e_sigma=0 and
n_sigma.e_(-sigma)=-2uv now give the COMPLETE row table

    Q_ij=0                           if sigma_i=sigma_j,
    Q_ij=-2uv*b_i*p_j                if sigma_i!=sigma_j.
                                                               (3.2)

Every factor is nonnegative. No chronology assumption was used, so this
includes both orientations of all remote rows and every terminal row.
If a sample is the tie, p=b=0, and both directions involving it are0,
independently of which side label was assigned there.

For the genuine optimum sample star, z_star=y_star=g_star=0 and
f_star=0. Directly in(3.1), or in the definition of Q, one obtains

    Q_(i,star)=0, Q_(star,i)=-u*p_i, Q_(star,star)=0. (3.3)

Do not interchange b_i*p_j with p_i*b_j in(3.2). Those latter
factors will occur in the different gradient-body normal orientation.

The actual update z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i*g_i is equivalently

    p_(i+1)e_(sigma_(i+1))+b_(i+1)n_(sigma_(i+1))
       =p_i e_(sigma_i)-(h_i-1)b_i n_(sigma_i)-h_i alpha.
                                                               (3.4)

This follows by subtracting alpha after substituting(2.5).
It does not supply an independently choosable next label: the new point
must have the actual sign in(2.6) and satisfy the same cap guards.

For reference, the exact folded equality is also available without
assuming its correction has a sign. Put r=u*s+v*|t| and
w=-v*s+u*|t|. For a physical step h_i=1+d define

    ell_i=max(0,-[v*(r_i-h_i*u)-u*d*w_i]).

Taking the absolute value of the actual new vertical coordinate gives

    r_(i+1)=r_i-h_i*u+2v*ell_i,
    w_(i+1)=-d*w_i+2u*ell_i.                        (3.5)

For a complete H_j, let B_i be the signed product of1-h over the
steps strictly after i within that actual child, and set
Lfold=sum_i ell_i, Zfold=sum_i B_i*ell_i. Summing the first equation
and expanding the second by induction gives

    r_end=r_start-S_j*u+2v*Lfold,
    w_end=-pi_j*w_start+2u*Zfold.

Substitution in(2.5) therefore gives the equality

    ||z_start||^2-D_j*2phi(z_end)
      =(r_start-D_j*u)^2+(1-D_j*pi_j^2)*w_start^2-D_j*Cfold,
    Cfold=4uv*Lfold-4u*pi_j*w_start*Zfold+4u^2*Zfold^2.       (3.6)

No nonpositivity or O(1/m) claim about Cfold is used. With no switches,
Lfold=Zfold=0 and(3.6) is the exact same-side identity. The two new
tests below retain the complete original radius rather than deleting
the last term by a presumed sign.

## 4. Mechanism1: one explicit all-row barycentric pairing

Use k_i=h_i for0<=i<n and k_n=1. Thus sum_i k_i=A.
For ALL0<=i,j<=n define

    lambda_ij=2D*k_i*k_j/A^2,
    P_bp=sum_(i,j)lambda_ij*(-Q_ij),
    E_bp=||z_0||^2-D*2f_n.                          (4.1)

The diagonal terms vanish. The optimum multipliers initially are zero.
These are literal formulas in the actual word; there is no unknown
matrix and no sum over admissible label lists. All opposite-face rows
receive positive weight and same-face rows receive the same weights
but have value zero.

This pairing is the nonnegative covariance payment associated with the
actual step-mass mixture. Specifically set

    gbar=A^(-1)*sum_i k_i*g_i,
    ybar=A^(-1)*sum_i k_i*y_i.

Since y_i.g_i=alpha.y_i, equation(3.1) also reads
-Q_ij=y_j.(g_j-g_i). Hence

    P_bp=2D*(alpha-gbar).ybar>=0,
    A*gbar=z_0-y_n.                                 (4.2)

The last identity telescopes the chronological updates and includes
the terminal weight1. In particular the exact remainder is

    R_bp=E_bp-P_bp
      =||z_0||^2-D*(2alpha.y_n+||g_n||^2)
         -2D*[alpha-(z_0-y_n)/A].ybar.              (4.3)

The tested proposal is R_bp>=0 on every legal complete H_m. Were it
true, (4.1)-(4.3) would pay the full endpoint slack by this explicit
nonnegative collection of bipartite normals and a nonnegative remainder.
The following exact full-history obstruction refutes that proposal.

## 5. One legal whole-word trajectory and the pairing obstruction

Keep ANY m>=1536 and its original entire H_m. Set

    u=sqrt(3)/2, v=1/2, R_tri=2(S+2),
    L=u*R_tri, H=v*R_tri, h=1+a,
    r_0=u*(h+A/2), w_0=3A/(4a),
    z_0=r_0*e_++w_0*n_+.                            (5.1)

This is ONE finite static triangle and one finite original start.
The initial upper-side projection is strict:

    p_0=r_0-u=u*(a+A/2) in(0,R_tri), b_0=w_0+1/2>0.

In this geometry kappa=u^2-v^2=1/2 and omega=2uv=u. The first
actual leaf sends the upper-frame coordinates to

    r_hat=r_0-h*u=u*A/2, w_hat=-a*w_0=-3A/4.

The change from upper to lower coordinates is
(r,w)=(kappa*r_hat-omega*w_hat,-omega*r_hat-kappa*w_hat).
It gives exactly

    r_1=u*A, w_1=0, z_1=A*q, q=u*e_-.
                                                               (5.2)

Its height is negative, so the new label is actual, not prescribed.
Every remaining original step keeps the lower normal coordinate zero.
At a sample j>=1 let t_j=sum_(1<=i<j)h_i, with t_1=0. Then

    r_j=u*(A-t_j), w_j=0, g_j=q,
    p_j=u*(S-t_j), b_j=v=1/2.                      (5.3)

The cumulative mass increases from0 to S-h, so

    r_n=u*(1+h)>u,
    u<r_j<=u*A<u+R_tri,
    w_j=0>-v                                       (5.4)

for EVERY remaining query and the original terminal. Thus every cap
and normal guard is strict. These inequalities prove the complete
trajectory without enumerating or replaying its steps. All its ordered
rows, in both directions, are the actual rows of(3.2)-(3.3).

The exact original radius and full terminal energy are

    N=||z_0||^2=(3/4)*(h+A/2)^2+9A^2/(16a^2),
    2f_n=(3/4)*(1+2h)=(3/4)*(3+2a).                (5.5)

Only rows joining sample0 to a later sample contribute to P_bp. Thus

    P_bp=(2D/A^2)*omega*h*
          [b_0*sum_(j=1)^n k_j*p_j
                 +p_0*v*sum_(j=1)^n k_j].          (5.6)

For an exact chronological summation put M=S-h and
V=sum_(i=1)^(n-1)h_i^2. The identity
sum_(i=1)^(n-1)h_i*t_i=(M^2-V)/2 follows by expanding M^2:
each product of two distinct chronological steps appears twice.
Using p_n=u*h, including the terminal weight1, gives

    sum_(j=1)^n k_j*p_j
      =u*[S*M-(M^2-V)/2+h]
      =u*[(S^2-h^2+V)/2+h]
      >=u*(S^2-h^2)/2.                             (5.7)

Discarding only positive terms in(5.6), using b_0>w_0 and
omega=u, yields

    P_bp>[9D*h/(16a*A)]*(S^2-h^2).                 (5.8)

Let T_bp=9A^2/(16a^2)>0. From(2.3), A>=100 and h<3/2:

    P_bp/T_bp
      >a*h*(D/A)*(S^2-h^2)/A^2
      >(14/25)*(199/100)*(97/100)
      =270242/250000>27/25.                        (5.9)

Here D/A=2-1/A>=199/100. Also
(S^2-h^2)/A^2=1-2/A+(1-h^2)/A^2
>1-2/100-5/40000>97/100. Every factor multiplied is positive.
For comparison,

    N/T_bp=1+(4a^2/3)*(1/2+h/A)^2
      <1+(25/108)*(21/40)^2
      =1+49/768<16/15.                             (5.10)

The last comparison is49*15<768. Combining(5.9)-(5.10) proves

    P_bp-N>T_bp/75=9A^2/(1200a^2)>0,
    E_bp-P_bp<-T_bp/75.                            (5.11)

This decisively refutes the specific pairing in(4.1). The contradiction
already occurs before paying the positive terminal energy in E_bp.
All original residual costs -Q_ij are nonnegative. Consequently any
coordinatewise increase of these multipliers only increases the
payment, as does adding nonnegative weights on -Q_(star,i)=u*p_i.
Weights on -Q_(i,star) add zero. None of these monotone extensions repairs
the failed nonnegative remainder. Different row weights are not excluded.

The construction in(5.1) is a whole-word stress test of this new pairing,
not a reopening of the already analyzed single-switch objective bound.
Its actual payoff is small; Section10 records that distinction exactly.

## 6. Required first-leaf and impossible-root tests

### 6.1 The prior strict complete leaf is not discarded

On the SAME enclosing-m parameters, take the finite triangle
u=sqrt(3)/2,v=1/2,R_tri=3 and upper start

    r_0=u*(13/8+a), w_0=15/(16a).

Its first leaf has upper-frame output(5u/8,-15/16). Changing to
the lower frame gives r_1=5u/4,w_1=0. Both projections satisfy
strict cap and normal guards: initial p_0=u*(5/8+a) in(0,3),
terminal p_1=u/4 in(0,3), and both b values are positive.
Its actual energy is2f_1=9/8. The continued-side expression is
273/256, while9/8=288/256, so Cfold=15/256. This rechecks the
known positive folded correction; no nonpositive-correction statement
is revived.

The NEW barycentric pairing, localized to this complete H_1, passes
this particular test. Its original slack is

    E_leaf=(3/4)*(13/8+a)^2+225/(256a^2)
                       -(3+2a)*(9/8)>15/4.         (6.1)

Indeed13/8+a>2, 225/(256a^2)>81/16, and
(3+2a)*(9/8)<69/16. The total of the two cross normal costs is

    -Q_01-Q_10=(3/4)*[15/(64a)+7/16+a/2]<15/16.

The weight is2D_1*h/(1+h)^2<25/12, using h<3/2,
D_1<4, and1+h>12/5. Hence P_leaf<125/64<2.
Thus E_leaf-P_leaf>7/4. Passing this first leaf does not fix the
whole-word obstruction(5.11).

### 6.2 The prescribed original-root two-fixed-side-child pattern is empty

Here is a joint rederivation, retaining the exact left attenuation.
Every nonempty prefix product of the factors h_i-1 in H_j has
magnitude at most a. The base H_1 has product a. For H_(j+1), a
prefix inside the left child has the inductive bound; one ending at the
bridge has product pi_j*d_j=beta^(2^j)/c<=a; one continuing in the
right child multiplies this by a right-prefix product at most1.

It follows that an entire nonempty child H_j and its terminal stay
on one fixed side exactly when

    u*(S_j+1)<=r_start<=u+R_tri,
    -v<=w_start<=v/a.                              (6.2)

For r this uses monotone decrease by the actual cumulative mass.
For w the start and first leaf force the two bounds, and the prefix
product estimate makes those bounds sufficient at every later sample.

Set k=m-1, Aleft=S_k, pleft=pi_k, d=d_k,
tau_rt=d*pleft=beta^(2^k)/c, kappa=u^2-v^2, omega=2uv.
Equations(2.2)-(2.3) imply

    Aleft+1>2d, d>1, 0<tau_rt<1/2.                 (6.3)

If the left child including the root query remains upper, then
r_0>=u*(Aleft+1), w_0>=-v. The root output in the upper frame is

    X=r_0-(Aleft+1+d)*u>=-d*u,
    Y=tau_rt*w_0>=-tau_rt*v.

If it enters the lower cell, its physical height satisfies
vX+uY<=0. Its lower tangent is r=kappa*X-omega*Y.
If kappa>=0, then

    r<=-(kappa*u/v+omega)*Y=-(u/v)*Y<=u*tau_rt.

If kappa<0, then

    r<=u*(-kappa*d+2v^2*tau_rt)
      <=u*(d+2tau_rt)<2d*u.

Both bounds contradict r>=u*(Aleft+1), which is necessary by(6.2)
for the complete right child and original terminal to stay lower.
Thus the prescribed root pattern is impossible at every positive angle
and finite cap. No cap inequality was relaxed to create an example.
This excludes only those two fixed-side children. The trajectory in
Section5 instead switches at the original first leaf. Its root query
and complete right child are lower, while its left child is not
fixed-side; it meets this test.

## 7. Mechanism2: one fixed body from all original gradients

For any allowed actual two-side history, define ONCE, using all its
already-fixed original samples including the terminal,

    C_grad=conv{g_0,...,g_n},
    D_grad=conv({0} union C_grad),
    F_grad(x)=max_(g in D_grad)[x.g-||g||^2/2].       (7.1)

The body is then fixed for the entire run. The maximizing point is the
Euclidean projection P_(D_grad)(x), by completing the square. Projection
is1-Lipschitz: apply its two normal inequalities at x and x' and add
to obtain ||P(x)-P(x')||^2<=(x-x').(P(x)-P(x')).
The maximum-value sandwich using the two maximizing points shows
F_grad is differentiable with gradient P_(D_grad); the error after its
linear term is bounded by ||x-x'||^2. It is convex as a maximum of
affine functions of x and is everywhere finite since D_grad is compact.

At an original sample, z_i-g_i=y_i. The COMPLETE body normal table is

    y_i.(g_j-g_i)=0                    on equal faces,
    y_i.(g_j-g_i)=-2uv*p_i*b_j         on opposite faces,
    y_i.(0-g_i)=-u*p_i.                            (7.2)

This is directly obtained from(2.5), n_sigma.e_(-sigma)=-2uv,
and y_i.g_i=alpha.y_i=u*p_i. All these numbers are nonpositive;
therefore g_i=P_(D_grad)(z_i) by the projection normal test against
every generator and then its convex hull. The tie p_i=b_i=0 gives
the same conclusion without a label ambiguity. The sampled values are

    F_grad(z_i)=z_i.g_i-||g_i||^2/2
              =u*p_i+||g_i||^2/2=f_i.              (7.3)

Thus the SAME actual original trajectory and all its sampled values are
realized by ONE static projection-gradient function. This is a scoped
identity for these side samples; it does not identify the original
affine-height class with all projection bodies.

For each occupied face sigma, define b_sigma,min and b_sigma,max as
the minimum and maximum of the finitely many b_i on that face. Then
its sampled-gradient hull is exactly the segment between

    alpha+b_sigma,min*n_sigma,
    alpha+b_sigma,max*n_sigma.

Replacing that face's samples by these two endpoints leaves both C_grad
and D_grad unchanged. There are at most four such endpoint generators,
and at most five when0 is included. Empty faces contribute nothing;
coincident endpoints or the tie may be retained without changing the
body. Removing the nearer endpoint in general is not justified.

The COMPLETE minimum set is the polar cone

    M_grad={x: x.g<=0 for every g in D_grad}
          ={x: x.e<=0 for every listed nonzero endpoint e}.     (7.4)

To prove this,0 is feasible in(7.1), so F_grad>=0. Every point in
the displayed cone has all competing values nonpositive, hence value0.
If x.g>0 for some g in D_grad, the segment[0,g] is in the body,
and a sufficiently small positive multiple t*g gives
t*x.g-t^2||g||^2/2>0. Thus every point outside the cone has positive
value. This proves(7.4), not just a subset of valid minima.

The original selected minimizer0 remains valid and gives the unchanged
radius ||z_0||. In general dist(z_0,M_grad)<=||z_0||, so changing to
the closest minimum INCREASES the normalized payoff. A bound using the
original radius does not automatically give the closer-radius bound.
Section9 shows that, for the explicit obstruction,0 is also the closest
minimum of this new function, so even that potential ambiguity vanishes.

## 8. The single complete-child normal-energy law tested

Keep C_grad and D_grad fixed as in(7.1). Define

    mu_grad=min_(g in C_grad)||g||^2,
    V_grad(g)=||g||^2-mu_grad, g in C_grad.          (8.1)

The minimum exists; it may be zero in a general history. We do not
silently substitute min_(D_grad)||g||^2=0 for this quantity. The proposed
law is, for each complete literal H_j in the same history,

    V_grad(g_end)<=pi_j^2*V_grad(g_start).          (G)

Its all-depth factor is explicitly quantified by

    q_0=1, q_(j+1)=d_j^2*q_j^2,
    q_j=pi_j^2=beta^(2*(2^j-1))*c^(-2j).          (8.2)

These are symbolic coefficient formulas, not an evaluated recurrence.
The body and reference mu_grad are not rebuilt in either child.

Why test this particular law? On one isolated fixed-side complete child,
all gradients lie on alpha+b*n_sigma=u*e_sigma+w*n_sigma. Its
initial normal w_start and its first-leaf normal -a*w_start bracket0.
Thus its sampled-gradient segment contains u*e_sigma, the closest
point on that line to0, and mu_grad=u^2. The exact normal evolution
w_end=-pi_j*w_start gives equality in(G). This suggests a concrete
two-ray extension using the fixed hull's least squared gradient norm.
Unlike the old folded-objective majorant, (G) concerns a fixed body's
normal energy and does not assert a sign for the affine-height correction.

The following full original trajectory proves that this extension fails.
In particular, merely replacing a local ray's reference level u^2 by
the least norm of the whole sampled-gradient hull is not a valid gluing.

## 9. Exact whole-child failure of(G), with the full minimum cone

Use the SAME triangle and full history from Section5, and abbreviate
w=w_0=3A/(4a). Then

    g_0=(3/4-w/2,u/2+u*w),
    q=(3/4,-u/2),
    g_i=q for every1<=i<=n,
    C_grad=[q,g_0], D_grad=conv{0,q,g_0}.            (9.1)

This is a genuine triangle, since det(g_0,q)=-(u/2)*(w+3/2)
is nonzero. In particular the closest point of C_grad to0 can be
computed without any optimization program. Let

    Vdiff=||g_0-q||^2=w^2+(3/2)w+3/4,
    t_c=-q.(g_0-q)/Vdiff=[(3/4)w+3/8]/Vdiff.

One has0<t_c<1: the numerator is positive, and Vdiff minus that
numerator is w^2+(3/4)w+3/8>0. Minimizing the one-variable quadratic
||q+t(g_0-q)||^2 on[0,1] therefore gives

    Delta=||q||^2-mu_grad
       =(9/16)*(w+1/2)^2/[w^2+(3/2)w+3/4],
    mu_grad=(3/16)*(w+3/2)^2/[w^2+(3/2)w+3/4]>0,
    V_grad(q)=Delta,
    V_grad(g_0)=w^2+Delta.                         (9.2)

Here w>1 by A>=100 and a<5/12. Moreover

    Delta-3/8
       =3*(4w^2-3)/(64*Vdiff)>0,
    0<Delta<3/4.                                  (9.3)

The upper bound follows from mu_grad>0 and ||q||^2=3/4.

On the ORIGINAL whole H_m, use A<c^m from(2.2). Equations(2.3)
and(9.2)-(9.3) imply

    pi_m^2*V_grad(g_0)
      =beta^(2n)*c^(-2m)*(w^2+Delta)
      <[9/(16a^2)+3/4]*beta^(2n)
      <(273/64)*beta^(2n).                        (9.4)

For a fully explicit uniform bound, beta^(-2n)>1+2n*eps by the
binomial inequality, since beta^(-1)>1+eps. For every integer m>=8,
2^m-1>=30m: at8 this is255>=240, and the induction follows from
2^(m+1)-1=2(2^m-1)+1>=60m+1>=30(m+1).
Thus2n*eps=n/(2m)>=15, and beta^(2n)<1/16. Consequently

    pi_m^2*V_grad(g_0)<273/1024,
    V_grad(g_n)-pi_m^2*V_grad(g_0)>111/1024>0.       (9.5)

This is a quantified legal WHOLE-H_m failure of(G), uniformly in
every permitted enclosing m. It uses both complete original children,
every physical query, and the true original terminal. It does not rely
on an isolated bridge or an impossible label pattern.

The original first leaf of this example satisfies(G): its output is q,
while a^2*w^2=9A^2/16>3/4>Delta. The strict leaf from Section6.1
also satisfies it. Its gradients are the same forms with
w=15/(16a), and a^2*w^2=225/256>3/4>Delta. Thus the old
first-leaf stress test is retained and passes the new normal-energy law.

There is also a direct explanation of the all-depth failure. In the
whole-history fixed body, every later gradient is q, but Delta>0.
Any subsequent complete leaf would require Delta<=a^2*Delta, which
is false because a<1. Resetting the reference to the later occupied
face would make that local surplus zero, but it would change the fixed
reference and leave a reference-change term to be paid. No estimate of
that missing payment is proved here. The full-word quantitative bound
(9.5) makes the failure explicit even without using this shorter test.

Finally the complete polar minimum cone of this ONE body is exactly

    M_grad={x: x.q<=0, x.g_0<=0}.                  (9.6)

By the actual first update and(5.2),

    z_0=A*q+h*g_0.                                 (9.7)

Both coefficients are positive. For every x in M_grad,
z_0.x=A*q.x+h*g_0.x<=0, whence
||z_0-x||^2>=||z_0||^2+||x||^2>=||z_0||^2.
Equality is attained at x=0 and only there. Thus0 is the closest
minimum of F_grad to this original start, and its true squared distance
is exactly the same N as in(5.5). The failures above cannot be explained
away by replacing the minimum set or using a different radius.

## 10. Actual payoff and finite orthogonal tilt

The obstructions reject the two specified proof mechanisms, not the
objective bound. For Section5's actual whole-word history,

    2f_n<23/8,
    N>T_bp>(81/25)*A^2,
    D*2f_n<(23/4)*A<N.                             (10.1)

The last inequality follows already from A>=100. Hence the actual
planar payoff is strictly below1/D. In particular neither(5.11)
nor(9.5) is a full-function lower witness above the benchmark threshold.

For completeness retain a concrete finite orthogonal lift. Let T>0 be
finite, embed K as Ktilde={(y_1,y_2,0):y in K}, and take

    lambda=(1,0,T),
    Phi(z,q_perp)=phi(z)+q_perp^2/2,
    X_0=(s_0,t_0,-s_0/T), z_0=(s_0,t_0).

This is exactly the affine-height envelope of Ktilde with height lambda,
not just a limiting construction. Its unique minimum is0 and
lambda.X_0=0. Each original step multiplies q_perp by1-h_i, so at
the full original terminal, with Q_perp=pi_m^2,

    ||X_0||^2=N+s_0^2/T^2,
    2Phi(X_n)=2f_n+Q_perp*s_0^2/T^2,
    E_T=[2f_n+Q_perp*s_0^2/T^2]/[N+s_0^2/T^2].     (10.2)

These are the true initial distance and actual terminal. Equation(2.2)
gives Q_perp<1/D. Combining it with(10.1), (10.2) is a weighted
average of two quantities strictly below1/D. Thus every finite T>0,
including the explicit T=1, also has E_T<1/D. No infinite-tilt concrete
witness is asserted. The two-ray gradient-body representation in
Section7 is a planar statement; no claim is made that this lifted
three-dimensional gradient set still consists of two affine rays.

## 11. Remaining interface and provenance

The complete row structure gives substantial exact information, but
neither tested way of using it closes the bound. A successful pairing
must change the weights enough to avoid the overpayment(5.11); simply
retaining every nonnegative normal with larger coefficients makes the
identified problem worse. A useful fixed-body complete-child energy
must account for the nonzero constant-gradient surplus in(9.2), or
must pay changes of the active reference energy. No such payment is
established here.

The task's full-history radius inequality remains the open quantitative
interface. In the folded notation it is

    D*Cfold <= (r_0-D*u)^2+(1-D*pi_m^2)*w_0^2,

or a uniformly quantified permitted relaxation of that inequality.
This is an open interface, not a newly proved assertion. Even a proof
on all the side histories would leave the other triangle cells and the
full affine-height function class untreated. Accepted931 cannot be
applied as a minimax conclusion from the present scoped results.

Read TASK.md, original PROBLEM.md, own continuation notes/events,
latest master_guidance8b730bb4f0c90f3c, relevant shared findings and
verdicts, corrected prior report3211401a8cc8819d, and the applicable
accepted facts. Fact searches did not supply the missing two-side
pairing or complete-child energy law. Initial broad recall outputs were
truncated; the exact needed statements and report sections were then
read separately. No omitted/truncated passage is a mathematical premise.

Skills applied: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving,
construct-counterexamples, within the explicit two-mechanism limit.
Program stage: mature_subproblem. Search mode: repair. Active program:
this same finite affine-height triangle with complete original
bipartite normals. Missing mechanism: payment using the original radius
through complete children. Matlas returned constant-step below2/L,
projected-gradient, and below1/L self-contraction statements, which
do not apply to the original long-step word. Web search and the exact
local text downloads/release_middle_sector/2309.16530.txt were used to
read the recursive-gluing background in Altschuler--Parrilo,
Acceleration by Stepsize Hedging II, arXiv2309.16530. Its different

No external mathematical premise is used in the new proofs.

The complete candidate report, both exact scoped failures and the
handoff are persisted as unverified shared findings. No fact_submit,
