# Near-critical ruler: complete quadratic screen and a quantitative cap obstruction

Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No solver, numerical sweep, or
fact_submit was used. All new estimates are proved here; prior unverified
findings are not premises. Accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d` supplies the
definitions and validity of K_n and the exact benchmark recursions; accepted
fact `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies smooth-convex interpolation. This does not
upper-bound the unrestricted W_n and does not settle the project target.

## 1. Exact schedule and all quadratic curvatures, including arbitrary b

Fix an integer m>=2. Within this document write

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),
    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.

The parameters c,a remain fixed for this entire horizon. They are not
recomputed when j changes. The symbol H_j here names a schedule, not the
conjugate jet quadratic from the project glossary. Put r=c-2 and A_mass=a/r.
Counting the occurrences of each level gives, for 0<=j<=m,

    S_j=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2),
    M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j.                       (1)

There are 2^(m-j-1) occurrences of d_j in H_m. Its scalar multiplier at
curvature t in [0,1] is exactly

    P_m(t)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j t)^(2^(m-j-1)).     (2)

Let p_m be H_m without its final d_0 and let h^(m,b)=(p_m,b), b>=0. Then

    sum h^(m,b)=S_m-d_0+b,
    R_(m,b)(t)=(1-bt) product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j t)^N_j,
    N_0=2^(m-1)-1, N_j=2^(m-j-1) for 1<=j<m.           (3)

The exact worst normalized quadratic objective over ALL finite dimensions
is

    Q_m(b)=max_(0<=t<=1) t R_(m,b)(t)^2.                (4)

Indeed diagonalizing a positive semidefinite Hessian bounded by the identity
makes the normalized error a weighted average of the displayed scalar
quantities. A scalar quadratic attains the maximum. Zero eigenvalues have
zero objective contribution; affine translations to a minimizer change
neither calculation. Thus this is a full quadratic-class computation.

For an exact finite characterization of (4), subdivide [0,1] at all its
distinct interior roots 1/d_j and, when b>0, 1/b. On each root-free open
interval the logarithmic derivative of the nonzero objective is

    L(t)=1/t-2b/(1-bt)-2sum_j N_j d_j/(1-d_j t),
    L'(t)=-1/t^2-2b^2/(1-bt)^2
                    -2sum_j N_j d_j^2/(1-d_j t)^2<0.   (5)

Consequently there is exactly one interior maximizer between consecutive
roots, also between zero and the first root. On the last interval the
maximum is either its unique root of L or t=1. Values at roots are zero.
Coincident roots are combined, and b=0 simply removes its term. Equations
(3)--(5) specify the complete spectrum without a numerical sweep.

The unit-curvature values alone are

    P_m(1)=-(ac)^(2^m-1)c^(-m),
    R_(m,b)(1)=(1-b)(ac)^(2^m-2)c^(1-m).                (6)

They are not used as a replacement for (4).

## 2. Exact single-cut screen for every appended b

For a cut with step d, preceding mass P and following mass T, the accepted
cut value is (d-1)_+^2/[(1+P)^2(1+2T)]. For a fixed d and total schedule
mass S, its reciprocal denominator is

    D(P)=(1+P)^2[1+2(S-d-P)].

Its derivative has the sign of 2(S-d)-3P. Therefore its minimum on any
closed interval is attained at an endpoint. Among all occurrences of a
given level it suffices to check the first and last occurrence.

For h^(m,b), all levels 1<=j<m remain present. Their first and last
preceding masses are, respectively,

    P_j^first=S_j, P_j^last=S_m-d_j-S_j.                (7)

Their following masses are S_m-d_0+b-d_j-P. At level zero, after deleting
the original last occurrence, the endpoint preceding masses are

    P_0^first=0, P_0^last=S_m-2d_0-d_1,                 (8)

with following masses S_m-2d_0+b and d_1+b. For m=2 the two occurrences
in (8) refer to the same remaining index. The appended b contributes

    (b-1)_+^2/(1+S_m-d_0)^2.                           (9)

Taking the maximum of the cuts in (7)--(9) and
1/[1+2(S_m-d_0+b)] is EXACTLY K_n(h^(m,b)). No omitted internal cut can
be larger, by the denominator argument. In particular the accepted final
cut already proves W_n(h^(m,b))>=1/B_n whenever

    b>=1+(1+S_m-d_0)/sqrt(B_n).                        (10)

The global Huber term proves the same comparison whenever
b<=(B_n-1)/2-(S_m-d_0), if that right side is nonnegative. These tests use
the actual B_n, not a guessed dyadic constant.

## 3. An exact all-cut result at the original final step

For the remainder take m>=128 unless indicated, and first set b=d_0, so
the actual schedule is H_m. The following rational bounds will be used:

    12/5<lambda<c<49/20<5/2,
    3/8<a<lambda-2<1/2,
    ac>=127/128>7/8,
    0<a<c-2=r<=9/20.                                   (11)

For example lambda<17/7 gives
c<2193/896<49/20 for m>=128; a=(1-1/m)/c then exceeds3/8.
The upper bound a<lambda-2 follows from a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda].
Consequently 0<A_mass<1, and (1) gives

    M_j<=c^j, M_(j+1)<=c M_j,
    a c^j<=r M_j.                                      (12)

Let M=M_(m-1). For a first occurrence of any level j, P=S_j and its
following mass is at least S_(m-1). Its cut is at most
r^2/(2M-1). For a last occurrence, its preceding mass is at least
S_(m-1), and its following mass is S_j. Since x^2/(2x-1) is increasing
for x>=1 and M_j<=M, its cut is also at most

    r^2 M_j^2/[M^2(2M_j-1)]<=r^2/(2M-1).              (13)

These statements include the unique central occurrence, for which the
first and last positions coincide. Using c<=5/2, r<=1/2 and M>=1,

    r^2(2M_m-1)<= (5M-1)/4 <= 2M-1.

Thus every single-cut term is no larger than the global Huber term:

    K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).                               (14)

## 4. Full-spectrum quadratic bound at b=d_0

This argument controls every t in [0,1], not only t=1. Write
v_j=a c^j t and, for the partial ruler H_j,

    Y_j=c v_j |P_j(t)|.

If no level 0,...,m-1 satisfies d_j t>=1, all factors in (2) are
nonnegative, and 1-u<=exp(-u) gives

    t P_m(t)^2<=t exp(-2S_m t)<=1/(2e S_m).             (15)

The last maximum is over all nonnegative t, so it also bounds the actual
restricted interval. Suppose instead that j is the first level with
d_j t>=1. If j=0, Y_0=ac t<=1. If j>=1, all factors in P_j are
nonnegative and t<=1/(1+a)<=8/11. Equations (1), (11) give

    t S_j>=v_j/r-t,
    Y_j<=c v_j exp(-v_j/r+t)<=cr exp(t-1)<1.            (16)

Here the maximum of v exp(-v/r) is r/e, cr<5/4, and
exp(3/11)>1+3/11=14/11>5/4. For every subsequent level l>=j the
factor is on its nonpositive side, and the exact recursion is

    Y_(l+1)=[1-(1-t)/v_l]Y_l^2<=Y_l^2<=1.             (17)

The bracket lies in [0,1]. If an earlier factor is zero the conclusion
holds directly as well. Therefore

    t P_m(t)^2<=1/[c^2 a^2 c^(2m)t]
                      <=d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m)),      (18)

using t>=1/d_(m-1). Equations (15),(18) prove the explicit full-spectrum
estimate

    Q_m(d_0)<=max{1/(2e S_m),
                 (1+a c^(m-1))/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))}.      (19)

Both entries are strictly below (14). For the first, S_m>=1 gives
(2S_m+1)/(2e S_m)<=3/(2e)<3/4. For the second, using (12) and ac>7/8,

    (2S_m+1)(1+a c^(m-1))/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))
       <=2/[(ac)^2 c^m]+2/[c^2 ac]
       <=16/49+25/63=2233/3087<3/4.                   (20)

For the first term of this last bound it already suffices that m>=3
and c>2. Thus, with considerable slack,

    Q_m(d_0)<(3/4)K_n(H_m).                            (21)

## 5. Exact benchmark comparison, with no fitted constant

Let rho=log_2(lambda). The accepted benchmark defines the primitive mass
C_n=S_circle(n)+1 with join

    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,

and B_n with join G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. We prove directly
from these recursions that

    C_n<=(n+1)^rho, B_n<=1+4n^rho.                     (22)

Put F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1, t>0. Direct differentiation, with t=exp(z), gives

    d^2/dz^2 log F(exp z)
      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.

The strict inequality follows by integrating the positive derivative of
(1+t)^rho-1-rho t for t>0. Convexity at z and -z gives
F(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. If A=t^rho B and
D=(1+t)^rho B, this says (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, or
D^2-(A+B)D-AB>=0. Hence

    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.

Zero arguments follow by continuity. Induction in the primitive
recursion proves the first bound in (22). Since G(A,D)<=4A+D,
induction in the dominant recursion, with a split k and l=n-k-1,
gives B_n<=1+4[(k+1)^rho+l^rho]<=1+4n^rho. The last inequality follows
by integrating the increasing derivative of x^rho, including l=0.

In our family (1) implies

    M_m/lambda^m
       =(1+1/m)^m[A_mass+(1-A_mass)(2/c)^m] -> e,        (23)

since A_mass->1 and 2/c is bounded below1. Thus the EXACT finite bound

    K_n(H_m) B_n
      <=[1+4(2^m-1)^rho]/(2M_m-1)                     (24)

has limit 2/e<1. This uses an upper bound derived for the actual B_n
at every integer horizon; it assumes no asymptotic phase or decimal
value for B_n/lambda^m. In particular, the full quadratic and all-cut
baseline is strictly below U_n^ZJ for every sufficiently large m.

## 6. Retained two-block geometry and all constant tails in its cap range

Here is the precise mechanism tested next. For ANY two selected cuts i<j
of an arbitrary schedule, let P be the mass before i, S the mass strictly
between i and j, T the mass after j, and define

    M_0=1+P, M_1=1+S,
    A=(h_i-1)/M_0, epsilon=(h_j-1)/M_1, sigma=2T+1.

Assume h_i,h_j>1 and choose the cap-range angle

    0<kappa<=A/(1+epsilon).                            (25)

Use the retained prefix gradients and proximal point

    p=(1,0)/M_0,
    q=(-kappa,sqrt(kappa(A-kappa)))/M_1,
    X=(-A,0), y=X-M_1 q, d=-M_1 q.                    (26)

These prefix data are actual jets of the single Moreau envelope of
J(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>), starting at (1,0). Before the first checkpoint
the proximal points are (1-(u+1)/M_0,0), 0<=u<=P, so the p-plane is
active. That checkpoint, of size1+A M_0, sends its input p to X. In
the second block, the proposed proximal points X-tq, 1<=t<=M_1, have

    <q,X-tq>=(A kappa/M_1)(1-t/M_1)>=0,
    <q-p,X-tq>
      =A kappa/M_1+A/M_0
          -t[A kappa/M_1^2+kappa/(M_0 M_1)]
      >=(A-kappa)/M_0>0.

This verifies every actual old query, even if individual ordinary steps
are large. At the second checkpoint its jet is

    x_j=y+q, g_j=q, f_j=||q||^2/2.

For completeness, the envelope minimizes J(z)+||x-z||^2/2. This has a
unique minimizer, and an active-plane slope g at z proves optimality at
x=z+g. At two proximal points, monotonicity of the subgradients gives
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, hence a 1-Lipschitz gradient. The two minimizing
inequalities identify the derivative as g; joint convexity proves
convexity of the envelope. Thus the claimed function has the required
global regularity, not only valid formal data.

The second checkpoint, of size1+M_1 epsilon, sends the iterate to
X_tail=y+epsilon d. The optimum jet is (0,0,0). Every interpolant of
this prefix has origin as an actual closest minimizer to (1,0): the
retained first checkpoint jet (p,p,||p||^2/2), interpolated with any
minimizer z, forces <p,z><=0. Its squared distance from (1,0) is
therefore at least1, attained at origin. This normalization persists
for all extensions below.

Allow ANY legal continuation with a single common gradient g at every
tail query, including the terminal sample; allow affine intercepts,
positive values and extra dimensions, and retain every prefix jet.
Its endpoint is y+epsilon d-Tg. The accepted interpolation inequality
from this endpoint to the old jet (y+q,q,||q||^2/2) gives

    2f_end<=2epsilon<d,g>-sigma||g||^2
                         <=epsilon^2||d||^2/sigma
                         =A kappa epsilon^2/sigma.

This uses a retained old query, not a reset to a new problem. From (25),
EVERY such constant-tail continuation obeys the exact universal cap

    2f_end<=U_ij:=A^2 epsilon^2/[(1+epsilon)(1+2T)].     (27)

In particular it covers all angles and all optimized tail planes of the
previous explicit two-checkpoint cap policy. It does not cover angles
kappa>A/(1+epsilon), different two-block prefixes, or variable-gradient
tails. No claim that (27) is attained for every pair is needed below.

## 7. The complete cap-range mechanism is dominated by K on H_m

Return to the full H_m, m>=128. For a selected pair i<j let their levels
be u,v; write d=a c^v=h_j-1, x=1+S, and epsilon=d/x. The individual
accepted cut terms at i and j are K_i,K_j. Direct cancellation gives

    U_ij/K_i=epsilon^2/(1+epsilon)
                          +2d epsilon/(1+2T),          (28)
    U_ij/K_j=(h_i-1)^2(1+P+h_i+S)^2
                          /[(1+P)^2 x(x+d)].           (29)

Every occurrence of level v has a full H_v immediately after it, so
T>=S_v. If v>=2 then M_v>=M_2=4+2a+ac>5, and by (11),(12),

    2d/(1+2T)<=2r M_v/(2M_v-1)<=10r/9<=1/2.

Consequently, for epsilon<=1, (28) is at most1.

If epsilon>1, then x<d. The first selected cut must lie INSIDE the
copy of H_v immediately preceding j and its level satisfies u<v.
Otherwise that copy lies between the cuts, giving S>=S_v and
x>=M_v>d. Inside this copy put P_loc for its mass before i. The
subcopies of H_u immediately before and after i yield

    P>=P_loc, x>=M_u,
    1+P_loc=M_v-(h_i-1)-x.

Also h_i-1<=d/c<=r M_v/c and x<d<=r M_v. For fixed x, the factor
(1+P+h_i+S)/(1+P) decreases with P, so substituting P_loc in (29)
gives

    U_ij/K_j
      <=(h_i-1)^2 M_v^2/
          [(M_v-(h_i-1)-x)^2 x(x+d)]
      <r^2/[2(1-r-r/c)^2]
      <=648/841<1.                                    (30)

The penultimate step uses h_i-1<=r M_u<=r x and x/(x+d)<1/2.
For the last, r<=9/20 and c>=12/5 imply 1-r-r/c>=29/80.

It remains to include the two small levels. If v=0, then d=a<1/2 and
epsilon<=d. Equation (28) is at most d^2/(1+d)+2d^2<=2/3. If v=1,
then d=ac<1. Unless i is the one level-zero step immediately before j,
the intervening mass includes H_1, so epsilon<=1/2 and T>=S_1>=1;
(28) is then at most1/6+1/3=1/2. For that one adjacent pair x=1 and
h_i-1=a. Equation (29) is bounded by

    a^2(2+a)^2/(1+ac)<=1/(1+ac)<1,

because a<=sqrt(2)-1 implies a(2+a)<=1. This covers EVERY ordered pair,
including pairs near the end and repeated occurrences of either level.

Combining (14),(27)--(30), every member of the complete constant-tail
mechanism in Section6 has normalized error at most K_n(H_m). Therefore

    max{Q_m(d_0), K_n(H_m),
        supremum of the Section6 constant-tail scores}
      =1/(2S_m+1),
    limsup B_n times this maximum <=2/e<1.             (31)

This is a quantitative failure of a concrete retained-history mechanism
on the ASSIGNED constant-level family. Its tail bound allows all
dimensions, intercepts and positive values once its prefix and angle
range are fixed. Thus retuning only its two cuts, its angle inside (25),
or its final constant-gradient plane cannot produce the target comparison
for all appended b: b=d_0 already defeats that possibility.

The unrestricted W_n may be larger. In particular, (31) gives no upper
certificate for it, no primitive-validity conclusion, and no decision of
the original all-schedule/all-horizon problem. The remaining exact branch
is to leave (25), change more than two prefix cells, or allow genuinely
varying gradients within the remaining ruler block while retaining all
queried jets and the closest-minimizer normalization.

## 8. The entire accepted alternating-ray family also collapses to K

We now invoke the exact ray recurrence from accepted fact
`1fca3e5dd555cb4a`. No unverified ray exclusion is used. For any two
indices i<j in H_m, write P,S for their preceding and intervening masses,
and let u,v be their levels. Put r_u=a c^u and r_v=a c^v. We claim

    r_u r_v<(1+P)(1+S).                               (32)

If u>=v, or if i lies before the full H_v immediately preceding j,
the local copies give 1+P>=M_u and 1+S>=M_v. Thus (32) follows from
r_u<=r M_u, r_v<=r M_v and r<1.

Otherwise u<v and i lies inside that preceding H_v. Define P_loc there.
The local H_u copies on both sides of i give

    1+P_loc>=M_u, 1+S>=M_u,
    (1+P_loc)+(1+S)=M_v-r_u.

Expanding [(1+P_loc)-M_u][(1+S)-M_u]>=0 shows

    (1+P)(1+S)>=M_u(M_v-r_u-M_u).

For fixed u define D_v=M_u(M_v-r_u-M_u)-r_u r_v.
At v=u+1, the identity M_(u+1)=2M_u+r_u gives

    D_(u+1)=M_u^2-c r_u^2
               >=(1-cr^2)M_u^2>0,

since cr^2<=3969/8000<1. At successive levels,

    D_(v+1)-D_v=M_u M_v+[M_u-(c-1)r_u]r_v>0,

because (c-1)r<=261/400<1. This proves (32) for every pair, including
the small levels with empty local blocks.

Suppose a ray construction selected at least two checkpoints i,j. Its
first two gradients and first old offset are

    m_0=1/(1+P), m_1=r_u m_0/(1+S), O_1=P m_0.

By (32), the second-return budget satisfies

    r_v m_1-O_1
       =m_0[r_u r_v/(1+S)-P]<m_0.                    (33)

With exactly two checkpoints, the terminal old-slope constraint requires
this quantity to be at least (1+T)m_0>=m_0. With three or more, the next
nonterminal gradient is this quantity divided by1 plus its block mass,
and hence is strictly below m_0, violating same-ray slope monotonicity.
Thus no accepted ray construction has two or more checkpoints. The
zero- and one-checkpoint cases recover K exactly, so

    F_ray_n(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).                  (34)

## 9. The complete universal complex lens is also below Huber

This concerns the accepted Lens_n and maximal path-value Gap_h from fact
`dfde2fa326c908bf`, not the larger schedule-dependent domain D_h. The
accepted bound is

    Gap_(H_m)(z)<=(2alpha-|z|^2)|P_m(z)|^2,
    z=alpha+i beta,
    kappa_lens=alpha-|z|^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2)).        (35)

All chronological prefix jets and their interpolation cycles remain those
of the accepted definition. We only bound their actual terminal polynomial.
We prove, for m>=128,

    sup_(z in Lens_n) Gap_(H_m)(z)<1/(2S_m+1).         (36)

The endpoints z=0 and z=1 follow directly: the former has zero error,
and at the latter the upper bound in (35) is P_m(1)^2, covered by (21).
Every other lens point has 0<alpha<1 and kappa_lens>0. Put

    delta=tan(pi/(n+2))<=1/100,
    tau_z=|1-z|^2/(2kappa_lens),
    q_decay=99/100.

The delta bound follows from tan x<=2x for 0<=x<=1/2 and pi<4,
since n=2^m-1. The tangent inequality follows from sin x<=x and
cos x>=1-x^2/2>=7/8 on that interval. From the lens inequality,

    |beta|<=delta alpha(1-alpha),
    kappa_lens>=alpha(1-alpha)(1-delta^2/4),
    tau_z<=F_delta(1-alpha)/(2alpha),
    F_delta=(1+delta^2)/(1-delta^2/4)<101/100.           (37)

Consider a partial ruler H_j all of whose levels l<j satisfy
a c^l<tau_z, with j>=1. In particular tau_z>a>3/8, and (37) gives
alpha<101/176<3/5. Each step d_l of H_j satisfies

    d_l alpha<(1+tau_z)alpha
       <=alpha+F_delta(1-alpha)/2<81/100.              (38)

Its real factor is therefore positive, at least19/100. The inequalities
log(1-u)<=-u and log(1+u)<=u imply

    log|1-d_l z|
      <=-d_l alpha+d_l^2 beta^2/[2(19/100)^2].

Sum with the exact multiplicities of the partial ruler. Its sum of
squared steps is at most its largest step times S_j, and (38) bounds
that largest step by81/(100alpha). Hence

    |P_j(z)|<=exp[-(1-12delta^2)alpha S_j]
                       <=exp(-q_decay alpha S_j).    (39)

For the numerical coefficient here,
(81/100)/[2(19/100)^2]=4050/361<12, and
1-12/10000>99/100. This is a symbolic bound; no spectral sampling occurs.

If no level l<m reaches a c^l>=tau_z, (39) applies to H_m. From (35),

    Gap_(H_m)(z)<=2alpha exp(-2q_decay S_m alpha)
                        <=1/(q_decay e S_m).

Since S_m>=2 and e>8/3, its ratio to Huber is strictly less than

    (5/2)/(q_decay(8/3))=125/132<1.                   (40)

Otherwise let j<m be the first level with a c^j>=tau_z. Put
Y_j=c a c^j |z| |P_j(z)|. If j=0, Y_0=ac|z|<=ac<1.
If j>=1, (39) and S_j>=a c^j/r-1 give, by maximizing
v exp(-q_decay v/r) over v>=0,

    Y_j<= [cr sqrt(1+delta^2)/q_decay]
                              exp(q_decay alpha-1)
       <=(9/8)exp(-2/5)<1.                           (41)

Indeed cr<=441/400, sqrt(1+delta^2)<=10001/10000,
and dividing their product by99/100 gives a number below9/8.
Also alpha<3/5 and exp(2/5)>1+2/5>9/8.

For every level l>=j, the definition of tau_z gives exactly

    |1-d_l z|^2
      =a^2 c^(2l)|z|^2+|1-z|^2-2a c^l kappa_lens
      <=a^2 c^(2l)|z|^2.

Consequently the ruler recursion implies Y_(l+1)<=Y_l^2, so Y_m<=1.
If alpha>1/16, (35) and |z|>=alpha imply that the ratio of its bound
to Huber is at most

    (2S_m+1) 32/[c^2 a^2 c^(2m)]
      <=64/[(ac)^2 c^m]<128/2^m<1.                   (42)

If alpha<=1/16, then tau_z>=(1-alpha)/(2alpha)>=15/2>a,
so j>=1. The same estimate before (41) now gives

    Y_j<=(9/8)exp(1/16)/e
                  <(9/8)(16/15)(3/8)=9/20<1/2.

Here exp(t)<=1/(1-t) for 0<=t<1 follows termwise from its series,
and e>8/3 follows by retaining its terms through degree four.
There is at least one update after index j, hence Y_m<1/4. The fact
that tau_z<=a c^(m-1), together with
|1-z|^2>=1-2alpha and kappa_lens<=alpha, yields

    alpha>=1/[2(1+a c^(m-1))]=1/(2d_(m-1)).

Equation (35) is therefore at most

    1/[8c^2 a^2 c^(2m)alpha]
       <=d_(m-1)/[4c^2 a^2 c^(2m)].                  (43)

By (20), its ratio to Huber is less than3/16. Equations (40)--(43)
cover every lens point and prove (36). Consequently

    V_lens(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=F_ray_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).      (44)

Combining (21), (31), (34), (44) gives the same exact 2/e limit-superior
obstruction even after including ALL accepted single-cut, alternating-ray,
and universal-lens baselines, as well as the complete quadratic class and
the retained two-block constant-tail mechanism from Section6. The full
schedule-dependent complex domain, general multidimensional nonlinear
orbits, and variable-gradient tails remain outside this statement.

# Mixed gradient-moment storage: exact transport and local loss

Status: candidate derivation, not verifier-accepted. No solver or new verifier
call is used. This completes the unfinished calculation in the previous round's
local notes. It does not establish the project's unrestricted target.

## Definitions

Normalize smoothness to 1. For a nonnegative GD block h with samples x_i,
g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), let

    S=sum h_i, A=S+1, q=g_n,
    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
        +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-S A||q||^2,
    b_h=sum_(i<n) h_i(h_i-2),
    B_h=sum_(i<n) h_i(h_i-2)||g_i||^2,
    P_theta(h)=V_h-theta(B_h-b_h||q||^2).

Here B_h is a trajectory quantity, whereas b_h is a schedule scalar; neither is
the project's benchmark denominator B_n. For actual samples of the same convex
1-smooth function set

    I_ij=f_j-f_i-<g_i,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2/2 >=0.

The proof of this inequality can be given without an external theorem: subtract
the affine function with gradient g_i from f, so x_i is a minimizer of the
resulting convex 1-smooth function. Its one-step descent bound from x_j with
step 1 gives f_j-f_i-<g_i,x_j-x_i> >= ||g_j-g_i||^2/2.

## 1. A complete objective certificate, including the price

Translate a chosen minimizer to zero and its value to zero. Write X=x_n.
Telescoping squared distances and expanding the optimum slacks gives

    V_h=||x_0||^2-||X||^2-2S f_n-S A||q||^2
        -2 sum h_i I_(i,*),
    B_h=||X||^2-||x_0||^2
        +2 sum h_i[I_(i,*)+I_(*,i)].

Thus the following is an EXACT equality:

    (1+theta)||x_0||^2-2(2S+1)f_n
      =P_theta(h)
       +2(1+theta) sum h_i I_(i,*)
       +2theta sum h_i I_(*,i)+2A I_(n,*)
       +(X,q)^T M_theta (X,q),                         (1)

    M_theta = [[1+theta, -A],[-A, A^2-theta b_h]].

For theta>=0 all displayed optimum-edge coefficients are nonnegative. The
matrix is PSD exactly when theta[A^2-(1+theta)b_h]>=0. Therefore, if

    P_theta(h)>=0 on every legal block trajectory,
    theta>=0,  b_h<=A^2/(1+theta),

then W_n(h)<=(1+theta)/(2A-1). In particular the extra factor 1+theta cannot be
omitted. Equation (1) gives the actual full boundary matrix and all new
interpolation multipliers, not only a distance estimate.

Negative theta is not certified by this conversion: its incoming optimum
coefficients are negative. Even the boundary matrix fails for -1<theta<0,
since b_h<=S^2-2S=A^2-4A+3<A^2 and hence
theta[A^2-(1+theta)b_h]<0. This is a restriction on (1), not on every conceivable
conversion of P_theta.

## 2. Generalized physical primitive join with all actual multipliers

Take physical head a of capacity K=1+sum a_i, ending at y with gradient g,
then a bridge of length 1+r, then physical tail b of capacity A=1+sum b_i,
ending at z with gradient q. Tail internal indices i mean all its gradient
query samples before z. Let

    0<=r<A,
    c=(K+r)/(A-r),  k=1+2c,
    Delta=r(K+A+r)-KA,
    J=2c[sum_(i internal to b) b_i(I_(i,y)+I_(i,z))
          +I_(z,y)+r I_(y,z)].

Every multiplier in J is nonnegative. Repeated or coincident samples are
interpreted as the actual sample data; no inherited point or value is reset.
Direct expansion yields

    V_(a,1+r,b)=V_a+k V_b+c Delta(||q||^2-||g||^2)+J.   (2)

For checking (2), subtract V_a+k V_b from the left. Its function part is
2(K+r)(f_y-f_z)-4c sum b_i(f_i-f_z). Its gradient part is

    (K^2-K-r-r^2)||g||^2
    -2c sum b_i(1-b_i)||g_i||^2
    +[-(K+A+r-1)(K+A+r)+k(A-1)A]||q||^2.

Expand J, use x_i=y-(1+r)g-sum_(j<i in b)b_j g_j and
z=y-(1+r)g-sum_(j in b)b_j g_j. The terms containing internal gradient products
cancel because c(A-r)=K+r. The remaining coefficients are precisely the
displayed ones after c Delta(||q||^2-||g||^2) is added.

Moreover

    b_(a,1+r,b)=b_a+b_b+r^2-1,
    B_(a,1+r,b)=B_a+B_b+(r^2-1)||g||^2.

Consequently (2) gives the exact new storage identity

    P_theta(a,1+r,b)=P_theta(a)+k P_(theta/k)(b)+J
       +[c Delta+theta(b_a+r^2-1)](||q||^2-||g||^2).    (3)

The scalar closure equation is therefore

    c Delta+theta(b_a+r^2-1)=0.                       (4)

Inputs require DIFFERENT labels theta and theta/k. Same-label copying is not a
valid consequence of (3). At theta=0, (4) is the usual primitive join, including
its exact Silver scaling. No loss is inserted into that calibration.

## 3. Nontrivial finite calibration and an exact equality test

For two empty inputs K=A=1, equation (4) reduces to

    (1-theta)r^2+2(1+theta)r-(1+theta)=0.

At theta=1 it gives r=1/2, i.e. the step h=3/2, and (3) reads

    P_1(3/2)=6 I_10+3 I_01 >=0.

At theta=0 it gives h=sqrt(2). This exhibits a real capacity gain when theta
is added, but the objective certificate at theta=1 is only 2/4=1/2.

For any block and the legal full-curvature function f(x)=||x||^2/2, define
Pi_h=product_(i<n)(1-h_i). Then

    P_theta(h)=[1+theta-(A^2+theta(1-b_h))Pi_h^2]||x_0||^2. (5)

This follows directly from the two identities in section 1: every optimum
slack vanishes and q=x_n=Pi_h x_0. If a recursively constructed block is built
from empty blocks using only (3)-(4), all interpolation slacks also vanish on
this quadratic, so the bracket in (5) must be exactly zero. It is a useful
hostile equality check for any proposed parameters; no numerical search is
needed.

## 4. The balanced deformation has a well-defined finite first variation

Let lambda=1+sqrt(2), t=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda. For each fixed m, define a local
family h_m(theta), of length 2^m-1, recursively near theta=0:

    h_0(theta)=(),
    h_(m+1)(theta)=(h_m(theta),1+r,h_m(theta/k)),

where r,k solve (4) and k=1+2(K+r)/(A-r), with

    K=A_m(theta), A=A_m(theta/k),
    A_m(theta)=1+sum h_m(theta).

This is an implicit finite family, not an assertion that a common fixed
positive theta works for every m. Its smooth local existence follows
inductively from the implicit-function theorem. At theta=0, one has

    K=A=lambda^m, r=t lambda^m, c=lambda, k=lambda^2.

The partial derivative of the first equation with respect to r is
2lambda sqrt(2)lambda^m>0. Its derivative with respect to k is zero: the tail
argument theta/k has zero k-derivative at theta=0. The derivative of the second
equation with respect to k is 1 for the same reason. Thus the two-by-two
Jacobian is invertible. All physical steps, k and A-r remain positive in a
small neighborhood for each fixed m. Inductively (3) then supplies a complete
nonnegative interpolation certificate for P_theta. The input labels retain
the sign of theta. For sufficiently small theta>=0, the objective matrix in
(1) is also PSD because b_h<A^2 at theta=0.

Define a_m=A_m'(0) and write b_m=b_(h_m(0)). The undeformed recurrence gives

    b_0=0, b_(m+1)=2b_m+t^2 lambda^(2m)-1,
    b_m=beta lambda^(2m)-gamma 2^m+1,
    beta=(8sqrt(2)-11)/7,
    gamma=(8sqrt(2)-4)/7.

Differentiating the capacity join at theta=0 yields

    a_0=0,
    a_(m+1)=sqrt(2)a_m
       -[b_m+t^2 lambda^(2m)-1]/[2lambda sqrt(2)lambda^m]. (6)

To see every derivative in (6), the standard capacity map
Omega(K,A)=(K+A+sqrt((K+A)^2+4KA))/2 has both first derivatives lambda/2
at K=A. The derivatives of the two child capacities are a_m and
a_m/lambda^2, so their combined contribution is sqrt(2)a_m. The direct
theta-derivative of (4) is the second term of (6); derivatives of c disappear
there because Delta=0.

Put eta=sqrt(2)/lambda=2-sqrt(2), d=beta+t^2=(10-6sqrt(2))/7, and
v_m=a_m/lambda^m. Then exactly

    v_(m+1)=eta v_m+[-d+gamma eta^(2m)]/[2lambda^2 sqrt(2)],
    v_m={-d(1-eta^m)/(1-eta)
           +gamma(eta^m-eta^(2m))/(eta-eta^2)}
          /[2lambda^2 sqrt(2)].                       (7)

In particular

    lim_(m->infty) v_m=-(10-6sqrt(2))/[14lambda sqrt(2)]<0. (8)

The operations here are: differentiate at theta=0 for each finite m, then
take the m-limit of the exact formula (7). No interchange with an unproved
fixed-theta asymptotic limit is being used.

## 5. Every finite balanced first variation loses after objective conversion

Compare the certified bound to its own undeformed primitive bound:

    R_m(theta)=(1+theta)(2lambda^m-1)/(2A_m(theta)-1).

For m>=1,

    R_m'(0)=1-2a_m/(2lambda^m-1).                    (9)

This is strictly positive for every m. An explicit uniform derivative bound
is available. Formula (7) gives v_1=1/(lambda^3 sqrt(2)). For m>=1 its forcing
term is at most the m=1 forcing term, and

    gamma eta^2-d < (1-eta)(gamma-d),

because the difference is gamma(eta^2+eta-1)-d eta<0, with
eta^2+eta-1=7-5sqrt(2)<0. Induction therefore gives v_m<=v_1 for all m>=1.
If v_m is negative, (9) is greater than 1. Otherwise the factor
2lambda^m/(2lambda^m-1) is at most its m=1 value. Hence

    R_m'(0)>=1-2/[lambda^2 sqrt(2)(2lambda-1)]>0.    (10)

Thus for EACH fixed finite balanced horizon, this concrete deformation is
worse for every sufficiently small positive theta. Its large-horizon
derivative has the limit

    lim R_m'(0)=1+(10-6sqrt(2))/[14lambda sqrt(2)]>1. (11)

This closes the proposed infinitesimal balanced improvement in the specific
P_theta storage and conversion (1). It does not rule out finite deformations,
unbalanced trees, another conversion, or more general moment storage. In
particular (10) alone supplies no common neighborhood in theta for all m.

## Literature and scope

The active mechanism was checked against the already downloaded exact text of
Fazlyab et al., arXiv:1705.03615, Theorem 3.1 and its full proof. Its method
requires a proved supply inequality, nonnegative coefficients, and an actual
terminal PSD bound. Equations (1)-(4) give those objects for this narrower
candidate; that paper supplies no completeness theorem or asymptotic
improvement. Matlas returned no relevant long-step composition result on the
new targeted query, and web search provided no additional useful mechanism.
No external theorem is a proof premise of the algebra above.

The practical next question is whether a function-value-coupled moment can be
transported without paying the same initial factor, or whether a concrete
nonlocal correlation changes the bridge closure. Re-optimizing (4) near the
balanced zero-label branch cannot supply the desired improvement.

# Three complete original-row laws for the unchanged terminal-objective survivor

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; ONE analytical invocation68
after actual ended baseline67. Ended ceiling68, attempted ceiling69, cap1.
TASK.md and allocation master_guidance3c739eae3e996e2b govern this round.
Later read guidancea32faa619edd28dc explicitly leaves max3r68 unchanged.
Consult OFF. No fourth coefficient law or automatic renewal is authorized.

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL SCREENING DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. All new
identities, flow calculations and formal counterexamples below are parts
of this one joint draft. No new fact, formal submission or full-class
objective upper is asserted. The original unrestricted target is OPEN.

The THREE coefficient laws are explicit on every original coordinate and
every ordered row. All signs and complete value flow pass. Each pays the
mandatory old module-only negative direction. Each fails a different
explicit full formal PSD test, uniformly in the actual depth. NONE of
these tests is an actual-function rejection of E, F or M.

## 1. Physical schedules and precise objective

All spaces are finite-dimensional real Euclidean spaces. For a nonnegative
length-N physical word h and L>0, W_N(h)=2R_N(h) is the supremum of

  2(f(x_N)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)

over every convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, every
minimizer x_star and x_0!=x_star, with the actual updates
x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t)/L. Every step is predetermined. The upper
target remains q_goal=10/(97 lambda^m), uniformly for sufficiently large m.

Set lambda=1+sqrt(2), A=lambda^m, zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A))/2,
e=A/zeta=(zeta-1)/2, and

  q_0=(), q_(j+1)=(q_j,1+(lambda-2)lambda^j,q_j),
  p_m=(q_m,1+e), b1=1+7A/10, b2=1+6A/5, tau_c=3/5.

The exact identities zeta^2-zeta=2A and 2e^2+e=A imply A-e=2e^2>0.
The physical lengths and sums of q_m and p_m are 2^m-1,A-1 and
2^m,A+e respectively. Write Dp=zeta^2=2A+zeta.

Keep the previous THREE physical words unchanged:

  H_F=(p_m,b1,q_m,tau_c,b2,q_m,1),
  H_M=(q_m,b1,p_m,tau_c,b2,q_m,1),
  H_E=(q_m,b1,q_m,tau_c,b2,p_m).

Their actual horizons are N_F=N_M=3*2^m+2 and N_E=3*2^m+1.
Their actual global Huber denominators are

  D_F=D_M=49A/5+2e+21/5,
  D_E=49A/5+2e+11/5.

For E let r=2^m-1. Its original indices are:

  first q: steps0,...,r-1, terminal/bridge query P=r;
  b1: step P;
  second q: steps r+1,...,2r, terminal/contraction query C=2r+1;
  tau_c: step C;
  b2: step/query B=2r+2;
  p_m starts at S=2r+3;
  its q part ends at the cap query K=3r+3;
  cap: step K;
  original terminal N=3r+4=3*2^m+1.

Thus the original contraction gradients are g_C and g_B, both independent
formal coordinates. Its two directed original rows are Q_(C,B),Q_(B,C).
The total physical mass before p_m is s_pre=39A/10+3/5. The last module
keeps its actual starting point y=x_S=x_0-Delta, where
Delta=sum_(t<S) h_t g_t. No reset of this point is made.

## 2. Recall, accepted inputs and retrieval

The full previous report1ef7c860ceeda980 was read in its entirety. A direct
text comparison of the shared stores confirmed that conclusion
0d1e581edfc0f1d9 and counterexample74cce3a2287feccd carry identical full
evidence. Dead_end31aca41559dc4949 was also fully read. Their status is
UNVERIFIED, not a predecessor fact. They show that the previous Huber/cut
and quadratic screens did not reject these physical words. Those restricted
upper calculations are not used as correctness premises here.

Only accepted facts04219554e41d9750 and1252b8823bb5a290 are used below:
the exact primitive/dominant original-space identities, unconditional
original benchmark/ray conclusions, interpolation necessity and the full
formal-space certificate framework. No improving-seed hypothesis from042
is assumed. Acceptedb98bb807d514e80d remains the exact lower-channel input
in the earlier report; no new physical lower witness is claimed here.

The query-memory, search-math-results, obtain-immediate-conclusions,
propose-subgoal-decomposition-plans, direct-proving,
construct-counterexamples and identify-key-failures procedures were used.
Retrieval stage: mature_subproblem. Active program: full original-row
coupling of the surviving typed words. Missing mechanism: payment of
module displacement and singular internal directions. Search mode: repair.

Matlas was called twice, on the endpoint/composition claim and the
zero-flow/internal-mode claim. All four and three returned hits were
inspected; none repaired this interface. The self-contracted-sequence
hit requires every step<=1/L, which these physical words violate.
A constant-stepsize hit requires eta<2/L; other hits concern different
problems or constraints. No result from these searches is imported.
Web search identified primary
composability sources. The exact existing paper text
downloads/zhang-jiang-2410.12395.txt, proofs3.1 and3.2, was read:
[Zhang--Jiang, arXiv2410.12395](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12395). Its cross-row argument explicitly uses
the prescribed bridge coefficient. Here the mismatch is retained as an
exact bilinear term instead of importing that theorem outside its scope.
No external theorem is used as a black box; no external_refs entry is
asserted. This round opens no new family of physical functions.

The THREE plans, in their attempted order, are a6c94c468d05431c,
682e502804cbb518 and3a1b340c862412cd. All new supporting algebra remains
within this joint unverified draft; no partial result is promoted or used
later as an accepted fact.

## 3. Original coordinates and full exact module arrays

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain the free initial vector
x_0, every original gradient g_0,...,g_N and every value f_0,...,f_N.
Use x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and

  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Accepted1252 proves Q_ij<=0 on actual smooth convex jets. A uniform upper
certificate requires nonnegative multipliers on ALL original distinct
ordered pairs and finite-node flow out_i-in_i=2 at N, zero elsewhere,
followed by full PSD. A pair assigned zero below is still an original
available row; it is not removed from the problem. Coincident positions
never impose equality of formal gradients.

For a word w of sum s and local starting vector a, put

  V_w=2sum_(t<length(w))w_t(f_t-f_z)
       +sum_t w_t(1-w_t)||g_t||^2-s(s+1)||g_z||^2.

Here z is its original terminal index. Accepted042 gives the following
explicit entire finite array Lambda^q for q_j, with

  V_(q_j)+sum Lambda^q_ij Q_ij=0.                         (3.1)

Define it by symbolic induction, not an executed recurrence. The empty
array is zero. At a q_j--q_j join let a_j=lambda^j,
d=(lambda-2)a_j, kappa=lambda^2, p its original bridge query and z its
original terminal. Embed the entire left child array with factor1 and
right child array with factorkappa. For every right-child nonterminal
query t of original physical step w_t, add

  Lambda_(t,p)+=(kappa-1)w_t,
  Lambda_(t,z)+=(kappa-1)w_t;
  Lambda_(z,p)+=kappa-1,
  Lambda_(p,z)+=(kappa-1)d.                              (3.2)

All unspecified pairs get zero; multiple contributions add. Every sign
is nonnegative. The identity d(2a_j+d)=a_j^2 and accepted042's full join
identity make the parent remainder the left child remainder plus kappa
times the right, hence zero by induction. Its finite flow is -2w_i on
each nonterminal i and +2s on the terminal. All original internal gradients
and values occur in (3.1); no endpoint-gradient identification is used.

For the actual p_m inside E define the weights on EVERY original module
query, including its terminal, by

  u^p_i=h_i for S<=i<K; u^p_K=A+1; u^p_N=zeta.
  Up=sum_(i=S)^N u^p_i g_i; sum_i u^p_i=Dp.

Let Fp be its finite array: the q_m array on S,...,K plus the single
entry (N,K) of coefficient4A. All other finite pairs in this module are
zero. The actual unscaled module identity from accepted042 is

  ||y||^2-2Dp f_N+sum Fp_ij Q_ij+2sum_i u^p_i Q_(i,star)
                                  =||y-Up||^2.         (3.3)

The correct whole-run target expression from this array alone is

  M0(q)=Dp q||x_0||^2-2Dp f_N+sum Fp Q+2sum_i u^p_i Q_(i,star)
       =Dp q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,Up>+2<Delta,Up>+||Up||^2.  (3.4)

Equivalently it is Dp q||x_0||^2-||y||^2+||y-Up||^2.
The two mixed terms in (3.4) remain in every law derived from this array.
Its finite-plus-optimum flow is 2Dp at N and zero at every other original
finite index. Dividing the complete array and quadratic by Dp gives the
normalization2 at N. The physical module displacement is

  Sp=sum_(i=S)^(K-1)h_i g_i+(1+e)g_K,
  Up-Sp=(A-e)g_K+zeta g_N.                             (3.5)

Thus Up does not determine Sp. This distinction will be decisive.

## 4. Two explicitly expanded zero-flow additions

For any of the THREE actual q_m occurrences, starting at a and ending at
z, define the following entire ORIGINAL circulation:

  Tq=sum Lambda^q_ij Q_ij
       +2sum_(nonterminal i in q)h_i Q_(i,star)
       +2(A-1) Q_(star,z).                             (4.1)

Its multipliers are all nonnegative. Its value flow is zero: each
nonterminal has -2h_i+2h_i=0; the terminal has
2(A-1)-2(A-1)=0. Direct expansion of (3.1) gives the EXACT identity

  Tq=||x_z||^2-||x_a||^2+(A^2-1)||g_z||^2.             (4.2)

For completeness, the value coefficients cancel in
-V_q+2sum h_i Q_(i,star)+2(A-1)Q_(star,z). Its remaining internal
gradient terms are sum h_i^2||g_i||^2-2sum h_i<g_i,x_i>.
Writing S_q=sum h_i g_i and x_i=x_a-local_prefix_i turns this into
||S_q||^2-2<x_a,S_q>=||x_z||^2-||x_a||^2.
The endpoint coefficient is (A-1)(A+1)=A^2-1. This proves (4.2)
on every independent original formal vector, retaining the actual x_a
through all earlier physical steps. In particular the second/third
starting square is not independently normalized. Denote the THREE
such circulations by T1,T2,T3 in chronological order in E.

The independent contraction circulation is

  Ttau=Q_(C,B)+Q_(B,C)
       =||g_C-g_B||^2-(3/5)<g_C-g_B,g_C>.               (4.3)

Both directed original coefficients equal1, and its full value flow is
zero. No damping relation or equality of these gradients is assumed.
On actual trajectories (4.3)<=0, which gives the ball centered at
(7/10)g_C of radius(3/10)||g_C|| for g_B. The present proof uses the
entire quadratic (4.3), not a fixed endpoint of that ball.

## 5. Law I: the complete actual-bridge right-spine array

The purpose is to carry the exact row formula through every ACTUAL bridge
and keep the resulting mismatch. The empty prefix before b2 is an empty
primitive child of capacity1. Define symbolic numbers and weight vectors
as follows; this rule is never executed by a program:

  eta3=(1+sqrt(1+8/Dp))/2, D3=2+eta3 Dp,
  u3_B=2; u3_i=eta3 u^p_i for every S<=i<=N;

  eta2=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D3))/2, D2=2A+eta2 D3,
  u2_i=h_i on r+1<=i<C; u2_C=A+1;
  u2_i=eta2 u3_i on B and every S<=i<=N;

  eta1=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D2))/2, D1=2A+eta1 D2,
  u1_i=h_i on0<=i<P; u1_P=A+1;
  u1_i=eta1 u2_i on every query of the right child.

The sums of u3,u2,u1 are D3,D2,D1 respectively. These D1,D2,D3 are
declared coefficient masses, NOT the actual global massD_E. In particular
no physical bridge has been replaced by the one associated with them.

Every finite coefficient is given by the following entire recursive law:

  F3=eta3^2 Fp; add (4/Dp)u^p_i to (i,B) for every S<=i<=N.
  F2=Lambda^(second q)+eta2^2 F3;
       add (4A/D3)u3_i to (i,C) at every right-child query.
  F1=Lambda^(first q)+eta1^2 F2;
       add (4A/D2)u2_i to (i,P) at every right-child query.

Add (C,B) and (B,C) with coefficient1, and optimum rows
(i,star) with coefficient2u1_i for every original query. Every unlisted
pair, including every (star,i), has coefficient0. This lists ALL final
multipliers as nonnegative sums, including both contraction orientations.
Divide every coefficient by D1 when expressing the usual normalized
certificate at any proposed levelq.

Here is a complete finite-value-flow audit. At any one join, a primitive
left child of capacitya and a right child with weightsv of sumD use
eta^2-eta=2a/D. The inherited finite right flow is -2eta^2 v_i
off its terminal; new cross edges contribute4a v_i/D and optimum rows
contribute2eta v_i. Their sum is0. At the bridge the left finite flow
is2(a-1), its optimum row is2(a+1), and cross edges bring incoming4a;
the net flow is0. The right terminal gets2eta^2D=2(2a+eta D).
The empty-left casea=1 has the same cancellation. The q internal flows
cancel against their exact optimum weights. Thus the entire unscaled
array has flow2D1 at N, zero everywhere else. Ttau adds zero flow.

To expand its full quadratic define

  R3=2g_B+eta3 Up,
  R2=sum_(r+1<=i<C)h_i g_i+(A+1)g_C+eta2 R3,
  U1=sum_(i<P)h_i g_i+(A+1)g_P+eta1 R2,
  d3=b2-(1+1/eta3),
  d2=tau_c-(1+A/eta2),
  d1=b1-(1+A/eta1)=A(7/10-1/eta1).

The EXACT complete identity is

  D1 q||x_0||^2-2D1 f_N+sum F1 Q+2sum_i u1_i Q_(i,star)+Ttau
   =(D1 q-1)||x_0||^2+||x_0-U1||^2+Ttau
      +2eta1^2 d1<g_P,R2>
      +2eta1^2 eta2^2 d2<g_C,R3>
      +2eta1^2 eta2^2 eta3^2 d3<g_B,Up>.               (5.1)

This is an identity on the ENTIRE original coordinate space. To verify
the mismatch at one join, define the algebraic energy explicitly by

  J(h,u)=||x_start||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)
         -2(sum_i u_i)f_z-||x_start-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.

The coefficient massD=sumv need not equal1+2sum(right physical steps)
in this algebraic extension of accepted042's J definition. No dominance
property of the mismatched right child is assumed. Its canonical identity
uses beta0=1+a/eta. With the ACTUAL beta=beta0+d, the right starting
vector becomes x_p-beta g_p; retain that vector in J_right. Relative to
the canonical algebra, the parent optimum terms change by
2eta d<g_p,sum v_i g_i>, while the cross rows on the opposite side
change by -2(eta^2-eta)d<g_p,sum v_i g_i>. Their difference is
2eta^2d<g_p,sum v_i g_i>. All internal right differences are unchanged
by this translation. This proves the one-join mismatch identity and
successive substitution proves (5.1). It uses only sumv, not a false
claim that an already mismatched right child is dominant.

Mandatory old test: x_0=0, g_0=-v0/h_0, all other pre-p gradients0,
all internal p gradients0, g_N=v0/zeta, with v0!=0 and h_0=sqrt(2).
Then Delta=-v0,Up=v0, all three bridge gradients are0, Ttau=0, and

square||U1||^2. This new array pays the old module-only direction.

D2=G(A,D3)>=G(A,2A)=(3+sqrt(5))A>5A. Consequently

(10/7)^2-10/7=30/49>2/5 and the polynomial is increasing above1,

Choose x_0=0, g_P=v0, all second-q and contraction/b2 gradients0,

  h_0 g_0=-(A+1+eta1 eta2 eta3)v0.

  2eta1^2 d1 eta2 eta3||v0||^2<0.                      (5.2)

Thus law I fails FULL PSD for every m>=1 and every levelq, including

x_0=0 direction. The correct signs/flow and old-mode payment do not

## 6. Law II: all pre-p terminal circulations and exact prefix arrays

Begin with the complete p array (3.3), add T1,T2,Ttau, and add

  Cpre=sum_(t<S)h_t[Q_(t,N)+Q_(N,t)].                  (6.1)

This is an explicit cross-module row at EVERY original pre-p physical
step, including both long bridges and the contraction. All coefficients
are nonnegative. Each bracket has zero value flow. T1,T2 are expanded
by (3.2),(4.1), so no unknown prefix array remains. The complete unscaled
flow remains2Dp at N, zero elsewhere. Divide the array by Dp for normalized
flow2. The exact full pulled-back target quadratic is

  MII(q)=Dp q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,Up>+2<Delta,Up>+||Up||^2
                                  +T1+T2+Ttau+Cpre.   (6.2)

Neither any starting square nor any original internal gradient/value has
been omitted. The incoming optimum rows of (4.1) are explicitly allowed;
this is a full original-row law, not a selected terminal-oracle argument.

Mandatory old test: the same vector as in section5 has M0=-||v0||^2,
T1=||v0||^2,T2=Ttau=0. Its exact terminal circulation is

  Cpre=[1/h_0-1+(2-h_0)/zeta+s_pre/zeta^2]||v0||^2.    (6.3)

For A>=1, zeta<=2sqrt(A)<=2A, since the defining polynomial evaluated

New full original test: choose a unit vector v0, x_0=0 and h_0 g_0=v0.

  g_S=-(A+1)t v0/h_0, g_K=t v0, g_N=0,                (6.4)

with all other module gradients0. The actual first physical step of its

The obstruction extends beyond the selected coefficients1 in T1,T2:

## 7. Law III: complete all-query terminal transport plus all q circulations

The final permitted law retains (3.3) and adds T1,T2,T3,Ttau and

  Call=sum_(t<N)h_t[Q_(t,N)+Q_(N,t)].                  (7.1)

All ORIGINAL queried indices, including every internal q gradient, the

The exact original quadratic is

  MIII(q)=Dp q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,Up>+2<Delta,Up>+||Up||^2
                             +T1+T2+T3+Ttau+Call.       (7.2)

The old mandatory test has T3=0 and
Call=Cpre+(A+e)||v0||^2/zeta^2, so (7.2) is strictly positive by (6.3).

This law ALSO pays the explicit law-II failure vector (6.4) with

   +[A(A+1)+e(A-e)+(A+1)^2/h_0]/(A-e)^2>0.            (7.3)

Every term in the last numerator is positive, and

New zero-displacement internal test: for every m>=4 the first q_m

Choose x_0=0, a unit vector v0, and

with EVERY other original gradient, including all module endpoints,

Delta=0,Up=0 and all THREE q starting/ending positions and endpoint

The starting point before i is0, the query position x_j is -v0,
and x_N=0. Only these two terms in (7.1) are nonzero:

  h_i[Q_(i,N)+Q_(N,i)]=1/h_i,

Their sum, and hence the ENTIRE full formal quadratic(7.2), is exactly

This failure is uniform for every m>=4 and every q. It occurs on an

The same direction defeats any positive common coefficient on Call,
regardless of the nonnegative coefficients of T1,T2,T3,Ttau. Those

This is NOT an obstruction to general original multipliers: extra rows

## 8. Exact benchmark audit at the TRUE horizons

Keep the original ALL-split recurrences

  C_0=B_0=1,
  C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
  B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),

All split indices are integers; no optimizer/DP or arithmetic replay was

  B_(n+1)-B_n<=8sqrt(2)(n+1)^(rho-1),
  lambda^(-j)B_(3*2^j-1)<=Z_3
      <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(3*2^j))+3/100)^2.            (8.1)

These conclusions are unconditional within that fact, independent of its

  B_(N_X)/A >= B_3071/lambda^10 >64000/6730>19/2,        (8.2)

where the last comparison is128000>127870. This uses the true offsets:

Thus a re-derived unconditional upper interface at every actual horizon is

  B_(N_X)/A <= Z_3+72*2^(-m)
       <= (sqrt(B_3072)+3/100)^2/lambda^10+72*2^(-m).   (8.3)

The previous JOINT UNVERIFIED report quoted a specific n=3072 table

## 9. Failure synthesis and precise handoff

Law I: ALL original signs and flow pass; mandatory old direction is paid.
The noncanonical bridge leaves the explicit bilinear mismatch(5.1).
The independently free first-q displacement cancels the final square
and makes(5.2) negative. This excludes exactly the displayed right-spine

Law II: ALL original signs and flow pass; mandatory old direction is

Law III: ALL original signs and flow pass; the old direction and the

endpoint gradients. Every q circulation and every inherited square
misses this direction; full physical terminal sampling is negative.

These THREE laws are mutually explicit full arrays, not unknown PSD

# Full primitive certificates: cut slack and quantitative prefix control

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4's final
authorized round23. Fact04219554e41d9750 supplies the precise full seed
interface and its conditional asymptotic consequence, but does not verify
the new statements below. No seed existence or nonexistence is claimed.
No optimizer, numerical evaluation, finite replay, or fact_submit is used.

Novelty relative to the shared record: f7cee3034df8cb7f already derived the
exact saturated-cut structure and the signed tail credit. The new content
here is an exact positive-slack budget, including steps below1, its full
matrix consequences, and explicit quantitative control of a prefix when
a cut is near equality. The equality case is rederived as a consistency
check; the old claim of automatic nonnegative tail restriction is not used.
All new implications are proved together here; no unverified finding is a
premise. The accepted inputs are04219554e41d9750 and1252b8823bb5a290.

## 1. Full formal interface and the gradient-difference matrix

Let r>=1, h_0,...,h_(r-1)>=0, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t for0<=i<=r,
s=P_r, and C=1+s. Set h_r=0 only for formulas. Indices of the formal
gradients range over0,...,r. Let e be the all-ones column, and define

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)),
    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,  M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.

Assume Lambda_ii=0, all off-diagonal Lambda_ij>=0, L^T e=omega,
and M>=0 on the entire original space R^(r+1). Thus this is precisely
the full finite certificate in fact04219554e41d9750, with arbitrary r.
No multiplier support, rank, or equality between formal gradients is imposed.

For x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)+sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                               -s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

The full coefficient identity from fact04219554e41d9750 is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = <M,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.       (1)

The residuals Q_ij are nonpositive on every actual convex differentiable
1-smooth trajectory by fact1252b8823bb5a290. Hence this interface implies
V_h>=0 on all such trajectories with a minimizer.

Here is an algebraic proof of the constant-gradient kernel, needed below.
Since Te=e+(P_0,...,P_r)^T and
2sum_(i<r)h_i P_i=s^2-sum_(i<r)h_i^2, we have

    e^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2,
    e^T LT e=omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2.

Thus e^T M e=0. Positive semidefiniteness implies Me=0: otherwise for
some y the linear term in (e+t y)^T M(e+t y) would be negative for one
sign of sufficiently small t.

For0<=j<r let u^j be the indicator of indices0,...,j. Let U have these
r columns and put K_jump=U^T M U. For any scalar formal vector g,

    g=e*g_r+sum_(j<r)u^j(g_j-g_(j+1)),
    g^T M g=sum_(i,j<r)(K_jump)_ij(g_i-g_(i+1))(g_j-g_(j+1)). (2)

In particular K_jump>=0. Conversely, under Me=0, its PSD property is
equivalent to full M>=0 by(2). This is a congruence identity retaining
all formal gradients, not a deletion of zero-step coordinates.

## 2. A legal cut trajectory for every positive step

Fix j with b=h_j>0. In this section define

    P=P_j, R=s-P_(j+1), A=1+P, B=1+R, d=b-1,
    v=1-b                 if 0<b<=1,
    v=(1-b)/B             if b>=1,
    w=1-v>0.

The two formulas agree at b=1. There is one legal scalar convex1-smooth
function with a minimizer whose GD trajectory has gradients1 at all
indices i<=j and v at all indices k>j, starting from x_0=A. Its positions
are

    x_i=1+P_j-P_i                         (i<=j),
    x_k=1-b-v(P_k-P_(j+1))                (k>j).             (3)

All sampled values can be chosen as f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.

For b>1, use the derivative clip(x,[v,1]) and f(0)=0. The bridge lands
at -d=vB, the suffix remains at or below v, and its terminal point is v.
The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, and0 is a
minimizer. On each plateau the stated values follow by integration.

For0<b<=1, v=1-b>=0. Put zeta=v(1-R)-1, strictly below every suffix
sample and below v. Define a derivative on the whole real line by

    f'(x)=0                  for x<=zeta-v,
          x-zeta+v           for zeta-v<=x<=zeta,
          v                  for zeta<=x<=v,
          x                  for v<=x<=1,
          1                  for x>=1.

Choose the integration constant by f(v)=v^2/2. This derivative is again
continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, and its zero region contains
minimizers. The bridge lands at v. The suffix stays in[zeta,v], so its
gradient is v. Integration gives the stated sampled values on both
plateaus. Degenerate intervals when v=0 cause no difficulty. The minimum
value need not be zero; V_h and every finite Q are invariant under adding
a constant to f.

For any scalar data satisfying f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, telescoping the GD
squares gives

    V_h=x_0^2-(x_r+s g_r)^2.

On(3), x_r=-d-vR, hence the exact energy is

    E_j=A^2-[(A+d)v-d]^2.                                (4)

The same sampled-value identity gives Q_ik=(g_i-g_k)(x_k-g_k).
Define, for k>j,

    t_jk=d+v(1+P_k-P_(j+1)).

These numbers are nonnegative, with the more transparent forms

    t_jk=v(P_k-P_(j+1))          if0<b<=1,
    t_jk=(-v)(s-P_k)             ifb>=1.

Every within-block residual is zero. Every cross residual is exactly

    -Q_(i,k)=w*t_jk,
    -Q_(k,i)=w*(P_j-P_i),              i<=j<k.             (5)

Since g=v e+w u^j and Me=0, substitution into the complete identity(1)
gives the exact slack budget

    E_j=w^2(K_jump)_jj
       +w sum_(i<=j<k)[t_jk Lambda_(i,k)
                                      +(P_j-P_i)Lambda_(k,i)]. (6)

Every term on the right is nonnegative. Equation(6) keeps every original
cross multiplier, including both directions and both endpoints.

## 3. Step-only matrix bounds and a quadratic compatibility test

For each positive h_j define beta_j=E_j/w^2 using the preceding formulas.
A feasible full certificate necessarily has beta_j>=0 and

    0<=(K_jump)_jj<=beta_j.                              (7)

When b>=1, the expression simplifies to

    beta_j=[A^2 B^2-C^2 d^2]/(B+d)^2,
    C=A+B+d.                                             (8)

For0<b<=1 it is

    beta_j=[A^2-(1-b)^2(A+b)^2]/b^2.                     (9)

No negative-v trajectory is used in deriving(9). In particular the
small-step case is not obtained by applying a wrong-sign Huber residual.

For this section suppose all steps are positive. Set beta_(-1)=beta_r=0
and Drow_i=sqrt(beta_(i-1))+sqrt(beta_i) for0<=i<=r.
For a PSD matrix, its2-by-2 principal minors give
|(K_jump)_ik|<=sqrt(beta_i beta_k). Equation(2), applied to a standard
coordinate vector, therefore gives

    M_ii<=Drow_i^2,
    |(M u^j)_i|<=Drow_i sqrt(beta_j).                    (10)

The second inequality is the PSD Cauchy--Schwarz inequality; it also
follows by requiring (e_i+t u^j)^T M(e_i+t u^j)>=0 for every real t.
For every real vector z of length r,

    0<=z^T K_jump z<=(sum_(i<r)|z_i|sqrt(beta_i))^2.      (11)

An additional necessary condition involves no unknown multipliers.
Put p_0=1, p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i. The actual quadratic f(x)=x^2/2,
started at1, has x_i=g_i=p_i and every finite Q_ik=0. Consequently

    V_h=1-C^2 p_r^2,
    0<=1-C^2 p_r^2
          <=(sum_(i<r)|h_i p_i|sqrt(beta_i))^2.           (12)

The upper inequality follows from(2),(11), since p_i-p_(i+1)=h_i p_i.
Equation(12) is only a necessary compatibility test. Neither it nor the
scalar inequalities beta_j>=0 are asserted sufficient.

## 4. Explicit stability at a cut with b>1

Assume all h_i>0 and fix a cut j with b=h_j>1. Continue using the local
notation P,R,A,B,d above, and set

    a=d/B, w=1+a, delta=beta_j,
    m=min_(i<r)h_i>0, Delta_cut=AB-Cd.

Equation(8) implies Delta_cut>=0 and d<B: if d>=B, then Cd>=CB>AB.
Write u=Lambda_(j,r), v_k=Lambda_(k,j) for k>j, and set
c_k=h_k for j<k<r, c_r=1; thus sum_(k>j)c_k=B.

The ideal cross array has only the entries

    Lambda^0_(j,r)=d q, Lambda^0_(k,j)=q c_k (k>j),
    q=2(A+d)/(B-d),                                     (13)

with every other cross entry zero. At exact cut equality this is the
usual primitive-join array. Away from equality it is just an explicitly
defined comparison array; it is not claimed to be a certificate.

Here are step-only error bounds, with empty sums interpreted as zero:

    Fbd=w delta/(a m) if j<r-1, and Fbd=0 otherwise,
    Bbd=w delta/m     if j>0,   and Bbd=0 otherwise,
    Ubd=[2 sqrt(delta)sum_(i<j)Drow_i+P Bbd]/B,
    eta=Fbd+Bbd+Ubd,
    Ebd=2 sqrt(delta)sum_(k>j)Drow_k+2C(r-j)eta,
    Lbd=[2B/(B-d)] [eta+Ebd/d].                         (14)

Then the sum of the absolute differences of ALL crossing coefficients
in the supplied full array and(13) is at most Lbd:

    sum_(i<=j<k)(|Lambda_(i,k)-Lambda^0_(i,k)|
                      +|Lambda_(k,i)-Lambda^0_(k,i)|)<=Lbd. (15)

Here is a derivation, keeping track of the off-support terms.
First (6) with t_jk=a(s-P_k) yields

    Factual=sum_(i<=j,j<k<r)Lambda_(i,k)<=Fbd,
    Bactual=sum_(k>j,i<j)Lambda_(k,i)<=Bbd.              (16)

The weights s-P_k and P_j-P_i in these sums are at least m. The
remaining potentially unwanted cross terms are u_i=Lambda_(i,r),i<j.
For i<j, summing the complete mixed matrix entries gives

    2(M 1_right)_i
      =-B u_i-sum_(j<l<r)(1+P_l-P_(j+1))Lambda_(i,l)
       -sum_(k>j)Lambda_(k,i)
       +h_i sum_(k>j,l<=i)Lambda_(k,l).                 (17)

In(17), 1_right=e-u^j. By Me=0 and(10), the absolute value of its left
side is at most2Drow_i sqrt(delta). The two subtracted sums are
nonnegative. Thus

    B u_i<=2Drow_i sqrt(delta)+h_i Bactual.

Summing gives Uactual=sum_(i<j)u_i<=Ubd. Hence the total mass of all
cross entries absent from(13) is at most eta.

For completeness, the full off-diagonal formula for i<k is

    2M_ik=-(Lambda_ik+Lambda_ki)
          -h_k sum_(l>k)Lambda_il+h_i sum_(l<=i)Lambda_kl. (18)

It follows directly by multiplying L and T; set h_r=0 in this formula.
For fixed k>j, summing absolute values in(18) over i<j gives

    sum_(i<j)|2M_ik|
       <=(1+h_k)(Factual+Uactual)+(1+P)Bactual<=C eta.

Since (M u^j)_k=M_kj+sum_(i<j)M_ki, equation(10) gives
|2M_jk|<=2Drow_k sqrt(delta)+C eta.
Formula(18) at i=j gives, for k<r,

    2M_jk=d v_k-h_k u
        -Lambda_jk-h_k sum_(k<l<r)Lambda_jl
                                  +b sum_(l<j)Lambda_kl,

and at k=r gives 2M_jr=d v_r-u+b sum_(l<j)Lambda_rl.
The terms after d v_k-c_k u have total absolute value at most C eta.
Therefore, for every k>j,

    |d v_k-c_k u|<=e_k,
    e_k=2Drow_k sqrt(delta)+2C eta,
    sum_(k>j)e_k=Ebd.                                  (19)

Sum the original value-flow equations over the left block. Its outward
flow is u+Uactual+Factual and its inward flow is sum v_k+Bactual. Thus

    |sum_(k>j)v_k-u-2(A+d)|<=eta.

Together with(19) this implies

    |u-d q|<=[d eta+Ebd]/(B-d)=:Uerr,
    |v_k-q c_k|<=[c_k Uerr+e_k]/d.

Adding these bounds and the off-support mass eta gives

    eta+(1+B/d)Uerr+Ebd/d
        =[2B/(B-d)] [eta+Ebd/d]=Lbd,

which proves(15). In particular this controls the entire cross array,
not a selected sparse ansatz. No assertion about the internal arrays'
signs after tail restriction has entered the calculation.

## 5. Quantitative primitive-energy bound for the actual prefix

Let h_left=(h_0,...,h_(j-1)), of sum P and capacity A. Under the
hypotheses of Section4, EVERY actual convex1-smooth trajectory of this
prefix obeys

    V_(h_left)>=-[Rbd+(2P+b+1/2)Lbd] Gmax^2,             (20)
    Rbd=(A+d)Delta_cut/(B-d),
    Gmax=max_(0<=i<=j)||g_i||.

The energy of an empty prefix is0. For a nonempty prefix, no initial
distance normalization or bounded minimizer distance is assumed in(20).

To prove the claim, take any actual prefix data. Translate positions
and shift values so x_j=0 and f_j=0. Append formal tail data by setting
every g_k=f_k=0 for k>j and x_k=-b g_j for k>j. These tail data are NOT
asserted to arise from an actual function. They are permitted for
evaluating the full formal PSD identity(1). All tail-internal Q vanish.

Using the ideal cross array(13), direct substitution yields

    V_h+sum_cross Lambda^0_ik Q_ik
                      =V_(h_left)+Rbd||g_j||^2.         (21)

For an explicit coefficient check before the f_j=0 shift, V_h-V_left
has value coefficient2(P+b)f_j and gradient coefficient
b(1-b)+P(P+1) on ||g_j||^2. The ideal cross sum contributes
q(d-B)f_j+q[B/2-d(b-1/2)]||g_j||^2. The value coefficients cancel
because q(B-d)=2(A+d)=2(P+b). The remaining coefficient is

    A^2-(1+q)d^2
      =[(A+d)(AB-d(A+B+d))]/(B-d)=Rbd.

The actual prefix inequalities Q_ik<=0 and Lambda_ik>=0 make their
contribution in(1) nonpositive. Every cross residual on these formal
data has absolute value at most(2P+b+1/2)Gmax^2: actual convexity gives
|f_i-f_j|<=Gmax||x_i-x_j||<=P Gmax^2, GD gives
||x_i-x_j||<=P Gmax, and the formulas are

    Q_(i,k)=f_i+<g_i,-b g_j-x_i>+||g_i||^2/2,
    Q_(k,i)=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2,                    i<=j<k.

Equations(1),(15),(21) and full M>=0 now give(20).
This proof uses the unobserved formal extension only inside a PSD
identity; it never claims those data constitute a legal GD witness.

The error Rbd is linear in the scalar cut gap:

    delta=Delta_cut(AB+Cd)/(B+d)^2,
    Rbd=delta (A+d)(B+d)^2/[(B-d)(AB+Cd)].              (22)

Consequently, along a bounded positive-step family with m and d bounded
away from0, B-d bounded away from0, and all beta_i bounded, the right
side's coefficient in(20) is O(sqrt(delta)). This asymptotic notation
is only a consequence of the explicit bounds(14),(22).

An actual prefix witness with V_left=-nu Gmax^2, nu>0, therefore forces

    nu<=Rbd+(2P+b+1/2)Lbd.                              (23)

This is an explicit necessary condition on the parent steps. It supplies
a quantitative route from a prefix obstruction to a positive gap at the
parent cut, rather than assuming an exactly saturated cut.

For a concrete symbolic instance, take a parent whose first step is3/2
and select j=1, assuming its selected bridge h_1>1 and all steps positive.
For its one-step prefix, f(x)=x^2/2 started at1 has gradients1,-1/2,
Gmax=1 and V_left=1-(5/2)^2(1/2)^2=-9/16. Therefore every such parent
full certificate must satisfy

    Rbd+(b+7/2)Lbd>=9/16.                              (24)

In any bounded family meeting the positivity conditions after(22),
this prohibits approaching delta=0 with that fixed first step. It does
not exclude a parent with a positive cut gap or any unrelated seed.

## 6. Equality, zero steps, and exact symbolic checks

At delta=0, every quantity in(14) vanishes and Delta_cut=0. Thus all
cross entries are exactly(13). Their mixed gradient block is zero by
(18),(19). The left internal multipliers are nonnegative and give a
full certificate for V_left, since(21) now has Rbd=0 and the full left
matrix is the corresponding principal PSD block. This recovers the
old saturated-prefix restriction by a new limiting-compatible estimate.

Writing kappa=(C/B)^2 at equality gives q=kappa-1. The accepted full
primitive-join identity then shows that the tail has a certificate for

    V_tail-(1-1/kappa)sum_(k<r)h_k Q_(k,r),

where this sum is over nonterminal TAIL indices. Removing this signed
credit would subtract q h_k from its actual coefficient Lambda_(k,r).
Nothing in the preceding estimates asserts that those differences are
nonnegative. Thus no automatic ordinary primitive-tail restriction is
inferred; the old counterexample remains relevant.

Zero steps require care. The formulas(1),(2),(6) at a POSITIVE cut hold
even if other steps vanish. But(7)--(15) for every coordinate are not
claimed for an original word containing zeros. A full certificate can
have arbitrarily large curvature in an unused zero-step gradient direction.
For example h=(0), Lambda_01=Lambda_10=t>=0 satisfies the complete
flow equations and has M=t[[1,-1],[-1,1]]. Its jump matrix is[t], with
no finite step-only bound on t. This is an exact counterexample to
extending beta=2-b^2 continuously to a formal coordinate at b=0.

For necessary schedule tests one may instead compress zero steps in a
justified way. Retain the original index of each positive step and the
original terminal index. Identify formal jets within each repeated-query
group only to RESTRICT a given full certificate. The GD formulas and
V_h become exactly those of the positive-step compressed word. An edge
within one group vanishes after restriction; every other edge becomes
the corresponding compressed Q with its nonnegative coefficients summed.
Thus a full original certificate implies a full compressed certificate.

Conversely, a full compressed primitive certificate embeds into the
original formal space by assigning its edges only to the retained indices
and assigning zero to other edges. Zero steps contribute nothing to V_h
or to any queried displacement, and the original terminal is retained.
Therefore the embedded identity is the pullback of the compressed PSD
matrix and is PSD on ALL original independent gradients. No equality
between zero-step gradients is needed for this converse. The all-zero
word has the zero certificate. This proves feasibility equivalence while
also explaining why the original matrix entries need not be bounded.

In particular the tests above may be applied to the compressed word,
and the exact saturated-prefix conclusion may be lifted to the original
prefix by this embedding. The quantitative constants for a compressed
word refer to its compressed coefficient array, not an unjustified bound
on every possible original zero-step coefficient.

For one positive step b, the only value-flow and constant-kernel solution
is Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=2b+2. Direct expansion gives

    V_(b)+2Q_01+(2b+2)Q_10=(2-b^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.

Both versions(8),(9) reduce exactly to beta_0=2-b^2. Thus they recover
the complete one-step interface and its endpoint b=sqrt(2), including
the b<1 branch, without a numerical evaluation.

## 7. Scope and remaining missing object

For the C78 problem set r=4 and require sum h_i>=168/25, retaining all20
original nonnegative multipliers and the full5-gradient matrix. The new
constraints(6),(12),(15),(20) are necessary for that complete interface.
They complement scalar cut bounds and can support exact rejection or
reconstruction of a proposed seed. No candidate word has been evaluated
numerically here; no witness with the required capacity has been found,
and no separator for the entire feasible set has been established.

The original target remains open. The sole accepted scalar/composition
bridge is fact04219554e41d9750. This draft has no verifier allocation and
may not be used as a fact. The final-cut equality subgoal in the round23
plan was already present in shared work; the positive-slack estimates,
small-step trajectories and zero-coordinate warning are the additions.

Justification: explicit actual-function construction, coefficient
identities and elementary PSD inequalities. Main fragility checks are
the direction of cross residuals, small-step plateau legality, the full
off-diagonal signs, and the distinction between a formal extension and
an actual trajectory. Each is expanded above. Confidence before independent
verification:0.97; no verifier acceptance is claimed.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001348 -->
Definitions from accepted b98bb807d514e80d: P_n=S_circle(n)+1, B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, P0=B0=1. Their exact recursions are P_n=max_(k+l=n-1)F(P_k,P_l), B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)G(P_k,B_l), with F(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2 and G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.

SCALAR COMPARISON. Set alpha=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda)>0, L=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha. Its coefficients exceed1, so L>=x+y. Exact expansion gives alpha^2[L^2-(x+y)L-xy]=(2x/lambda-sqrt2 lambda y)^2>=0. Thus L>=F(x,y), the positive root of z^2-(x+y)z-xy=0. At z=lambda F(x,y), the polynomial defining G(x,lambda y) evaluates to z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2=x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha F(x,y)]>=0. Also z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, so it lies above the larger root G. Equality holds for x=0 and for x/y=lambda^2/sqrt2 with y>0. Homogeneity gives G(x,c y)/c=G(x/c,y), strictly decreasing in c for x>0, so any c<lambda fails at that equality point. No squaring-sign condition was omitted.

DP INDUCTION. G is nondecreasing in its second argument. If B_l<=lambda P_l, then G(P_k,B_l)<=G(P_k,lambda P_l)<=lambda F(P_k,P_l). Taking maxima proves B_n<=lambda P_n from the base.

DYADIC PRIMITIVE BOUND, NO PROFILE LIMIT. Let rho=log_2lambda>1. For t>0 put D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 and g(z)=log D(exp z). With t=exp z, differentiation gives g''(z)=rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/D(t)^2>0. The bracket is positive by its derivative rho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1]>0 and its zero value at0. Consequently D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. For x,y>0 put A=x^(1/rho),B=y^(1/rho),T=(A+B)^rho. This inequality gives (T-x)(T-y)>=2xy, so T^2-(x+y)T-xy>=0; T>=x+y by superadditivity of the power, and hence F(x,y)<=T. Zero arguments follow directly. Induction now gives P_n<=(n+1)^rho. Balanced joins give P_(2^m-1)>=lambda^m, hence equality. Therefore B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1) at every m.

ACTUAL NORMALIZATION. For c_m=lambda(1+1/m),a_m=(1-1/m)/c_m and H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j), M_j=1+sum H_j satisfies M_(j+1)=2M_j+a_m c_m^j. Thus M_m=A_m c_m^m+(1-A_m)2^m, A_m=a_m/(c_m-2)->1, and M_m/lambda^m->e. Define explicitly K_Hub,m=1/(2M_m-1), without presuming that this equals every earlier K notation. Then limsup B K_Hub<=lambda/(2e). If a genuine full certificate supplies q_m<=C K_Hub,m+o(K_Hub,m) for any C<2e/lambda, then limsup q_m B<=C lambda/(2e)<1. Equivalently q_m M_m has limsup<e/lambda as a sufficient condition. In particular q_m<=1/M_m would give limsup q_m B<=lambda/e<1; lambda<5/2<e. This is a sufficient scalar threshold only, not an upper certificate. The same proof yields thresholds 2exp(tau)/lambda and exp(tau)/lambda for the fixed deformation c_m=lambda(1+tau/m),a_m=(1-sigma/m)/c_m. All new statements remain unverified candidates; accepted b98 provides definitions, and all new inequalities are proved here rather than using unverified findings as premises.
